## B5 report — #1084

**Branch** `fix/settings-unproven-first-run` · **PR** [#1149](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1149) (draft, `Closes #1084`)

**Files touched:** `crates/engine/src/settings.rs`, `crates/engine/tests/vault_settings.rs`, `crates/engine/tests/write_plane.rs` (appended only), `blueprint/engine.md`. No other batch's files.

**Gates (committed tree):** fmt=0, clippy=0, `cargo test --workspace`=0, wasm32 check=0, `lint:tracker-refs`=0.

### What shipped

`DefaultsReason::NoRecord` was derived from the per-name sequence floor alone — a mark only a *confirmed adopt* raises. A settings record leaves three durable marks; the two the sequence floor misses are the ones that matter:

- **The publish mint counter**, raised by `next_revision` ahead of everything a publish can fail at. A member whose placement save never landed still carries durable proof of the choice — the moment reverting it is cheapest.
- **The adopted body revision**, raised by a store write separate from and not atomic with the sequence floor's (both `let _ =`, and `commit_floors` is documented non-atomic). A device that took one and lost the other demonstrably adopted a record.

All three are now read on the withheld branch; any present makes a missing record `Suppressed`, which already refuses. Unreadable marks fail closed to `FloorUnreadable`. `NoRecord` → `UnprovenFirstRun`, because absence is only ever a verdict about *this device* and this is the one degraded arm that still authorises a write. No gate moved.

### Design constraint worth knowing

Refusing the unproven first run outright is not available: `publish_settings` has no production caller (#1144), so "no record" is the universal state and a blanket refusal would refuse every content write on every account. The mint-counter half is therefore dormant until a settings-save command is wired; the adopted-revision and unreadable-mark halves are live now.

### Review gates

- **`/simplify`** (4 angles): killed a `SettingsTraces` struct + helper as derivable state, a fallible `placement_of(&amp;default())` with a dead `Err` arm, five copies of one invariant, and a hard-coded `settings-revision/` prefix literal duplicated across the crate boundary. Extracted a `publish_unconfirmed` fixture shared with the test that already needed it.
- **`/security-review`**: clean on trust boundaries, namespacing, determinism, and leaks. Flagged the adopted-revision mark I had dropped during simplify — restored it.
- **`/crypto-privacy-review`**: confirmed no gate reordering, rule-8 symmetry intact, no new persisted state or traffic. Found one MEDIUM I could not fix (below), and correctly insisted I restore the byte-destination assertion the altitude reviewer had talked me out of.

### Left undone — lives in B1's `facade.rs`

The crypto review found the residual path *destroys* the signal that could close it. `hosted_quota_pre_flight` only runs on a placement that resolved — after this change, that includes the assumed `Hosted`. A fresh device with the record withheld therefore PATCHes the account to `byo=false`, clearing the account-wide flag on a placement nothing authenticated, and removing the server-side backstop that would have rejected its hosted upload. Also: `self.placement` is set once in `start` and never recomputed, so a successful in-session settings save cannot clear a `NoPlacement` refusal until restart — while the refusal's own message says "save your settings again".

Filed as **[#1159](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/issues/1159)** (`v2-build`, native `blocked_by` edge to #1084), with the two-part fix spelled out: gate the reconcile on an authenticated placement, and read the flag in the restricting direction only. Doing both closes #1084's documented residual — worth routing to whoever picks up the facade next.</result>

## PR #1145 — #1037, zeroize the upload chunk in the engine worker

`fix/1037-zeroize-worker-upload-chunk` · https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1145
Files: `packages/client/src/worker/engineHost.ts`, `engineHost.test.ts`

**Ownership finding (the thing the issue turned on):** the chunk reaches `EngineHost.pushChunk` by transfer at every hop — `transport.ts:135` and the follower's `broadcastTransport` → `leaderRelay` chain both `postMessage(..., [chunk])`, so the buffer is detached in the sender's realm. The worker is the terminal owner, and no same-realm caller still holds it. `crates/wasm/src/host.rs:289` takes `chunk: Vec&lt;u8&gt;`, so wasm-bindgen marshals the view into linear memory *before* `push_chunk` returns its promise; the wipe cannot race the copy.

`EngineHost` now has one private `scrubbing` helper (wipe in a `finally`, so the reject path scrubs too); `start` and `pushChunk` share it instead of repeating the idiom. Two tests assert the buffer is zero after a resolved and a rejected push, with the WASM-side copy asserted to have seen real bytes so the wipe can't pass by firing too early.

## PR #1150 — #1051, fail closed in `buildCommand`

`fix/1051-fail-closed-build-command` · https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1150
Files: `packages/client/src/worker/commandCodec.ts`, `commandCodec.test.ts`

Every field of all 16 command kinds now passes a checker taking `unknown`. Beyond the issue's ask I also fixed `nodeKind`/`permission`, which silently fell through to `Folder`/**`Write`** on an unrecognised literal — the same coercion class, one layer down, and a silent privilege upgrade. The `default` arm uses the relay's house idiom (`unknownCommand(descriptor: never)`), verified by temporarily adding a 17th kind: `tsc` fails with *not assignable to type 'never'*. Six tests, driven against a fake whose every builder succeeds so only the codec can reject.

**Note on #1144:** the exhaustiveness bound is a hard compile error, so whoever lands the new command kind must add a builder in the same diff — that is the intended cost, not a surprise.

Both branches, from the committed tree: `pnpm typecheck` 0, `pnpm test` 0, `pnpm lint` 0, `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` 0. Full CI settled with zero non-success checks on both.

Caught it via `gh pr view --json files` showing four files instead of two; rebased `--onto d11bfce11`, re-ran all four gates, force-pushed with lease. PR #1150 is now two files, independent of #1145.
- `/simplify`, `/security-review`, `/crypto-privacy-review` ran as subagents on #1145; the pool was saturated by sibling batches for #1150, so I did a manual pass and then got one independent crypto-privacy agent through. Findings folded in: the shared scrub helper, the house exhaustiveness idiom, and a coverage gap where nothing asserted the *second* literal of either mirror enum (`'folder'`, `'write'`).

## Out-of-scope defects found and filed

Both real, both outside `packages/client/src/worker/**` or outside the issue's surface, both with a native bidirectional dependency edge:

- **#1154** (blocked by #1051) — `beginWrite`/`snapshot`/`download`/`openContentStream` read the same untrusted message with the same coercion. Sharper than the command case: wasm-bindgen's `passArray8ToWasm0` is `Uint8Array.prototype.set(arrayLike)`, so a 16-character *string* reaches `NodeId::from_bytes` as sixteen zero bytes and passes the Rust length check — a request against the wrong node rather than a refusal.
- **#1155** (blocked by #1037) — `EngineClient.pushChunk` and `CorrelatedTransport.request` drop a chunk they refuse *before* any transfer, so it is never detached and never wiped. `EngineClient.start` and `LeaderRelay` already scrub on their equivalent refusals; only the sending side is missing it.

Nothing was left undone in another batch's files. Expect a rebase on both if B1's `#1016` makes additive edits to these two files.</result>

## PR #1148 — #775 (base `main`)
`fix/775-cold-seed-anchor-role-from-scope-identity` — https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1148

`gate::floor::cold_seed_checked` took `AnchorRole` as an argument, so a future shared-scope caller could have selected it from a `RepointObject` field and suppressed the read-epoch regression check at the vault anchor. The role is no longer an input at all — it is derived from the re-point's own `scope_id` against the session's root scope id, both trusted. The `AnchorRole` enum was deleted outright (review found it became dead public surface once nothing could pass it).

Files: `crates/engine/src/gate/floor.rs`, `crates/engine/src/sync/boot.rs`, `crates/engine/tests/sync.rs`.

**The recorded #1141 rebase check was a no-op in practice**: the new caller stayed in `gate/floor.rs` + `sync/boot.rs`, so it touched no `mint_grant_row` call and constructed no `ScopeRootIdentity`. No conflict with #1141, confirmed by a clean `cargo test --workspace` on top of `d11bfce11`.

**A production `AnchorRole::Shared` caller could not be wired, and this is a real tracker gap.** The grantee-plane shared-scope resolve does not exist: `scope_pointer_name` derives the pointer name from the *owner's* `ownerPointerSeed`, which a grantee never holds, and no wire field carries that name to a grantee. `open_repoint` has no non-test caller, `grants::accept` persists a `pointerReadKey` nothing reads back, and `Command::AcceptShare` returns `Unimplemented`. The invariant is now structural so the future caller cannot get it wrong, which is what #775 actually asks for.

## PR #1157 — #703 (base = the #775 branch)
`fix/703-revival-corroborates-recovered-sequence` — https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1157

`revive` trusted a single `recovery_fetch` record as its CAS basis. Now the routing fan-out corroborates it (taking the tie at `&gt;=`, since a same-sequence fork is designed-for after an unconfirmed publish retry), the head CID is read from the same record as the sequence, and a basis below the durable sequence floor fails closed as `ReviveError::StaleSource`.

Files: `crates/engine/src/net/revival.rs`, `crates/engine/tests/net.rs`.

## Review gates
All three ran on each PR's own diff. Real findings folded in both times; full detail is in the PR bodies. Two worth flagging:

- Security and crypto-privacy independently flagged that #775's derivation rests on an encoder-only invariant. I verified the mechanism rather than applying the suggested core-side check literally: the AEAD tag covers the AAD and the AAD is derived from `object.scope_id`, so a mismatched block cannot open. A decode-side check would be unreachable and could carry no reject vector, which `crates/core`'s coverage law requires. Pinned behaviourally instead.
- **A review sub-agent ran `git checkout --` on my working tree** and destroyed three in-flight edits, mistaking concurrent work for a rogue agent. Recovered fully. Later reviewers were briefed with an explicit no-git-writes rule.

Two #703 residuals were filed as **#1161** (`blocked_by` #703): `fanout_get_verify` cannot distinguish "no endpoint answered" from "no endpoint has it", and a basis exactly at the floor is admitted with any content. Neither is live — `revive` has no production caller.

## #856 — not shipped, analysis posted instead
https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/issues/856#issuecomment-5217819029

The diagnosis holds. Both viable fixes are out of B4's reach:

- **A.** Per-account `root_scope_id` needs a new frozen-KDF edge plus its KAT — the root scope id is a KDF input, a pointer-payload AAD field, and the envelope scope tag.
- **B.** An account-namespacing `FloorStore` decorator (my file, `seams/floor_store.rs`) — every downstream consumer is already generic over `F: FloorStore`, so the *entire* cost is ~10 call sites in `facade.rs`, plus a fail-closed accessor because `load_settings` touches floors inside `start()` before the session is committed.

B is the right design, but two of its ten sites fall inside B1's open `#1151` hunks (`@@ -2262,7 +2308 @@` and `@@ -2272,14 +2321 @@`). A ten-site restructure of that file from another batch, stacked behind two PRs, is the exact shape that leaves both branches `MERGEABLE` and still breaks `main`. **Recommend scheduling design B as its own slice once #1151 merges, with `facade.rs` ownership for the duration.**

## Report

**Branch:** `feat/wire-facade-contact-import-and-command-outcome`
**PR:** [#1151](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1151) — draft, MERGEABLE, `Closes #1016`

### What landed

**The shape change** (the thing the issue said to land first, never last — six issues sequence behind it). `Engine::command` now returns `Result&lt;CommandOutcome, EngineError&gt;` instead of `Result&lt;Option&lt;OpId&gt;, EngineError&gt;`: `Done`, `Queued { op_id }`, `ContactImported(Contact)`, plus `CommandOutcome::op_id()` for the event-correlation case.

**`Command::ImportContact` fully wired** onto `grants::import_contact` — mandatory fail-closed binding verify, answering with the `Contact` itself (no public constructor, so holding one *is* the proof). Refusals split correctly: bad binding → `TrustViolation`, undecodable → new `EngineError::MalformedInput { check }`, over-cap → refused before the decoder via `MAX_CONTACT_CODE_BYTES`.

### Six arms NOT wired — real blockers, not plumbing

- **`RotateNow`/`Revoke`/`Downgrade`**: need a production `OwnerScopeKeys`. Its `writer_pseudonym` edge takes *pairwise material* and **no production code anywhere derives the owner's half** — every `owner_pseudonym_pk` producer in the tree is a test fixture. That's a KDF-catalog decision, not facade wiring. `rotate_scope` also needs a sweep task and **`SweepResolver` has no production impl**.
- **`Grant`**: `create_read_grant` takes a `SweepResolver` (same gap) plus the recipient's enc subkey, which needs a contact book the engine doesn't have.
- **`CreateInviteLink`**: `RecordedInvite` is the owner's authority over the link and no seam persists it; claim-to-grant conversion unlanded (issue says so).
- **`AcceptShare`**: needs `ReceivedShareStore`, a grants-layer seam outside the nine host seams — adding a tenth changes `SeamSet` and every host.

These are documented in the PR body with checkable conditions. **Worth filing as follow-up issues with dependency edges** — particularly the owner-pseudonym derivation, which blocks all three rotation arms and is currently an invisible tracker gap.

### Gates (from the committed tree)

| Gate | Exit |
|---|---|
| `cargo fmt --all -- --check` | 0 |
| `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` | 0 |
| `cargo test --workspace` | 0 |
| `cargo check -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --all-targets` | 0 |

No TypeScript work, so no TS gates; verified the diff carries no tracker refs.

### Review gates

- **`/simplify`** (4 angles): found `ImportedContact` duplicating `grants::Contact` → replaced with `Contact` itself; wasm `kind` was a numeric enum diverging from the `Event::kind` string precedent → made it a camelCase string; trimmed absence-justifying and restated docs; deleted a tautological test. All folded in, commit `967aec458`.
- **`/security-review` + `/crypto-privacy-review`** (combined, crypto-privacy-reviewer): 0 Critical/High. **Medium** — unbounded `contact_code` into the CBOR decoder inside the single-writer lock → added `MAX_CONTACT_CODE_BYTES`. **Lows** — malformed collapsing into `TrustViolation` → added `MalformedInput`; missing `NotStarted` vector for the arm → added. **Info** — derived `Debug` leaking a peer's identity key → hand-wrote `Debug` like `Command`'s. Confirmed clean: binding verify unbypassable, no key material in error strings, no zeroization violation, no timing channel, encode/decode symmetry holds, unimplemented arms genuinely fail closed. Commit `2e1696e3c`.
- Skipped one finding: wasm returns an exported class rather than a plain JS object (GC-latency, ~72 bytes/command). Every other boundary type here is an exported class, so this matches the existing design; noted rather than changed.

### Ownership boundaries

- **`crates/fuse/src/ops.rs` — NOT touched.** Its `command` helper already discards the value (`self.engine.command(command).await?; Ok(())`), so the signature change passes straight through. The issue's cited line numbers were stale.
- **`crates/fuse/src/error.rs` — one line added.** B2 owns `crates/fuse/**`. The `From&lt;EngineError&gt; for VfsError` impl is deliberately exhaustive with no wildcard, so my new `MalformedInput` variant is a compile error there by design. Added `EngineError::MalformedInput { .. } =&gt; VfsError::Invalid`. **B2 should expect this one-line conflict.**
- **`packages/client/src/worker/**` — NOT touched.** `engineHost.command` returns `Promise&lt;void&gt;` and discards, so B7 needs no change. Flagging for B7: the imported contact is currently unreachable from TS; wiring it means reading `identityPublicKey`/`encPublicKey` and calling `.free()`.
- **`crates/engine/tests/write_plane.rs`** (shared with B3/B5): mechanical `.op_id()` insertions at the 14 sites that read a queue id. No restructuring, no test added or removed.
- **`crates/wasm/Cargo.toml`**: added `cipherbox-core` as a wasm-target **dev**-dependency so the boundary test can sign a real contact code. Never enters the cdylib.</result>

## Report — batch B8

### PR 1 — #1126, resource-exhaustion bound
- **Branch** `feat/bound-live-stream-tickets` → **PR** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1146 (draft), `Closes #1126`
- **Files**: `packages/client/src/media/{broker,service}.ts` + tests, `apps/web/src/hooks/useFileDownload.ts`, **new** `apps/web/src/hooks/useFileDownload.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/FileBrowserActions.{tsx,test.tsx}`
- **Design**: bounded by ticket *lifetime*, not a cap. `MediaBroker.whenIdle(ticket, startWithinMs)` resolves when the ticket's last body ends (cursor count → 0, so the 5s playback linger stays out of the wait) and reports whether a body ever claimed it. `MediaService.whenStreamIdle` maps a URL to it. `save` awaits and revokes in a `finally`, so `downloadSelection`'s sequential loop holds one ticket for a whole selection instead of one per file. `save` now returns `false` for a refused save and the batch stops there.

### PR 2 — #1064, recoverable stream refusal
- **Branch** `feat/media-player-recoverable-refusal` → **PR** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1160 (draft), `Closes #1064`
- **Files**: `packages/client/src/media/{broker,service,registry}.ts`, `correlatedTransport.ts`, `index.ts`, `service.test.ts`; `apps/web` — **new** `components/file-browser/MediaPlayer.tsx`, `FilePreviewDialog.tsx`, `hooks/useFilePreview.ts`, `vault/previewKind.ts`, `styles/dialogs.css`, `FileBrowserActions.test.tsx`
- **Design**: `MediaBroker` reports every read it gives up on, classified by `engineErrorCode`/`isRecoverableEngineError`; `MediaService.onStreamError` hands the URL's holder a `MediaStreamFailure`. `MediaPlayer` shows a retryable notice for a ceiling refusal (reusing the vault browser's existing notice dialect), a terminal one otherwise, with the element's own `error` as the floor. Audio and video became preview kinds, streamed with no buffered fallback.

**Deviation from the issue you should know about**: #1064 asks for `cb:media:error` to carry the engine's code across the port. Re-checked against the tree — that would be a dead field. The only `cb:media:error` carrying an engine code is raised by `MediaBroker.fail`, which runs **in the tab**, same context as the player; the SW's sole action on it is to error a body whose error a media element cannot read, and `MediaPipe`'s own failures have no engine code. So `protocol.ts` is untouched and the code is reported tab-side. Reasoning is written into the PR body.

### Gates (committed tree, both branches)
`pnpm typecheck` **0** · `pnpm test` **0** · `pnpm lint` **0** · `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` **0**. No Rust surface in either PR, so the cargo gates are N/A.

### Review gates
Both PRs ran `/simplify` and `/security-review` on their own diff; findings folded into a second commit on each.

- **#1126 security found two real defects, both fixed**: (1) settling idle waiters in `MediaBroker.close` was wrong — a replaced port is the normal recovery for a killed Service Worker and the pipe re-opens against the fresh port, so settling revoked the ticket before the retry could name it and *every cold-worker download would 404*; `close` now re-arms. (2) The stall deadline could expire on an open body between pulls (Save-As dialog, paused download) and revoke a live transfer; the deadline now covers only the window before a body claims the ticket, and an expiry with a live cursor re-arms. It also flagged that a blocked batch would burn the deadline once per file — hence `save` returning a refusal and the loop breaking.
- **#1064 security found nothing at or above the bar** (no plaintext/key leak in the report, MIME clamp + `nosniff` intact, single-code classifier so nothing widens into recoverable, listener unsubscribes on unmount and rebuild). <result>Security review of `feat/fuse-content-write-path` (`git diff main...HEAD`) is complete. No files were edited, nothing committed or pushed.

## 1. Non-zero `ftruncate` resurrects deleted plaintext into the published version

`crates/fuse/src/ops.rs:517-552` — **Severity: MEDIUM**, confidence 9/10. Confirmed.

**Mechanism.** `truncate_handle` records a shrink as only `pending.len = size`. It sets `pending.base = Base::Empty` only when `size == 0` (`ops.rs:547-549`), and `SpillFile::truncate` (`spill.rs:136-149`) only retains/zero-tails blocks the spill *already holds* — on a freshly opened handle the spill is empty, so it is a no-op. `Pending` (`ops.rs:70-79`) carries no truncation floor. Later, `version_block` (`ops.rs:642-652`) resolves any block the spill does not hold by reading the pinned **pre-truncate** base version via `engine.read_stream`, which knows nothing of `pending.len`. `push_version` (`ops.rs:592-598`) then walks `0..len` with no clamp and publishes those bytes.

**Repro — two syscalls, no write needed.** File has a published 100-byte version. `open(O_RDWR)`; `ftruncate(fd, 10)` → `Pending { spill: None, len: 10, base: Version, dirty: true }`; `ftruncate(fd, 100)` → spill is `None` so the spill truncate is a no-op, `len = 100`, `base` stays `Version`; `close(fd)` → every block falls through to the base stream and the published version is byte-for-byte the original 100 bytes. The shrink is entirely undone. The shrink-then-write-past-the-cut variant yields the same for the gap region, which POSIX requires to read as zeros.

**Why security-relevant.** The resurrected plaintext is re-sealed and published as a new *live* version, so (a) a file shared after the user truncated sensitive bytes away exposes those bytes to a recipient who never held the prior version, and (b) the deleted tail is carried into every later version and into a rotated epoch's reseal. Deterministic, no race, reachable from unprivileged `ftruncate(2)`. `create` correctly sets `Base::Empty` (`ops.rs:207`), so this hits exactly the pre-existing-file case where deleted content exists.

**Fix.** Add a truncation floor to `Pending` (e.g. `zeroed_from: Option&lt;u64&gt;`, set on every shrink, raised/cleared as writes fill it). Have `version_block` return zeros — never a base read — for indices wholly at or past the floor, and zero the tail of the straddling block after the base read.

**Test gap.** `crates/fuse/tests/fuse_op_core.rs` exercises truncate only at `size == 0` (lines 986, 1068, 1094, 1115) — the one case `Base::Empty` masks. Add shrink-then-grow and shrink-then-write-past tests.

## 2. `SpillArea::open` sweep + non-atomic dir hardening — UNVALIDATED

`crates/fuse/src/spill.rs:38-46` and `spill.rs:225-229` — candidate severity LOW. **I could not confirm this**: its validation agent never returned, so I have no verdict on it. Correcting an earlier statement of mine in this session that called it filtered — that was premature and wrong.

Raw claim: `open` does `create_dir_all` → `set_permissions(0o700)` → delete **every regular file** in the directory, with no `symlink_metadata` check that the path is a real directory and with a window between creation and the chmod. Claimed consequences: a symlink planted at `&lt;account&gt;/spill` is followed, its target chmod'd `0o700` and its regular files deleted; and the function takes an arbitrary `PathBuf`, so a caller passing `account_data_dir` would wipe the floor store, snapshot cache and staging store. Caveat weighing against it: the attacker must already have same-uid write access to the mount's private data dir, which likely reduces this to hardening. Fix if you want it regardless: reject non-directory/symlink via `symlink_metadata`; create with `DirBuilder::mode(0o700)` so the mode is atomic with creation; scope the sweep to the `spill.&lt;pid&gt;.&lt;n&gt;` name shape.

## Filtered out as false positives

3. **Spill AAD binds no generation counter** (`spill.rs:191`) — FP, confidence 9. Nonce scheme verified **sound**: fresh Entropy-seam key per `SpillFile`, strictly monotone `u64` counter incremented on every `put` including slot rewrites, `checked_add` overflow → `Err`. No nonce is ever reused under a key. Key is `Zeroizing`, memory-only, never logged. The restore-old-ciphertext rollback needs a same-uid local process that can already write the mount directly — hardening, not a vuln.
4. **`release` tears down `Pending` on a failed commit** (`ops.rs:457-465`) — FP, confidence 9. The security hook was factually wrong: `truncated.and(released)` with `release` returning `committed` means the no-handle truncate path returns `Err` on a failed commit, not a false success. `close(2)` is covered by `flush` (`ops.rs:444-446`), which propagates the commit error; the kernel discarding RELEASE's status is the FUSE protocol for every filesystem. Residual (mmap writeback after the last FLUSH) is reliability. Retaining spills after a failed commit would *increase* ciphertext-at-rest lifetime, inverting the PR's goal.
5. **Zero-length write extends the file** (`ops.rs:403-404`) — FP, confidence 8. Mechanically real (`pending.len = pending.len.max(end)` runs unconditionally, so `write(fh, big_offset, &amp;[])` sets the length and marks dirty), but the impact is pure resource exhaustion and the same amplification is reachable via a legitimate one-byte sparse write or `truncate_handle` to any size. Linux VFS short-circuits `count == 0` before FUSE. Worth a one-line `if data.is_empty() { return Ok(0); }` as a correctness fix, not a security finding.

## Verified sound (no finding)

Integer arithmetic on kernel-controlled offsets fails closed throughout (`ops.rs:377` `checked_add`; `ops.rs:647` and `spill.rs:157-163` `checked_mul`). Spill files use `create_new(true)` + `mode(0o600)`, so `O_EXCL|O_CREAT` refuses a planted symlink. Spill truncation and cross-slot transplants fail closed (in-memory `blocks` set, `read_exact` over the full slot, block index in the AAD). `flush`/`fsync` ack only after `stage_op` succeeds and leave `dirty = true` for retry. Write and truncate both refuse non-writable handles (`ops.rs:373-375`, `ops.rs:519-521`). All new plaintext buffers are `Zeroizing`. `spill_io` errors carry only `io::ErrorKind`, never a path or content byte. Entropy is injected via `Box&lt;dyn Entropy&gt;` — no direct `OsRng`/`thread_rng` anywhere in the diff.</result>

So the new `contactImported` outcome and its two public keys are unreachable from TS today. That is a wiring gap for a follow-up, not a merge hazard — and worth knowing before someone assumes the contact-import path is live end-to-end.

## Report

**Finding 1 — restating doc comments (verified, applied).** Both doc comments were introduced by *this* PR's diff, so they were in scope. Deleted both:
- `/// Assert no configured endpoint holds any record for `name`.` above `assert_no_endpoint_holds`
- `/// The verified `Value` of the record the first endpoint holds for `name`.` above `head_value_at`

Each was a pure restatement of a 5-8 line body whose name already says it. I did **not** touch the identically-shaped comment above `assert_all_endpoints_at` (line ~107) — `git diff main...HEAD` shows it is pre-existing, untouched code, and editing it would be the "unchanged-code apologia" scope creep AGENTS.md forbids.

**Finding 2 — the same-sequence tie-break (verified, test added).** Confirmed the mechanism in `crates/engine/src/net/revival.rs` before writing anything:
- line 97: `Some((observed, _)) if observed.sequence &gt;= recovered.sequence =&gt; observed` — the `&gt;=` is deliberate, the fan-out takes the tie.
- line 115: `head_cid_from_value(&amp;basis.value)` — the CID is read off `basis`, the *same* record the sequence came from, so a corroborated sequence cannot be paired with a stale CID.

Added `revive_takes_the_fan_out_side_of_a_same_sequence_fork` in `crates/engine/tests/net.rs`, beside the existing preference test. It seeds the recovery endpoint at `/ipfs/bafyrecoveryside` seq 5 and the fan-out endpoint at `/ipfs/bafyfanoutside` seq 5 — different CIDs, same sequence — then asserts `PublishOutcome::Published { sequence: 6 }`, all endpoints at 6, and the head value equal to the **fan-out** CID. Behaviour only; no source-text assertions.

**Inversion check — done, and the test earns its place.** Flipping line 97 from `&gt;=` to `&gt;` made the new test the *only* failure in the `revive` set (7 passed / 1 failed), with the expected diff — got `/ipfs/bafyrecoveryside`, wanted `/ipfs/bafyfanoutside`. The five pre-existing revival tests all still passed under the inversion, which is exactly the gap this test closes. I restored the `&gt;=` and confirmed `git diff` on `revival.rs` is empty, i.e. byte-identical to the committed version; the commit touches `crates/engine/tests/net.rs` only.

<result>All done. PR #1156 is still a draft at head `2ace6f603`.

## Per-finding verdicts

**Finding 1 — MAJOR, confirmed and fixed.**
Verified the mechanism first: `Invalidation::Data` exists (`crates/fuse/src/adapter.rs:17`) and its only other producer is `stream_for` (`crates/fuse/src/ops.rs:668`), which fires only at *bind* time when the resolved `contentCid` differs from `streamed`. A handle with a stream already attached returns early at `ops.rs:649` and never reaches it, and the kernel's page cache is never asked at all — so nothing on the commit path repainted data. Confirmed no other data invalidation on that path (`commit` calls `begin_write`/`push_version`/`commit_write`, then only `Attributes`; no `render()`).

Fix: push `Invalidation::Data { ino }` before `Attributes` in `commit` (`crates/fuse/src/ops.rs:540`). The ordering is not cargo-cult — the two are separate ordered seam messages, and a kernel that learns the new size while still holding pre-commit pages serves them as the new version, including across a range a grown size just made valid. Data-first makes the intermediate state a short read, not a stale one; the test asserts the order.

I deliberately did *not* also clear `self.streamed` on commit — a stale entry there can only cause an extra invalidation, never a missed one.

**Failing-test proof.** New test `published::a_commit_repaints_the_pages_of_the_version_it_replaced` (`crates/fuse/tests/fuse_op_core.rs:1846`). With the `Data` line reverted:

```
thread 'published::a_commit_repaints_the_pages_of_the_version_it_replaced' panicked at
crates/fuse/tests/fuse_op_core.rs:1865:9:
a commit must drop the pages of the version it replaced, got [Attributes { ino: 2 }]
```

**Finding 2 — confirmed, reworded.** `write` calls `begin_pending` at `ops.rs:367` before the block loop, and `base_len` (`ops.rs:478`) falls through to `stream_for` whenever `attrs(node).size` is `None`, alignment irrelevant. Doc on `stream_for` now scopes the skip to an already-projected size with whole-block replacement, and states that an unprojected size resolves regardless because only the resolve yields the base length.

**Finding 3 — confirmed, both halves done.** `writing_handle` (`fuse_op_core.rs:808`) returns the handle from `create`, and `create` inserts `Pending::over(0)` at `ops.rs:193`, so `begin_pending` hits its `contains_key` early return at `ops.rs:465` — no resolve was ever possible. Renamed to `a_write_into_a_created_handles_spill_never_parks` with a narrowed comment, and added `published::a_write_on_a_reopened_handle_never_parks_once_the_size_is_projected`, which asserts `Poll::Ready` *and* that `core.handle(handle).stream` is still `None` — direct evidence no resolution ran, not an inference from timing.

**Finding 4 — taken.** Two doc lines on the non-unix `open_private` / `restrict_dir` arms in `crates/fuse/src/spill.rs`, stating the seal argument once and cross-referencing.

**Finding 5 — deferred, issue filed: #1168.** Reasoning: the staleness worry the prompt raised does **not** apply — `ChunkCache` is keyed by `(StreamHandle, index)` and a stream pins one head version for its whole life (`crates/fuse/src/cache.rs` module header), so a cached block can never be served as a slice of a different version. But two other things make it not-small:

1. `version_block` fetches `want = block_bytes.min(base_len - at)` and zero-fills the rest, because bytes past the floor a truncate left must read as a hole. That clamp is per-handle `Pending` state, not a property of the stream. Caching the clamped, zero-padded block under `(stream, index)` poisons `read` for any other handle sharing that stream; reading a cached full block without re-applying the clamp re-introduces exactly what `d312a25b3` — this branch's own previous commit — fixed.
2. `push_version` walks the file linearly once, so inserting every base block it touches evicts the reader's hot blocks for zero reuse against a 64-block budget. The commit walk wants read-through-without-insert, which the cache doesn't offer.

So the correct fix is a semantics change on the clamp the head commit just corrected plus a new cache mode. #1168 carries both constraints and acceptance criteria; labels `v2-build` + `comp:desktop`, milestone `v2.0.0`, `parent_issue_url` → `issues/991`, `blocked_by` → `[1110]` (all verified by re-read).

## Verdict on A — half refuted, half real

**Refuted: the overwrite cannot happen.**
- `MediaPlayer.tsx:45` — `onError: () =&gt; setRefusal((held) =&gt; held ?? { recoverable: false, message: FAULT_NOTICE })`. A held refusal wins; a recoverable one is never replaced and the retry control never disappears.
- `broker.ts:441-446` — `fail()` posts `cb:media:error` to the port and calls `onFailure` on the next line, synchronously. The port message still has to reach the SW, error the response body, and surface as an element `error` event — all strictly later tasks. So a subscribed listener always hears the classified reason *before* the element's unclassifiable one.

**Real: the subscription window.** `service.ts:89-93` keeps a plain listener `Set` with no replay — a failure reported before the subscription is lost, leaving `refusal` null so only the terminal notice shows.

**Fix (minimal, no restructure):** `useEffect` → `useLayoutEffect` in `MediaPlayer.tsx:24`. Layout effects run inside the same synchronous commit that inserts the element, so no event-loop turn can deliver anything between the element getting its `src` and the listener existing. I did **not** add the reviewer's URL-scoped readiness state — withholding `src` would delay every playback for a race that needs several message round-trips to land inside one commit.

## Changes
- `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/MediaPlayer.tsx` — layout-timed subscription + 2-line why comment.
- `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/FileBrowserActions.test.tsx` — describe-level fake gained an opt-in `transfers` map so `whenStreamIdle` parks until `endTransfer(url)` releases it (`fakePipe` shape); other tests leave it null and keep immediate resolution.
- `apps/web/src/hooks/useFileDownload.test.ts` — timer test advances 999 ms → unmount → 1 ms.

| Test | Red proof |
|---|---|
| `keeps the retry when the element reports the refusal it was already told about` (new) | Dropping `held ??` → `expected 'playback failed' to contain 'too many streams are open right now'` |
| `saves a selection one ticket at a time` (rewritten) | Loop at `FileBrowserActions.tsx:93-96` → `Promise.all` → `expected [ … ] to have a length of 1 but got 2` |
| `revokes each blob url on its own timer rather than at unmount` (rewritten) | `REVOKE_AFTER_MS=900` → 999 ms assertion fails; `=1_100` → final assertion fails; adding `URL.revokeObjectURL` to the unmount cleanup → post-unmount assertion fails |

Literals stay literal in the timer test rather than importing `REVOKE_AFTER_MS` — importing it would let a delay change pass, which was the complaint.

# CipherBox v2 — Wave 8 Recon

Method notes up front, because they determine the numbers:

- **"Not blocked"** = `GET /repos/FSM1/cipher-box/issues/N/dependencies/blocked_by` returns either an empty array or only `state: "closed"` entries. I queried this endpoint for all 110 open issues individually; an empty result is treated as unblocked. **Caveat:** this only models blockers recorded as native GitHub dependency edges. Prose-only blockers are invisible to it — I found one concrete case (#1152, see §2).
- **Parentage** via `.parent_issue_url` (never `.parent`). The tree is **three levels deep**, not two: #655 → track → sub-track (#635, #642, #648, #752, #872) → leaf. A naive `sub_issues` call on a track therefore under-counts.
- **No open issue is a true orphan** — every one of the 101 non-track open issues resolves to a parent that rolls up to #655.

---

## 1. Track-level state

Counts are **recursive** (direct + nested sub-track children).

| Track | Title | Open | Closed | What's left |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **#989** | core: codec, crypto, KDF catalog | **2** | 13 | Effectively done. Two crypto hardening items: #1131 (move gate stage-3 section auth into core) and #1142 (reject non-prime-order X25519). |
| **#990** | api: NestJS residual, registry, mailbox, republisher | **0** | 8 | ✅ **COMPLETE.** No open work. |
| **#991** | engine: foundation — seams, gate, sync, grants, rotation | **45** | 41 | The bulk of the build. 11 direct + sub-track **#635** (33 open / 17 closed — rotation, sweep, name wave, grants) + **#752** (1 open / 14 closed). |
| **#992** | engine: the write plane | **20** | 31 | 18 direct + **2 nested under closed #872** (see flag). Mostly correctness/perf defects on drain, retention, dead letters, plus #1094 version retention. |
| **#993** | web: packages/client and apps/web | **15** | 40 | 9 direct + sub-track **#642** (6 open / 22 closed). #643 (sharing/bin/settings/invite) not started, blocked. |
| **#994** | desktop: apps/desktop and crates/fuse | **7** | 8 | 5 direct + sub-track **#648** (2 open / 2 closed). #649 host adapters and #650 mount lifecycle are the mass. |
| **#995** | ci: gates, harnesses, build config | **9** | 5 | Cache/perf work is ready; #651 cross-client matrix and #652 nightly are chain-blocked. |
| **#996** | deploy: cutover, release mgmt, dashboards | **3** | 0 | **Zero ready.** All three (#653, #654, #656) are terminal in the dependency graph. |

**Counting flags:**

1. **#992's count is misleading via the API.** #1124 and #1125 are parented to **#872, which is CLOSED**. `gh api issues/992/sub_issues` returns 18 open; the true recursive figure is **20**. Anyone counting the track by its direct sub-issues silently drops two open SECURITY/correctness items.
2. **Two component-label / track mismatches.** #1168 (`fuse: version_block bypasses the chunk cache`) is labeled `comp:desktop` but parented to engine track **#991**. #846 (`no path from host to engine for a measured staging budget`) is labeled `comp:engine` but parented to desktop track **#994**. Neither is wrong exactly — both straddle the boundary — but label-based batching and track-based batching will disagree on these two.
3. **#655 itself carries a stale `blocked_by` edge to closed #893.** Harmless, but it means the umbrella is not a clean "no dependencies" node.

## 4. Critical path — 15 issues

The release funnel is unambiguous from the graph: **#653 (staging cutover) ← #642, #651; #651 ← #643, #650; #643 ← #635, #642; #650 ← #635, #648, #649.** Everything downstream of v2.0.0 terminates in four nodes: **#635 (rotation/grants), #642 (web shell), #648 + #649 (desktop fuse + host adapters).** The blueprint corroborates: rotation, sweep, the name wave, grants, vault settings, version retention and the adoption gate are all marked **required** for v2.0 (`blueprint/engine.md` "Rotation primitives" :413–524, "Grants and ledger" :558–631; "Content plane" :772–782 — *"Retention default: keep all versions within quota, with an explicit user-initiated prune op"*), and all three desktop backends are status-cell **"ship v2.0"** (`blueprint/desktop.md` :124–128).

Within #635 the deepest unmet chain is `#1019 ← {1129, 1130, 1136, 1137, 1139, 1163}` and `#1016 ← {1025, 1163, 1164, 1165, 1166}`. Those ten leaves are all READY and all in the same substrate. **That is the wave-8 core batch.**

| Rank | # | Why it's critical |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **#1169** | Throughput multiplier. `Web E2E Smoke Result` is a *required* check that fails intermittently; every wave pays a re-run tax. Verified untouched: `playwright.config.ts:36` still `retries: 0`, no `expect` timeout override, no first-load readiness condition. |
| 2 | **#1025** | Sweep is blueprint-required (`engine.md` :447–476) and is the top actionable unblock — releases #1016, #1017, #1067. Still a testkit-only wave. |
| 3 | **#1163** | Blocks both #1016 and #1019. `OwnerScopeKeys` has only `#[cfg(test)]` impls (`net/rotation.rs:1665`, `:3123`, below the `cfg(test)` at `:1607`); `writer_pseudonym_signer` has no production caller. Owner rotation cannot name its pseudonym today. |
| 4 | **#1164** | Blocks #1016. `Command::Grant/Revoke/Downgrade` have **no facade arm at all** (`facade.rs:2372` → `EngineError::Unimplemented`); `ImportContact` at `:2348-2364` discards the contact. Grants — a required v2.0 capability — are entirely unreachable. |
| 5 | **#1165** | Blocks #1016 and #1128. `RecordedInvite` is never persisted; `CreateInviteLink` unimplemented. Invites are blueprint-required (`engine.md` :600–605). |
| 6 | **#1166** | Blocks #1016. `ReceivedShareStore`'s only impl is `InMemoryReceivedShareStore` (`testkit/fakes/received_share_store.rs:38`). The accept flow cannot mount in production. |
| 7 | **#1139** | Blocks #1019 and #764. `reseal_scope_root` copies `write_history_link` verbatim (`rotation/reseal.rs:496`); a resumed write rotation cannot recover its seed. Data-loss shaped. |
| 8 | **#1136** | Blocks #1019. `publish_scope_pointer` never inserts into the held set (`net/rotation.rs:1478-1481`), so the **only landed re-point channel lapses at its EOL** — a silent partition of every lagging reader. |
| 9 | **#1137** | Blocks #1019. `publish_repoint` returns `Err(NotLanded)` unconditionally for Mailbox and Tombstone (`net/rotation.rs:1589-1603`). With #1136 unfixed, re-point has zero durable channels. |
| 10 | **#1129 + #1130** | Both block #1019 and are trust-boundary defects: the write wave re-seals against the *record's* commitment rather than the owner-verified one, and trusts an enumeration-snapshot floor instead of re-reading at republish. Fixing these after #1019 lands means re-auditing the wave. |
| 11 | **#1124** | **SECURITY / data loss.** `expand_retire_targets` (`content/retention.rs:199`) takes no retained set, and the `kept` guard at `drain.rs:1683-1697` compares only *root* CIDs — a doomed root naming a retained version's **leaf** CIDs unpins live content. Shipping this destroys user data. |
| 12 | **#1144** | An entire blueprint-required capability is absent end-to-end: `publish_settings` (`settings.rs:466`) has no facade caller and `Command` has no settings variant (`facade.rs:528-544`). The read half is wired; nothing ever publishes. Also blocks #1043. |
| 13 | **#1094** | Version retention is a fixed blueprint **default**, not an enhancement (`engine.md` :772–782). Blocks #1095. Nothing user-facing exists for restore/delete. |
| 14 | **#648 + #649** | The whole desktop leg of the release funnel. Three backends are "ship v2.0"; #649 (FUSE-T/libfuse3/WinFsp adapters) is the largest unstarted deliverable in the repo and gates #650 → #651 → #653/#654. Start #648's remainder now so #649 can begin. |
| 15 | **#913 + #914** | #642 gates both #643 and #653. #913 is a **SECURITY** decision (`coreKit.ts:97` still `storage: window.localStorage` with no gate on the accepted key set — brushes the repo's #1 security rule) and #914 leaves `apps/web` with **no route guard at all** (`App.tsx:8-10` routes `/files/:nodeId?` straight to `FilesPage`). |

<result>I confirmed the diagnosis and then hit the file-ownership wall the brief anticipated. **I made no code changes** — `fix/write-plane-history-link` is still at `a4683d0a3`, no commits, no PR.

## Why I stopped

The fix cannot land without `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs`. Evidence:

1. **The defect is real and as described.** `reseal_scope_root` copies the link verbatim — `crates/engine/src/rotation/reseal.rs:496` (`write_history_link: committed.write_history_link.to_vec()`), fed by the plain byte slice `CommittedSet::write_history_link` at `reseal.rs:135`. Nothing in the repo ever mints one: every construction site passes `&amp;[]` or carries the old value through (14 sites across `net/rotation.rs`, `grants/create.rs`, `grants/invite.rs`, `rotation/{rotate,cascade,sweep}.rs`, `testkit/owner_root.rs`, plus tests).

2. **The only site that could mint is in the forbidden file.** The write-plane cut happens in `WriteWaveNet::reseal_root` (`net/rotation.rs:1288-1336`), which passes `write_history_link: &amp;plane.write_body.write_history_link` at `net/rotation.rs:1326` — the old root's link, carried onto the new root.

3. **The two inputs the mint needs are already in hand there and are dropped.** `root_source` recovers the *old* write scope seed at `net/rotation.rs:1067` and the old write epoch at `1068`, then builds `RootPlane` (`net/rotation.rs:1086-1091`, struct at `857-878`) **without** the seed. So `reseal_root` has the fresh seed but not the previous one — the mint is impossible from where it stands.

4. **No API shape avoids that file.** Adding a field to `ResealSeeds`/`CommittedSet` breaks 14 struct literals in 8 files, 9 of them in files I'm barred from (`net/rotation.rs` ×3, `grants/create.rs` ×4, `grants/invite.rs`, `sweep.rs`). The one zero-blast-radius shape — a standalone `mint_write_history_link` helper in `reseal.rs`, whose bytes are simply passed to the existing `CommittedSet::write_history_link: &amp;[u8]` — still has `net/rotation.rs:1326` as its sole consumer. Routing it through `RepublishedNode` or `RotateScopeWritePlan` instead is also blocked: both are constructed in `net/rotation.rs` (`3240`, `4184`).

5. **The required resume test would be fake without the wiring.** The resume the task asks for is only meaningful across the publisher/resolver seam. Written in `rotate_write.rs` against the in-memory fake it reproduces exactly the sin the issue names — `mid_wave_crash_resumes_from_published_records_only` (`rotate_write.rs:1243`) already passes by handing the seed back in memory at `rotate_write.rs:1256-1261, 1295`.

## The delta to sequence (small, ~10 lines in `net/rotation.rs`)

- `reseal.rs`: add `pub fn mint_write_history_link(entropy, v, scope_id, write_scope_seed, write_epoch, prev: &amp;PrevEpochSeed) -&gt; Result&lt;Vec&lt;u8&gt;, ResealError&gt;` — key `kdf::structure_key(write_scope_seed, STRUCT_TAG_HISTORY_LINK)` (write-plane analogue of `reseal.rs:478`; no collision with the read plane, independent base seeds), AAD `ctx_for(v, scope_id, write_epoch, STRUCT_TAG_HISTORY_LINK)` (the **write** epoch, matching the write-body's own AAD at `reseal.rs:504-509`), payload `HistoryLinkPayload::new(prev.seed, prev.epoch)`, nonce from the injected `Entropy` seam via `fill::&lt;{aead::NONCE_LEN}, E&gt;`. Release-active `Err` when `write_epoch &lt;= prev.epoch` — the write-plane mirror of the read ratchet's `prev_epoch &gt;= epoch` reject at `reseal.rs:257`, so the encode side can never mint a link no walk can step through. `seal_history_link` already zeroizes its plaintext (`core/src/seal/grant.rs:655-658`); the caller keeps ownership of `prev.seed`.
- `net/rotation.rs`: carry the old `write_scope_seed` on `RootPlane` (it is already live at `1067`, just discarded at `1086`), and call the mint in `reseal_root`, replacing `1326`.

## Two design points worth deciding before that lands

- `WriteBody.write_history_link` is a **single** `Vec&lt;u8&gt;`, not a chain (contrast: the read plane retains 64 links, `reseal.rs:56`). So the write ratchet steps back exactly one epoch per published root; recovering further back requires the prior root record, which only lingers for the migration window. That bounds what #764 can reclaim.
- On the recovery direction: a link sealed under the new epoch's key yields the **old** seed from the **new** root — which is what "a resumed name wave enumerates old names via the write-plane history link" (`blueprint/engine.md:419`) needs, and what lets a post-flip resume retire the interior old names it currently abandons (`rotate_write.rs:269-272`). The **fresh** seed is recoverable only via the pointer → new root → owner-write blob; a crash in the window between root republish and pointer flip leaves it genuinely unreachable, and the correct behaviour there stays "mint a new one". Whoever wires `resume_write_scope_seed` (`rotate_write.rs:246-251`) should not be told the history link supplies the fresh seed — it supplies the previous one.

Also noted for sequencing: `rotate_scope_write` has **no production caller** yet — it is exercised only from tests (`net/rotation.rs:4307, 4341, 4370`), and there are currently **zero open PRs** on the repo, so the sibling batch has not pushed.</result>

## #913 — the decision, and why it went the other way

**Decision: keep `localStorage`, and bound the window with an explicit `sessionTime` instead of the storage scope.** The defined default (`sessionStorage`) would have broken a real flow.

**Evidence.** Cross-tab Core Kit restore *is* load-bearing — the one-leader model does not make it moot, it makes it mandatory:

- `packages/client/src/engineClient.ts:322-375` — `promote()` calls `provideFailoverSecret()` → `SecretSource.provideSecret()`, which throws outright with no source.
- `apps/web/src/engine/loginHandoff.ts:63-77` — `LoginSecretSource.provideSecret` calls `_UNSAFE_exportTssKey()` on **this tab's** Core Kit. The secret never crosses the BroadcastChannel; `packages/client/src/broadcastTransport.ts:143-152` says a follower never receives it.
- `apps/web/src/auth/CoreKitProvider.tsx:45-49` builds a session and calls `restore()` **per tab**; `apps/web/src/auth/useAuth.ts:151-157` arms the secret source only after that tab's own handoff, gated on `session.isLoggedIn()`.

So under `sessionStorage`, a tab the user did not personally log in through (bookmark, second window) renders signed out *and*, when the leader dies, aborts promotion — the origin loses its engine and every remaining tab churns the lock.

**Cost accepted:** the store stays readable by any same-origin script. Both review passes independently established the value in it is not an opaque handle — `corekit_store.sessionId` is a secp256k1 scalar that both addresses and decrypts a Web3Auth-held record carrying `postBoxKey`/`factorKey`/`signatures`, with no `allowedOrigin` binding. The comment now says that rather than the reassuring half.

**`sessionTime` = 8h, not 2h.** I started at 2h; both reviews found in the SDK source that `sessionTime` also feeds `Torus.setSessionTime` → `session_token_exp_second`, which expires the `signatures` `_UNSAFE_exportTssKey` needs. 2h would have capped the *working session* at 2h and made a mid-session failover promotion fail silently over a UI still rendering signed in. 8h outlasts a working day and still does not survive a night on a shared machine (24h does).

**Also fixed, found by the crypto pass:** the SDK's `logout()` blanks its session id *in place* and leaves the rest of its store standing — a device factor share included, once MFA is reachable. `Web3AuthSession.logout()` now clears the store, on the refused path too.

## #914 — BLOCKED, not attempted

Threading a session/auth event unavoidably edits `crates/engine/src/facade.rs`, which you told me to stop on:

- The `Event` enum is `facade.rs:598-660`; Rust cannot extend it from another file.
- `Command::Logout` (`facade.rs:521`) has **no arm at all** — it falls through to `Unimplemented` at `facade.rs:2373`. `Engine::start` (`facade.rs:1672-1818`) emits nothing auth-shaped. Both emit sites are in `facade.rs`.
- No facade getter for session state exists (`Engine.started` is private, `Engine::session()` is `pub(crate)`).

Downstream is then mechanical: `crates/wasm/src/lib.rs:736-747` (exhaustive `kind` match, compiler-enforced) + a payload getter; `packages/client/src/worker/protocol.ts:163` union; `commandCodec.ts:255` `readEvent` (its `default` throws). Items 2–4 all hang off item 1 — item 4's guard is specified "on top of the derived state" — so I landed none of it rather than half-land a guard the next batch rewrites. Sequence me and it is a small diff.

## New tests and red-proofs (every one proven red)

`apps/web/src/auth/coreKit.test.ts`:
- *persists origin-wide…* — red via `storage: window.sessionStorage` → `AssertionError: expected Storage{…(1)} to be Storage{…(3)}`.
- *caps how long a persisted session stays restorable…* — red via `sessionTime: 86_400` → `expected 86400 to be less than 86400`. Keeps `toBeGreaterThan(0)` deliberately: the SDK restores 86400s on a **falsy** value, so an upper bound alone passes on `0`.
- *is cleared on logout…* — red by deleting the `finally` teardown → `expected '{"sessionId":"a-session-id"}' to be null`.

## Two things you should know

1. **I edited `tests/web-e2e/tests/smoke.spec.ts`, not a `login*` file.** `tests/web-e2e/playwright.config.ts` sets `testMatch: '**/smoke.spec.ts'` for the `e2e` project — a new `login.spec.ts` would never run, and P1 owns that config. Appended at end of file to minimise conflict.
2. **The e2e assertion is deliberately narrow and I said so in the code.** CI's build carries no `VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID`, so `createCoreKitSession` throws and Core Kit never constructs — the allow-list gates that *nothing else* starts persisting. The unit test is the real gate on the storage decision.

## The hypothesis can't be tested — there is no "before"

**The `Web E2E Smoke` job was created on 2026-08-05.** It did not exist on 2026-08-02 when `565a2d6b1` landed.

- **Before 2026-08-02T00:00Z: 0 executions, 0 failures**
- **2026-08-02 → 2026-08-05T00:00Z: 0 executions, 0 failures**
- **On/after 2026-08-05T00:00Z: 126 executions** (116 actually ran; 9 skipped, 1 cancelled), **8 failures** = 6 Playwright flakes + 2 infra

Widening the sweep to 2026-07-01 cannot help, so I didn't burn the API calls. Proof from git:

- `git log -S'Web E2E Smoke' -- .github/workflows/ci.yml` → exactly one commit, `3d087d673` (2026-08-05 23:29:20 +0200)
- Same for `.github/workflows/web-e2e.yml`, the reusable workflow ci.yml calls → same single commit
- `git show 5516bd8e1:.github/workflows/ci.yml | grep web-e2e` (the 2026-08-04 version) returns **nothing** — ci.yml didn't call it at all; web-e2e.yml's only job was then named `Web E2E Tests`, the v1 suite
- `tests/web-e2e/playwright.config.ts` in its v2 form was created by that same commit
- PR #1078 `feat/809-web-introspection-hook-and-e2e-smoke-gate`: created `2026-08-05T11:05:16Z`, merged `2026-08-05T21:29:20Z`

Earliest possible execution anywhere is 2026-08-05T11:05Z; observed earliest is **2026-08-05T11:06:03Z** on the PR's own branch. "Failures started 2026-08-05" is fully explained by "the job started existing on 2026-08-05."

## What the data does say

The first failure is the **12th execution ever** — 2026-08-05T23:33:30Z, job 92475698569, about 2h10m after the gate merged to main. Eleven clean, then a flake. **The gate never had a clean period.** Nothing regressed on 08-05; the gate started observing a condition already present in main.

That leaves the SW hypothesis *consistent* — `565a2d6b1` was in main from 08-02, three days before anything could watch for it — but not *established*. CI has no before/after to offer. Settling it needs a bisect (run the suite at a commit before `565a2d6b1`, or with `serviceWorkers: 'block'`), not more log archaeology.

<result>Done. **PR #1177** (draft) on `fix/rotation-repoint-channels`, head `5310a9d72`. Two of the four issues closed; the other two were not landable inside this batch's file ownership and are triaged on the issues themselves.

## Closed

**#1129 — re-seal bound to the owner-authorized commitment.** `WriteWaveNet` gains `authorized_commitment: &amp;GrantSetCommitment`. `remint_grants` refuses release-active when the resolved record's `section.commitment` is not that set, then mints from the authorized one. The comparison is full struct equality, so `entries`, `ownerPseudonymPk` and preserved unknowns are all covered, not just `ipnsName`. I deliberately did **not** re-verify the owner signature on the field: the adoption gate already owner-verifies the record's commitment and name-binds it (`gate/adoption.rs:545-552`, and the at-floor recovery re-uses that same authenticated candidate per `net/adopter.rs:226-229`), so equality with it *is* the proof — a separate `verify_grant_set` would have been dead code.

**#1130 — write-epoch floor re-read at the root republish.** The floor is re-read immediately before the seal. The crypto gate caught a residual in my first cut: `node.write_epoch &lt;= max(snapshot, live_floor)` still accepted the window `snapshot &lt; floor &lt; node.write_epoch`, where the parked record has been superseded by another device's re-point but the epoch still clears. Since `plane.write_epoch` is itself a floor read, the correct refusal is `write_floor &gt; plane.write_epoch`, which is what landed.

## Not closed — evidence

**#1136** — the fix needs `HeldRecord`'s shape *and* the `HeldRecords` key type. Both reach `sync/drain.rs` production code (literal at `2247-2255`; keyed inserts at `1229/1637/2155/2340`), which another batch owns. The key is also the blocker #1043 explicitly defers to #1144: the pointer has no node id, and `scope_id` collides with the scope root's own held-set key (`facade.rs:2100`). `WriteWaveNet` also has no held-set handle and no production construction site.

**#1137** — asymmetric. The **tombstone** has no `movedTo` record type, encoder, decoder or chase resolver anywhere; `blueprint/api.md` lists it as an open edge. Landing it means inventing a format; the issue's alternative (delete the variant) is `rotation/rotate_write.rs`, off-limits here. The **mailbox** payload *is* specified (`blueprint/engine.md`: "the same owner-signed re-point object as the pointer record"), so the stub's "unspecified" comment is wrong — but landing it needs a 7th generic parameter for the `Mailbox` seam plus recipient plumbing, and there is no consumer at all (`FocusTarget::MailboxPoll` has no executor, `poll_verified` has zero production callers). Posting alone would publish into a void.

<result>All gates green. Done.

## What changed — PR #1173 (draft), branch `fix/prune-retention-aliasing`, head `3969b37da`

### #1124 — prune subtracts the retained versions

`Drain::prune_debt` (`crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs`) expands every doomed **and** every retained version off its own fetched root block — **ahead of the publish**, so a debt the pass cannot compute leaves the history it read standing — and subtracts the union of the retained expansions from each doomed target set. `OwedRetire` (`crates/engine/src/seams/retire_ledger.rs`) gained `retained` (the aliased CIDs, normally empty) and `manifest_bytes` (the expansion's bound), so `retire_owed` skips them and `owed_bytes` is what the retire actually frees. `Expansion` now carries a per-target byte figure (`RetireTarget`) because a subtracted leaf frees nothing and the closed form `size + 40n + root_len` no longer holds.

### #1125 — one CID-set derivation

`registry_cids` is gone. Both paths go through `version_cids(root, leaves, RootPlacement)` in `content/retention.rs`, with the ordering an explicit argument **of the call**. `the_retire_expansion_names_exactly_what_registration_pinned` pins the two sets equal.

### Gate exit codes (all 0)

`cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (38 suites, 0 failures), `cargo check -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`. CI: **Engine Tests, Core KATs, Cargo Check &amp; Test ×3, Lint, Tracker Refs, Web E2E Smoke, Contract Suite — all SUCCESS**; TS/API gates SKIPPED by path filter (no TS in the diff).

### New tests and red-proof (every one proven red with the production change reverted)

- `a_doomed_root_naming_a_retained_versions_leaf_never_retires_that_leaf` (`tests/write_plane.rs`) — **the adversarial shape**: a planted root that addresses correctly and whose link count matches its declared `size`, linking the owner's live first leaf plus one block of its own. Red twice with `prune_debt`'s protected set emptied: first on the reclaim figure (`217` vs `161` — it quoted the hostage leaf's bytes), then, with that assert removed, on `"the leaf the retained head lives on is never retired"`.
- `a_prune_whose_root_no_source_serves_spends_its_budget_and_dead_letters` — red by reverting the halt class: hits `"the attempt budget must be finite"` at 50 passes.
- `the_retire_expansion_names_exactly_what_registration_pinned` — red by dropping a leaf from `RootPlacement::First`.
- `a_target_a_retained_version_also_names_is_never_retired`, `the_reclaim_figure_counts_only_the_targets_a_retire_still_names` — red with `split_retained`'s predicate neutered.
- `a_retained_target_is_never_named_by_the_retire_that_carries_it`, `an_entry_that_protects_less_than_the_incoming_one_is_replaced`, `an_entry_whose_retained_set_names_its_own_root_retires_nothing`, `a_stored_entry_this_build_did_not_write_reads_as_nothing` — each red against its own reverted guard.
- The `RetireLedger` conformance kit now pins the retained set durable and widening-only — red via `cipherbox-desktop-seams`.

### Review gates — two live defects found and fixed

`/simplify`, `/security-review` and `/crypto-privacy-review` all ran on `git diff main...HEAD`.

1. **An unfetchable root wedged the whole op queue.** `expand_version` mapped a `read_block` failure to `Halt::Unclassified`, which charges nothing — one grantee-authored version whose root is never served held the FIFO head, and every op behind it, forever. Now charged as `Halt::UploadAttempt`.
2. **`RetireLedger::owe` could lose a retained set.** It skipped any held target outright; one root `contentCid` can ride two nodes' histories, so a prune could find a `retained: []` entry from an unrelated prune and lose its protection — re-introducing the exact class. An entry that protects less is now replaced whole; protection only widens.

Quality fold-in: `split_retained` states the predicate once and dedups the held set (a manifest may repeat a link); `decode_entry` takes the target from the key; the pass-through `owe_retires`/`registry_cids` wrappers are inlined; the pin-row rationale is stated once at its home; `version_cids`/`RootPlacement` are off the crate-root re-export. `expand_retire_targets`' doc claimed its bounds stop a hand-framed root naming arbitrary CIDs — they do not, since the same author supplies the `size` the plan quotes from — corrected.

### Residual, filed as #1176

Both security reviewers independently flagged that the protected set is only the **pruned file's own** survivors. A grantee can read another file's root block off the gateway (they are keyless plaintext) and author a version whose `links` are *that* file's live leaves — same unpin, one indirection away. Plus the snapshot/TOCTOU window and two denial-of-reclaim vectors. Closing it wants an account-wide live-CID view the engine lacks, or a registry that reference-counts per referencing record — beyond what #1124 scopes. **#1176** carries all three, labelled `v2-build`/`comp:engine`, milestone v2.0.0, `blocked_by` #1124, and attached as a sub-issue of umbrella #992.

### Scope note

Beyond the files I was given I also touched `crates/engine/src/net/retire.rs`, `src/seams/retire_ledger.rs` and `src/testkit/conformance/retire_ledger.rs` — the retained set has to reach the ledger drain, and none of those are on the forbidden list. `chunk.rs` needed no change. `publish_prune`'s publish-then-journal ordering (#1170) is untouched.</result>

## #1152 — wasm-bindgen-cli from a prebuilt binary

`taiki-e/install-action@6c6fd71fe4fb72c3697d269963d0e15df8adedad # v2.85.10`, SHA-pinned (SHA verified against the tag). **Measured on this PR's own runs: the whole composite takes 11–16s** vs 104s for the install alone. All five call sites pass. `~/.cargo/bin` dropped from four cache path lists — it held only the from-source build.

Three hardening changes came from the security pass, not from me:
- The Cargo.lock version becomes an install-action tool spec, and **that input splits on whitespace and commas** — `version = "0.2.126 evil-crate"` survived the old `sed` and would have `cargo install`ed a second unpinned crate. Now requires a bare three-part version. The old `cargo install ... --version "$V"` was injection-proof by construction; the rewrite lost that, and I put it back.
- **`fallback: none`**, not `cargo-install`. The default `cargo-binstall` wants `github.token`, and on a version the pinned manifest lacks it silently trades the archive's sha256 for an unverified source build — the exact cost this change removes, and it would go green.
- A step asserts the CLI on PATH is the locked version; install-action prints it but asserts nothing.

## #1169 — the flake

**The issue's premise is wrong and I did not implement what it asked for.** It is not a first-paint race: `goto` completes cleanly and the next call dies ~1s later. The `toBeVisible` failure *aborted* at 1321ms against a 5000ms budget, and one failure died inside a `waitForFunction` — itself a readiness wait. No timeout or readiness condition could fix either.

Root cause, from `DEBUG=pw:browser` on a probe run: **`Received signal 11 SEGV_MAPERR`**. Playwright's default `chrome-headless-shell` segfaults on a page that registers a Service Worker. Fix is `channel: 'chromium'` — Chrome's own headless.

I could not reproduce locally (16 runs, then 200 executions at 2 workers with the box CPU-saturated, all green), so I A/B'd in CI on throwaway branches at `--repeat-each=25`, 125 executions per run:

| probe | runs failed |
|---|---|
| unchanged | 2 of 3 |
| after the `NODE_ENV` fix, still on the shell | 2 of 3 |
| `serviceWorkers: 'block'` | 0 of 3 |
| `channel: 'chromium'` | 0 of 3 |
| **final, merged content** | **0 of 3** |

Blocking Service Workers also works but drops production behaviour from a gate whose premise is that the shipping artifact is the one tested.

Two further defects surfaced on the way, both fixed:
- **The suite was testing development bundles.** `NODE_ENV: test` sat at job scope, and Vite reads it to decide whether a build is production. `release-bundle.spec.ts` — whose entire subject is the shipping artifact — was asserting against a dev build. Two new tests assert the tab comes under `/sw.js`; red-proved in one run against both bundles side by side (`[release]` passed, `[e2e]` built with `NODE_ENV=test` failed with `Received has value: null`).
- **The per-test login secret was published** as a `page.evaluate` argument, recorded verbatim into a trace this public repo uploads. Now minted in-page.

`tests/web-e2e/fixtures.ts` taps console/pageerror/requestfailed/crash/close and fails with the cause — it is what surfaced the MIME error that unravelled this.

I initially wrote up a timeline argument (Service Worker landed 08-02, flakes start 08-05). **That was wrong** — the job did not exist before 08-05, so there is no before/after. Removed. The honest figure is 6 genuine flakes in 114 executions that reached Playwright (5.3%); 2 further failures were infra. ## Verdicts

**A — `action.yml:29` ambiguous `wasm-bindgen` pin: REAL. Fixed.**

Confirmed the mechanism rather than assuming it. The "Verify the CLI on PATH matches Cargo.lock" step (`action.yml:46-55`) compares the installed CLI against `steps.locked.outputs.version` — the *same* value the extractor produced — so a wrong pick passed its own safety net instead of tripping it. It's worse than "arbitrary": Cargo.lock sorts a name's entries by ascending version, so `head -1` deterministically picked the **lowest**, meaning a second entry would silently downgrade the CLI while the assertion still went green.

Replaced with an awk pass over `[[package]]` blocks plus an exact-count guard. Kept the three-part-semver validation (it's what blocks whitespace/comma injection into install-action's tool spec) — layered, not replaced.

My own security pass caught a fail-open I'd introduced: `[ "" -ne 1 ]` returns 2 with "integer expression expected", which is *falsy*, so an empty `COUNT` would skip the guard and a multi-line value would then slip past `grep -Eq` (which matches if any line matches). Hardened to `${COUNT:-0}`.

**B — `vault.page.ts:30` hex codec: REFUSED, with evidence.**

Three independent reasons, all posted on the thread:

1. **The suggestion misreads the code.** The decode + 32-byte assert it asks to "move into `loginHandoff.ts`" is already there — `fromHex` at `loginHandoff.ts:28`, length check at `:33-36`. The page object only *encodes*; it never decodes.
2. **Acting on it would be a coverage regression.** The hex string is the shape of the real Core Kit seam (`LoginSecretExporter._UNSAFE_exportTssKey(): Promise&lt;string&gt;`, `loginHandoff.ts:10-12`). `introspection.ts:65-68` impersonates that interface precisely so the E2E cold start drives the *production* handoff — decode, length assert, buffer copy, zeroize. Taking a `Uint8Array` would route the suite around `exportLoginSecret`, stripping the only runtime coverage from the code that validates and scrubs the login secret.
3. **The Security &amp; Privacy framing doesn't hold.** Your read was right: `introspection.ts:57` early-returns unless `VITE_E2E_HOOK === 'true'`; `vite.config.ts:100-102` throws at build time for a deployed build; `config.ts:130-131` + `config.test.ts:131-149` pin the rule both directions.

I also checked the "smaller genuine point" you flagged. The un-zeroizable hex string is equally true in production (`_UNSAFE_exportTssKey` returns a string there too), and `loginHandoff.ts:17-21` already documents it with the mitigation. Separately, `toHex` is *unreachable* from that call site — the code runs inside `page.evaluate` in the browser realm and cannot close over a Node-side import, so reusing it would require a new `window` tap to avoid a one-line encoder. Net: no defect worth a production-adjacent API change.

## Red-proof

No test harness exists for composite actions in this repo, and inventing one for a single action would be scope creep on an already-large PR — so I proved it by executing both extractors against synthetic lockfiles:

| fixture | old | new |
|---|---|---|
| one entry | exit 0, `0.2.126` | exit 0, `0.2.126` |
| zero entries | exit 1 | exit 1, "found 0" |
| **two entries `0.2.100`+`0.2.126`** | **exit 0, `0.2.100`** | **exit 1, "found 2"** |
| real `Cargo.lock` | — | exit 0, `0.2.126` |

The two-entry row is the regression closed. Empty-`COUNT` also verified fail-closed.

**One thing worth your attention:** finding A is arguably a *security* fix, not just data integrity as CodeRabbit labelled it — a PR that adds a lower-versioned duplicate `wasm-bindgen` entry to `Cargo.lock` could have steered CI into installing a downgraded CLI, with the version assertion still passing. That's a supply-chain steering vector, and it was live on `main` before this PR too.</result>

## Per-finding verdicts

**1. `coreKit.test.ts:44` — sessionTime range — ACCEPTED.** Now asserts `toBe(28_800)` exactly. Dropped the range comment; the "why eight hours" rationale already lives once at `SESSION_SECONDS` in `coreKit.ts`.

**2. `coreKit.test.ts:53` — logout branches — ACCEPTED.** Made the SDK mock drivable per case (status, status-after-login, logout rejection, call count) and added three behaviour tests: SDK logout rejects (store cleared, refusal propagates); status not `LOGGED_IN` (SDK logout never called, store cleared); login lands `REQUIRED_SHARE` with a refused rollback (login throws, store cleared).

**3. `coreKit.ts:85` — rollback cleanup — ACCEPTED, premise corrected.** Verified in mpc-core-kit 3.5.0: the failed login writes nothing — `currentStorage.set("sessionId", …)` only runs from `createSession()` via `finalizeTkey()` (`dist/lib.esm/mpcCoreKit.js:1091`, `:1171`), which needs `state.factorKey`, exactly what `REQUIRED_SHARE` lacks. What *does* survive a refused rollback is a residual entry: a stale `sessionId` the SDK's logout only blanks in place (`:788`), and a device-factor share (`:852`) once MFA is reachable. Real asymmetry, so both teardown paths now go through one private `clearStore()`.

**4. `coreKit.ts:111` — bearer material in localStorage — REFUSED for this PR, already tracked.** Established what's in the store and who writes it, per your instruction: the **vendor SDK** writes it via `AsyncStorage(this._storageBaseKey, options.storage)` (`:78`). This diff does **not** introduce the persistence — `storage: window.localStorage` is already on `main`; the PR only names the store, caps the session, and adds logout teardown. No `privateKey` and no seed lands there, so it is not a literal AGENTS.md rule 1 breach — but `sessionId` *is* a bearer capability, and the risk is worse than CodeRabbit stated: `new SessionManager({ sessionTime })` (`:80`) passes **no `allowedOrigin`**, so possession alone redeems the Web3Auth-held record (`postBoxKey`, `factorKey`, `tssShare`, `signatures`) offline from any origin. The proposed remedy buys nothing (sessionStorage/IndexedDB/"non-exportable" all sit behind the same same-origin API), and `disableSessionManager` would delete follower promotion — the very behaviour this PR decides. **Issue #1175 "web: seal the Core Kit store under a non-extractable key" already exists**, labelled `v2-build`, natively `blocked_by` #913, carrying the AES-GCM `IAsyncStorage` design and its gate. No new issue filed.

**5. `smoke.spec.ts:73` — allow-list exactness — ACCEPTED, suggestion refused literally.** The hole is real, but CodeRabbit's `/^(?:loglevel|corekit_store)$/` **would turn a passing gate red**: `loglevel` persists one key per *named* logger as `loglevel:&lt;name&gt;` (`loglevel@1.9.2/lib/loglevel.js:154-157`), and the torus stack builds five (`http-helpers`, `torus.js`, `mpc-core-kit`, `broadcast-channel`, `customauth`). I probed it empirically — importing `@web3auth/mpc-core-kit` under jsdom leaves `["loglevel:http-helpers","loglevel:torus.js","loglevel:mpc-core-kit"]`. Anchored the namespace instead: `/^(?:loglevel(?::[\w.-]+)?|corekit_store)$/`, verified to reject `loglevel-unexpected`, `loglevelfoo`, `corekit_store2`.

<result>## Findings — issue #1175 recon

Note on baseline: the working tree is at `cfe5a6873`, one commit **behind** `origin/main` `390a579b5`.
