## Q1 — Does an async storage wrapper drop in? **Yes on the SDK side. No on ours, without changes.**

**The option accepts it.** `«ck»/dist/types/interfaces.d.ts:296` — `storage: IAsyncStorage | IStorage;`. `interfaces.d.ts:16-24` defines both; `IAsyncStorage.getItem(key): Promise&lt;string|null&gt;` / `setItem(key,value): Promise&lt;void&gt;`, plus a vestigial `async?: boolean` that is **never read** anywhere in `dist/lib.esm/`.

**`AsyncStorage` wraps a sync store transparently.** `«ck»/dist/lib.esm/helper/browserStorage.js:21-57` — every method is `async` and `await`s the underlying store: `toJSON` `:29`, `resetStore` `:36-37`, `getStore` `:41`, `get` `:44`, `set` `:48,:50`, `remove` `:53,:55`. `await` on a non-promise is a no-op, so one code path serves both interfaces.

**Every SDK store touch is awaited.** Complete grep of `dist/lib.esm/` for `getItem|setItem|localStorage|sessionStorage` hits only `helper/browserStorage.js` and a dead `utils.js:63-76`. The six real sites:

| `«ck»/dist/lib.esm/mpcCoreKit.js` | call | awaited |
|---|---|---|
| `:78` | `new AsyncStorage(this._storageBaseKey, options.storage)` | constructor, no I/O |
| `:232` | `await this.currentStorage.get('sessionId')` in `init()` | yes |
| `:788` | `await this.currentStorage.set('sessionId','')` in `logout()` | yes (comment `:787` says "to accommodate async storage") |
| `:852` / `:859` | `setDeviceFactor` / `getDeviceFactor` | yes |
| `:1171` | `await this.currentStorage.set('sessionId', …)` in `createSession()` | yes |

No sync fast-path, no direct `localStorage[key]`, no `store[key]` read. Constructor `:56-85`, `init()` `:174-252`, `commitChanges()` `:818-833` (remote metadata only), `logout()` `:782-794`, `resetState()` `:1305-1313` (in-memory only, never nulls `currentStorage`), `_UNSAFE_exportTssKey` `:871` — all clean. `this.storageLayer` (`:34,197,205,1309`) is the *remote* `TorusStorageLayer`, unrelated.

**Where it breaks: our own code, landed by #1174.**
- `apps/web/src/auth/coreKit.ts:37` types the field `private readonly store: Storage` and `:91-93` does a **synchronous** `this.store.removeItem(this.coreKit._storageKey)`. Called from three places: `logout()` `:82`, the REQUIRED_SHARE rollback `:64`. An `IAsyncStorage` wrapper has no `removeItem` and no sync surface — `clearStore` must become `Promise&lt;void&gt;` and the field must stop being `Storage`.
- `_storageKey` is read directly at `coreKit.ts:92` (getter at `«ck»/dist/lib.esm/mpcCoreKit.js:99-101`). That is fine but it's the one place the app bypasses the wrapper.
- The #1174 gate test asserts the *identity* of the store: `apps/web/src/auth/coreKit.test.ts:63` — `expect(sdk.options?.storage).toBe(window.localStorage)` — and four more tests (`:79,:89,:100,:112`) read the plaintext blob back synchronously with `window.localStorage.getItem(STORE_KEY)`. All five need rewriting by #1175.
- The web-e2e allow-list `tests/web-e2e/tests/smoke.spec.ts:73` is `/^(?:loglevel(?::[\w.-]+)?|corekit_store)$/` and `:93` asserts `sessionStorage` is empty. It does **not** cover IndexedDB, so a wrapping-key DB would pass silently — worth extending.

**Store layout:** single JSON blob under one key `corekit_store` (`mpcCoreKit.js:38`), fields `sessionId` and one entry per padded tkey pubkey-X holding `{factorKey}` (`:851,:858`). So the wrapper only ever sees `getItem('corekit_store')` / `setItem('corekit_store', json)`.

**Caveat:** `AsyncStorage.set` (`browserStorage.js:47-51`) is a non-atomic read-modify-write over the whole blob. Sequential today; see Q4.

Unaffected by the `storage` option: `@toruslabs/customauth@20.4.0` writes its own `torus_login_${scope}` keys straight to `window.localStorage` (`.../customauth/dist/lib.esm/utils/StorageHelper.js:47,52,76,82`) during the OAuth redirect. Not sealed by #1175.

---

## Q2 — `allowedOrigin`: real, server-enforced, but **not reachable** through mpc-core-kit 3.5.0

**It is not plumbed through.** `«ck»/dist/lib.esm/mpcCoreKit.js:79-82`:
```js
this.sessionManager = new SessionManager({ sessionTime: options.sessionTime });
```
`sessionTime` is the only option passed. `allowedOrigin` has **zero** occurrences in `«ck»/dist/lib.esm/*.js` and `«ck»/dist/types/` (options list at `interfaces.d.ts:296-306`). So today the session is always created with the default.

**Default is `"*"`.** `@toruslabs/session-manager@3.2.0` at `.../session-manager/dist/lib.esm/sessionManager.js:29-33` — falsy `allowedOrigin` (including `false` or `""`) becomes `"*"`.

**It is sent, never checked client-side.** Written into the request body at `sessionManager.js:50` (`POST {base}/v2/store/set`, `:54`) and `:97` (`PUT /v2/store/update`, `:101`). `authorizeSession` (`:60-85`, types at `dist/types/sessionManager.d.ts:12-14`) takes only `{headers}`, posts `{key, namespace}` to `/v2/store/get` `:73`, and performs **no** origin comparison anywhere. Base URL `https://api.web3auth.io/session-service` (`@toruslabs/constants@14.2.0/dist/lib.esm/constants.js:79`).

**Therefore enforcement is necessarily server-side.** I cannot prove the server enforces it from installed client code — flagging that plainly. The strongest available evidence is the vendor's own live-network integration test, `.../session-manager/test/SessionManager.test.ts:166-176`, which asserts `authorizeSession({headers:{origin:'https://malicious-site.com'}})` **rejects**, and `:205-214` that a missing origin header also rejects. No client code can produce those rejections.

**Would it break `EngineClient.promote`? No.** `promote` never calls `authorizeSession` — `packages/client/src/engineClient.ts:322-367` closes the follower transport `:329-330`, spawns the worker `:338`, and calls `provideFailoverSecret()` `:342` → `apps/web/src/engine/loginHandoff.ts:64-78,:23` → `_UNSAFE_exportTssKey()` on the tab's **already-init'ed** Core Kit (`«ck»/dist/lib.esm/mpcCoreKit.js:871`, no store or network access). `authorizeSession` runs only inside `init()` (`mpcCoreKit.js:237`), which every tab already does from the app origin. Binding to the app origin would break nothing.

**But it is weaker than it looks.** The signature at `sessionManager.js:43` covers `keccak256(encData)` only — `allowedOrigin`, `timeout` and `namespace` travel **outside** the signed payload. An attacker holding the stolen `sessionId` holds the private scalar and can therefore sign an `updateSession` (`:92-104`) whose body carries *their* `allowedOrigin: "*"`. Whether the server pins the value at first-set is not observable from client code.

**Cost:** the field is public (`mpcCoreKit.js:118,234,1140`), so the only route is replacing `coreKit.sessionManager` with your own `SessionManager` after construction and before `init()`/`createSession`. That is an unsupported reach into SDK internals, not a config line. It is cheaper than #1175 in code volume but relies on undocumented server behaviour plus an unsigned field — I would treat it as a **complement**, not a substitute.

---

## Q3 — Desync failure behaviour: **two modes, and the bad one wedges the app**

**Mode A — parseable but stale/unredeemable session: graceful.** `«ck»/dist/lib.esm/mpcCoreKit.js:237-239` swallows the `authorizeSession` rejection (`.catch(async err =&gt; { log.error(...) })`), and `rehydrateSession`'s own throws (`:1103,:1107`) are caught and only warned at `:1127-1129`. `init()` resolves, `isLoggedIn()` is false, user lands at login, store still usable.

**Mode B — corrupt / undecryptable blob: propagates, then wedges.** `browserStorage.js:43-45` does `JSON.parse((await getItem(...)) || "{}")`; the call at `mpcCoreKit.js:230-232` sits in an `init()` body (`:174-252`) with **no try/catch**. So the throw escapes `restore()`.

Where it lands — `apps/web/src/auth/CoreKitProvider.tsx:66-72`:
```js
const settle = () =&gt; { ... if (live) setValue(settled); };
restore.current.then(settle, settle);
```
The **same** `settle` handles fulfil and reject, and `settled` (`:61-65`) is `{session, status:'ready', error:null}`. So a rejected restore is indistinguishable from "restored, signed out": no unhandled rejection, no console surface. `apps/web/src/auth/useAuth.ts:56-57` then computes `isSignedOut === true`, `apps/web/src/routes/FilesPage.tsx:15-17` bounces `/files` to `/`, and `LoginPage.tsx:53,63,74` renders **enabled** login buttons. It fails closed to the front door.

**But it does not self-heal, and it poisons the forward path.** Nothing in `apps/web/src` clears `corekit_store` on a failed restore. A fresh login hits the same `JSON.parse` at `mpcCoreKit.js:1171` (`createSession`) and throws again, surfacing as a raw `SyntaxError` message via `useAuth.ts:67-69` → `LoginPage.tsx:78`. Logout cannot rescue it either: `coreKit.ts:80` is `if (this.isLoggedIn()) await this.coreKit.logout()` and `isLoggedIn()` is false — though #1174's `finally { this.clearStore(); }` at `:81-83` **does** now unconditionally remove the key, so an explicit sign-out click is the escape hatch. A user who never clicks sign-out stays wedged across reloads. There is no `ErrorBoundary` anywhere in `apps/web/src`.

**Why this matters for #1175:** under IDB eviction the wrapping key is *gone* while the blob remains — the expected steady-state failure, not an edge case. An AES-GCM `decrypt` rejection inside `getItem` reproduces Mode B exactly. The wrapper must instead **fail closed by returning `null` and purging the stale blob**, converting the eviction into Mode A. That behaviour is not currently covered by any test: there is no `CoreKitProvider.test.tsx`, the only `restore` overrides in tests are never-settling promises (`apps/web/src/routes/FilesPage.test.tsx:39,:52`), the fake defaults to success (`apps/web/src/test/authFakes.tsx:87`), and `tests/web-e2e/` has zero references to `localStorage` or `corekit_store`.

---

## Q4 — Multi-tab: **every tab holds a live Core Kit session, unserialised**

**Blueprint** (`/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/blueprint/web-client.md`): leader election on an exclusive Web Lock `cipherbox-engine` `:88-92`; followers are thin mirrors that spawn no worker and "hold no keys" `:93-95`; failover `:124-127`; Core Kit runs on the UI thread and "login **or session restore** exports the login secret" `:191-196`. The blueprint **never** mentions `corekit_store`, localStorage sealing, or a wrapping key, and never scopes Core Kit to the leader. This is unspecified territory, not unimplemented spec.

**Implementation.** Election `packages/client/src/leadership.ts:59-79`; lock name `packages/client/src/broadcast.ts:138` used at `engineClient.ts:149`; real `navigator.locks` wired at `apps/web/src/engine/createEngineClient.ts:16`.

**Every tab.** `apps/web/src/main.tsx:45` wraps `&lt;App/&gt;` in `&lt;CoreKitProvider createSession={() =&gt; createCoreKitSession(...)}&gt;` unconditionally, sibling to `EngineProvider` `:42`. `CoreKitProvider.tsx:24` — "Called once per tab"; `:48-49` `session.current ??= factory.current(); restore.current ??= session.current.restore();` with **no** role or lock condition. Stronger: every tab also **exports the TSS key** — `useAuth.ts:151-157` runs the handoff whenever `isReady &amp;&amp; !isAuthenticated &amp;&amp; session.isLoggedIn()`; the follower only zeroes it afterwards (`engineClient.ts:170`, `broadcastTransport.ts:143-151`).

**`promote` does not touch the store** — see Q2. No `restore()`, no `init()`, no store read or write.

**Concurrent cold start: yes, nothing serialises it.** Every `locks.request` in the repo is at `leadership.ts:61`, `broadcastTransport.ts:124`, `leaderRelay.ts:253` — none in `apps/web/src/auth/**`. `CoreKitProvider` takes no lock (`:23-27`). The only serialisation is **intra-tab**: the `??=` latches at `CoreKitProvider.tsx:48-49` (documented `:29-34` as a StrictMode-remount guard) and module-scoped `inFlight`/`restoredFor` at `useAuth.ts:43-44` — per-realm, explicitly not cross-tab. So N tabs would run a wrapping-key get-or-create simultaneously, and the non-atomic `AsyncStorage.set` read-modify-write over one localStorage key has no cross-tab atomicity.

**Store writes today:** login only (`coreKit.ts:51` `loginWithOAuth` → `createSession`, `:67` `commitChanges`) and logout (`:63`, `:80`, plus #1174's unconditional `clearStore` `:82`). `setDeviceFactor` is unreachable — `enableMFA` has zero call sites in `apps/web/src` or `packages/client/src`, consistent with `coreKit.ts:60-63`.

**Reusable primitives:** `LockManagerLike` seam `packages/client/src/leadership.ts:22-29` with a deterministic FIFO fake at `packages/client/src/testkit.ts:289`; lock-naming hygiene rule at `broadcast.ts:143-149` (names are readable origin-wide via `navigator.locks.query()`); an existing memoised IndexedDB opener at `packages/client/src/seams/idb.ts:33-51` (in-realm memo only, `:53-60`). Nothing resembling `crypto.subtle`, `wrap`, or `deriveKey` exists in `apps/web/src` or `packages/client/src` today.

## Q5 — Scope and prior art

**#1175** — OPEN, `v2-build` + `comp:web`, milestone v2.0.0, https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/issues/1175. Full body is in the fetch above; the essentials: wrap `storage` in an `IAsyncStorage` AES-GCM wrapper under a non-extractable key kept in IndexedDB, keep the store origin-wide (per #913), clear the wrapping key on logout alongside #913's teardown. Gate: "web unit, over the wrapper: a round trip through it, and a stored value that is not the plaintext." No `trackedIssues`/`trackedInIssues`, no cross-reference timeline items.

**Parent (native sub-issue):** #642 *web: rebuild the web shell with login and the vault browser*.

**Dependency #913** — **CLOSED**, landed as `390a579b5` (#1174). It set `SESSION_SECONDS = 8h` (`coreKit.ts:107` — note the issue body says 2h, the merged code says 8h after review feedback), kept `window.localStorage` deliberately with the rationale at `coreKit.ts:112-117`, added `clearStore()` `:91-93`, and added both gates (`coreKit.test.ts`, `smoke.spec.ts:73-94`). **#1175 is unblocked.**

**Siblings touching `apps/web/src/auth/` or web session persistence** (open, `v2-build`):
- **#914** *web: derive auth state from the engine instead of a tab-local store* — reduces `authStore` to chrome, adds a `RequireAuth` guard. Overlaps #1175's restore-failure surface (Q3); worth sequencing awareness, no file conflict with `coreKit.ts`.
- **#1083** *client: authenticate the tab-leadership rendezvous on the origin-wide channel* — same same-origin adversary model; explicitly scopes itself to "passive or opportunistic same-origin", the same threat #1175 targets. Different files (`packages/client/src/leaderRelay.ts`).
- **#1033** *web: source the read-accelerator bearer from the session rather than build-time config* — another credential-at-rest question in the web app.
- **#953** *web-client: the browser mailbox seam sends no Authorization bearer and cannot refresh*.
- No open issue owns `apps/web/src/auth/coreKit.ts` other than #1175 — file contention is low.

**Branch `v1` prior art — there IS relevant precedent, though not for Core Kit.**
- v1 did **not** seal the Core Kit store: `v1:apps/web/src/lib/web3auth/core-kit.ts:24` and `v1:apps/desktop/src/auth.ts:81` both pass raw `storage: window.localStorage`. Zero hits on branch `v1` for `IAsyncStorage`, `AsyncStorage`, `corekit_store`, `_storageKey`, `sessionTime`, `allowedOrigin`, `SessionManager`, `subtle.wrapKey`.
- v1 **did** implement the exact primitive twice:
  - `v1:apps/web/src/lib/device/identity.ts:34,50-68,135+` — Ed25519 device keypair in IndexedDB (`cipherbox-device`/`keys`), AES-256-GCM under a **non-extractable** `CryptoKey` (`deriveKey(..., false, ['encrypt','decrypt'])`), HKDF-SHA256 from the vault private key, info `cipherbox-device-key-wrap-v1`, with a `STORAGE_VERSION = 2` migration that discards plaintext v1 entries.
  - `v1:apps/web/src/services/search-index.service.ts:82,204,255,333-346` — same pattern for the MiniSearch index (`cipherbox-search`/`index`, info `cipherbox-search-index-v1`).
- **The load-bearing difference:** both v1 wrappers derive the key by HKDF **from the vault private key**, which is only available *after* login. #1175's wrapper must open *before* login, to restore the session that produces that key — so the derivation approach does not transfer. #1175 has to use `crypto.subtle.generateKey({name:'AES-GCM'}, /* extractable */ false, ...)` and persist the `CryptoKey` object itself in IndexedDB, which is a different (and structurally weaker) construction than v1's. v1's `STORAGE_VERSION` migration and its explicit "keys are memory-only, never persisted" stance (`v1:apps/web/src/stores/auth.store.ts:44`, `v1:apps/web/src/stores/vault.store.ts:45`) are still worth copying.

## Could not establish

1. **Whether `api.web3auth.io/session-service` actually enforces `allowedOrigin` server-side, and whether it pins the value at first `set`.** Client code proves only that it is transmitted and never checked locally; the vendor's live integration test asserts server rejection but that is inference from test expectations. To establish: a live `curl` against `/v2/store/set` then `/v2/store/get` with a mismatched `Origin` header on a throwaway session id, plus a second `/v2/store/update` attempting to rewrite `allowedOrigin` to `"*"`. Both are ~10 minutes of network work against DEVNET.
2. **Whether replacing `coreKit.sessionManager` post-construction actually holds** across `init()` → `rehydrateSession` → `createSession`. The field is public and assigned at `mpcCoreKit.js:79-82` then read at `:118,:234,:1140`, so it looks viable, but it is unsupported and would need a real login run to confirm.
3. **Actual Safari ITP / storage-pressure behaviour on the localStorage-vs-IndexedDB pair.** Both are covered by the same 7-day script-writable-storage eviction on Safari, which argues they evict *together* rather than desynchronising — but I found no evidence in-repo either way, and the eviction is per-store in some pressure scenarios. To establish: a real-device test, or accept the risk and make the wrapper self-healing (Q3) so the answer stops mattering.
4. **Whether the wrapping-key IndexedDB database would be caught by any existing gate.** The web-e2e allow-list (`smoke.spec.ts:87-93`) inspects `localStorage` and `sessionStorage` only; nothing enumerates `indexedDB.databases()`. Establishing this is trivial but it is a scope item for #1175, not a fact I could look up.</result>

<result>## Done — PR #1183 (draft), commit `0abfa18e9`

### #1182: what changed and why

**`apps/web/src/auth/coreKit.ts`** — `Web3AuthSession.restore()` now catches an `init()` failure, clears the store via the existing private `clearStore()`, and rethrows. It also gained a `storeIsReadable()` probe.

The purge trigger is **narrow, not unconditional**, and that was the one substantive change I made beyond the issue's brief. Verified in the installed SDK (`@web3auth/mpc-core-kit@3.5.0`, `dist/lib.esm/mpcCoreKit.js`): `init()` swallows its own rehydrate failure (`:236-238`) and falls through to `await this.featureRequest()` (`:251` → `:1342`), which is a bare `await fetch(...)`. An unconditional purge would therefore delete a **good** store on an offline reload — and that store is origin-wide (`coreKit.ts:126`), so one background tab booting offline evicts what every other tab's leader promotion depends on. Worse, `setDeviceFactor` writes the device factor share into that same blob (`mpcCoreKit.js:843-855`), so once MFA lands this becomes share destruction on a network blip. `storeIsReadable()` probes exactly what the SDK requires — `JSON.parse(raw || '{}')[key]`, i.e. parses *and* is a non-null object.

**`apps/web/src/auth/CoreKitProvider.tsx`** — reject and resolve settle separately; a rejected restore records `RESTORE_FAILED`.

### How the failure surfaces, and why

It goes into the provider's `error`, which `useAuth` returns and `LoginPage.tsx:78` renders. I kept `status: 'ready'` and the session in hand deliberately: `useAuth` gates `login` on `isReady &amp;&amp; session`, so setting `status: 'unavailable'` would have made the user unable to retry — defeating the issue's own gate item 2.

The message is a **fixed string, not the underlying throw, and it is not logged**. V8 quotes the offending input in `JSON.parse` failures — I confirmed `JSON.parse('secret-key-bytes')` throws ``Unexpected token 's', "secret-key-bytes" is not valid JSON`` — and that input is bearer key material. So no cause reaches the UI or the console.

Fail-closed holds: `init()` opens with `resetState()`, `rehydrateSession` swallows its own errors and `init` returns immediately after it, so there is no path where `init()` throws *after* reconstructing a session. `isLoggedIn()` is false → `useAuth`'s restore effect returns early → `authStore.signedIn` never fires.

`pnpm typecheck` **0** · `pnpm lint` **0** · `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` **0** · `pnpm test` **0** (apps/web 31 files / 268 tests, packages/client 401, apps/api 179). Rust gates N/A — diff is TypeScript only.

### New tests — `apps/web/src/auth/CoreKitProvider.test.tsx`

Mounts the provider over a **real** `createCoreKitSession` (SDK class faked to reproduce its unguarded store read on both restore and login paths), so the store under test is the real one.

1. discards a store the restore could not read, and does not pass it off as a signed-out tab
2. leaves a login after a failed restore able to establish a session
3. keeps a readable store when the restore failed for some other reason
4. keeps a store the restore read cleanly, session in it or not

<result>Done. PR #1173 is still a **draft** at `ea1a5dbc25ad60e4ded4ebf680a1611c4f486214`; all 3 threads replied to and resolved.

## Per-finding verdicts

**1. `net/retire.rs:189` — CRITICAL, subset-coverage replacement. ACCEPTED, real, data loss.**
Held `{L1}` + incoming `{L2}` → stored `{L2}`, and `L1` was then retired while a retained version still named it. Confirmed against the crate's own ranking (`orphaned_head`: unpinning a live-named block is loss, a stale row is only a leak).

The key thing CodeRabbit's suggestion missed: a union **cannot be re-derived at the ledger**. `owed_bytes = manifest − w(retained)`, and the ledger has no per-CID weights, so it cannot compute `w(A ∪ B)`. Storing the union while keeping either figure would have made `retire_owed`'s `retirable.pinned_bytes != entry.owed_bytes` gate fail forever — trading the loss for a permanently undrainable debt. So three changes landed together: union of retained; `owed_bytes = min(held, incoming)` redocumented as a **ceiling**; gate relaxed `!=` → `&gt;`.

That ceiling is loose by exactly the shared weight on a merged entry, and my security pass found a working data-loss exploit through the slack (bend one retained CID in the store → recomputed figure reproduces the ceiling exactly → gate passes → the displaced leaf is retired). I reproduced it, then closed it by requiring `entry.retained ⊆ expansion.cids()` — an invariant honest entries hold by construction. Conformance kit moved to the union rule with the disjoint case, so the kit can't enforce the bug.

**2. `sync/drain.rs:1736` — MAJOR, debt lost if `owe` fails after publish. MECHANISM CONFIRMED, fix deferred to a filed issue (#1184).**
Verified end to end: `publish_node` → `mark_published` on the record ack → `queued_ops` drops any op at/below the mark → replay reloads the *published* short history so `plan_prune` finds nothing. Result is a leak, on the correct side of the crate's ranking. Both orderings reachable without a new protocol are worse or equal (`owe` before publish = loss on a failed publish; `owe` before `mark_published` = no improvement). A real fix needs a pre-journalled intent **plus** a reconciliation sweep carrying the originating node — neither exists, and an intent without reconciliation is a staging leak stacked on the pin leak. Filed as **issue #1184** with the full trace.

**3. `sync/drain.rs:1793` — MINOR, aliasing between doomed versions. ACCEPTED, but the sketch was NOT applied literally — it introduces a permanent leak.**
`protected.extend(retirable.cids())` includes the entry's own root. A history may name one `contentCid` twice (the upstream guard only refuses doomed-vs-survivor overlap), so the repeat's whole expansion lands in `protected` → `owed_bytes: 0`, retained containing its own root → merged entry protects its expansion key → `root != entry.target` defers it forever. I reproduced that. `prune_debt` now *also* skips an already-charged root.

<result>Done. **PR #1185** (draft), branch `feat/received-share-store-production`, pushed sha `5921dfe64`.

## The decision: shape B — no tenth seam

The evidence was decisive, not balanced.

- `crates/engine/src/seams/mod.rs:5-10` already names this exact shape: `RetireLedger` is "the exception that proves the shape — the same trait contract, with a conformance kit of its own, but the engine ships the implementation over a seam every host already provides." `StagingRetireLedger` (`crates/engine/src/net/retire.rs:139`) is that object, and a received-shares store is the same one.
- A tenth seam breaks `SeamTypes`/`SeamSet` (`seams/mod.rs:93-139`), `WebSeamTypes` (`crates/wasm/src/host.rs:45,:114`), the JS seam bag, `FakeSeamTypes`, and the unlanded desktop set — for zero capability, since no host has a better durable store.

**Carrier: `StagingStore`, not `SnapshotCache`.** `SnapshotCache` is contractually a *cache* of what the network can re-serve, with `clear()` = "forget this device" (`snapshot_cache.rs:16-18`). Once the mailbox item that delivered a share is acked, nothing re-serves its `pointerReadKey` — so this is durable state, and it belongs where the op queue, op marks, and retire ledger live. `CredentialStore` is contractually a no-op on web, so it was out.

## What I built

New core structure `received-shares` (tag `0x0d`) with KAT vectors — sealing was unavoidable (a bookmark holds a secret) and reusing the `settings-record` seal would have made the two mutually transplantable. Then `StagingReceivedShareStore`, a conformance kit on the `RetireLedger` precedent, `load` on the trait, and the orphan-GC carve-out so a sweep can't collect every accepted share.

## The review gates earned their keep

Security and crypto independently found the same HIGH, and security traced it to a concrete host bug: `put_staged_bytes` never promised an atomic replace, and `packages/client/src/seams/stagingStore.ts:101-126` truncates to zero then *deletes the file* on failure. With one key, a quota-exceeded persist would have destroyed every bookmarked `pointerReadKey` — for shares whose mailbox items are already acked. I reworked it to two slots with a monotonic revision, so a persist never targets the readable copy. Also folded: release-active bounds (an unbounded `displayName` from a hostile contact permanently shrank the upload budget), a frozen at-rest byte vector, codec-tree zeroization, and a shared `entropy::fresh_ephemeral`.

I declined one `/simplify` suggestion — dropping the KAT check-coverage row as a "no-op" — since removing it would silently lose coverage if the sibling vectors change.

## Gates — all exit 0

`cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo check -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`, `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`. KAT generation is idempotent. Every new test proved red by reverting the production change and restored via edit (single-slot → 2 failures; fail-open load → 2; bounds + version gate in **release** → 3; field rename → frozen vector fails; ephemeral guard → 2).

## One thing to flag

**The store has a production implementor; a production `accept_share` call site does not exist.** That is blocked, not skipped: `Command::AcceptShare` (`facade.rs:498-503`) carries a sealed pointer with no `item_id`, so nothing could be acked (#1016's wiring), and `accept_share` needs a persisted `Contact`, which nothing stores (#1164). I added dependency edges rather than folding a mount whose design those issues own. I also filed **#1186** for the seam-atomicity defect, which equally threatens the retire ledger and op marks and belongs at the seam rather than in each caller.</result>

<result>## Verdict: DEFER — mechanism confirmed, window unreachable today, durable fix filed as #1187. No code change.

### Reasoning

The finding is real in shape but its stated premise is wrong, and the residual is currently unreachable.

**The TOCTOU is real.** `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:1409` reads the floor, `:1413` refuses, and the record is not authored/signed/published until `:1416-1458` — re-seal, head upload, and the whole `publish_record` fan-out, all `.await` points. `OwnerRotationNet::check_publishable` at `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:518` has the identical shape. The consequence is a brick, not staleness: `open_write_body` unseals *at* the floor (`:470-476`), `recover_write_scope_seed` binds the floor into the owner-write-blob AAD (`crates/engine/src/net/adopter.rs:416-445`), and floors never move backward (`crates/engine/src/gate/floor.rs:25-27`). Correctly an AGENTS.md rule 8 concern.

**Premise correction — not "another device".** Floors are local durable engine state behind `FloorStore` (`crates/engine/src/gate/floor.rs:5-6`). No remote device can write this device's floor; the racing writer would be a concurrent task in the same single-writer engine.

**Unreachable in the landed engine.** `floor::advance_write_epoch_on_sight` (floor law item 3, `crates/engine/src/gate/floor.rs:22-24`) has **zero production callers** — every call site is test code (`net/adopter.rs:829`, `net/rotation.rs:1889`, `facade.rs:5585`, `tests/adoption_gate.rs`). The only production write-floor raise is `floor::cold_seed_checked` at `crates/engine/src/sync/boot.rs:211`, once at cold start, strictly before any wave. `crates/engine/src/rotation/rotate_write.rs` never touches the floor store; the wave's interior raises only the read-epoch and sequence floors, which are distinctly keyed (`gate/floor.rs:114-122`). The window opens only when the polled `ConsultReason::FocusTick` consult (`crates/engine/src/sync/pointer.rs:51`) is wired to the advance.

**The literal suggestion is worse than the bug** — this is the "never apply verbatim" trap firing again. Durably pre-raising the floor to the wave's target epoch self-bricks the device on any publish failure: the floor cannot be lowered, so a device that raised past its own still-canonical root can no longer open that root's write body, and `WritePublishError::NotLanded` fires on pure availability failures. That converts a retryable network error into a permanent local write-plane lockout. The correct primitive is *exclusion* (hold the floor still across the publish so a concurrent consult defers), needing a lease capability `FloorStore` lacks plus facade discipline — the heavy lift CodeRabbit itself tagged.

**On the test ask:** an interposing `FloorStore` that raises on the guard's own read would, against today's code, assert the publish *succeeds* — pinning the hazard as expected behaviour. It is instead an acceptance criterion on #1187 so it lands red-then-green with the fix.

Also checked and rejected as a cheap tightening: the existing guard is already logically stronger than "at or below the floor". Since `write_floor &lt;= plane.write_epoch` (`:1413`) and `node.write_epoch &gt; plane.write_epoch` (`:1392`), `write_floor &lt; node.write_epoch` is strictly implied at check time. Only the temporal gap remains — nothing to tighten logically.

## Per-finding verdict

**Finding 1 — `received_share_store.rs:139`, slot selection: CONFIRMED and fixed** (not with CodeRabbit's diff).

I enumerated all nine slot states against the old guard `a.revision() &gt; b.revision()`. Exactly one was wrong — `(Unreadable, Empty)` → target `0`, destroying the unreadable slot while a free slot sat untouched. Every other state was already correct, including `(Unreadable, Held)` and `(Held, Unreadable)`, because a `Some` does compare against a `None`. The defect is confined to the single pair where both sides are `None` for different reasons.

CodeRabbit's proposed `(Slot::Empty, _) =&gt; 0` / `(_, Slot::Empty) =&gt; 1` arms are mechanically correct but leave the ordering as four arms a reader must re-derive. Since this layout is the reference pattern for #1164/#1165, I made the distinction a property of the type: a `TargetPreference` enum with derived `Ord`, ordered worst-target-first — `Held(Reverse&lt;u64&gt;)` &lt; `Unreadable` &lt; `Empty`. Selection is now one total comparison. `Reverse` makes the newer list the worse target, preserving `(Held, Held)` behaviour exactly. The safety argument is stated once at the enum definition, and the `SLOTS` doc cross-references it so the sibling stores inherit it.

**The revision half of the finding: refused, filed as #1189.** CodeRabbit is right that `newest` ignores an unreadable slot, and right that preserving that slot is what makes the inversion observable — my fix widens its reachability. But it is unfixable at this layer: the revision lives inside the sealed det-CBOR body, so a slot whose seal won't open has a genuinely unknowable revision. Refusing the persist instead is a non-starter — a single torn *first* write leaves `(Unreadable, Empty)`, which would brick the store permanently on an ordinary failure.

**Finding 2 — `staging.rs:25`, orphan GC: REFUSED, false positive.**

`is_bookkeeping` at `staging.rs:38-45` already includes `key.starts_with(RECEIVED_SHARES_PREFIX)`, and `orphan_staging_keys` filters candidates with `!is_bookkeeping(key)`, so `cbx/rs/…` keys are never collection candidates. The prefix carve-out is deliberately *broader* than `slot_keys()`: threading `slot_keys()` through GC as suggested would narrow the referenced set to the logged-in identity, so a sweep on a multi-account device would collect another identity's list — the exact loss the carve-out prevents. The existing test at `staging.rs:552` writes a key with a **foreign** owner tag `[7u8;32]` that `slot_keys()` could never produce, and would fail under the proposed change.

<result>Done. **PR #1190** (draft) — https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1190, sha `c34e44c35`, branch `fix/write-plane-history-link`.

## Design choice and evidence

Took **option 2** from #1179 (extend `ResealSeeds`, mint the link), not the empty-link option. Evidence: `blueprint/api.md` makes the field load-bearing — "interior old names are batch-retired at wave completion (a resumed wave enumerates them via the write-plane history link)" — so emptying it would delete a specified mechanism, and #764 (`Reclaim orphaned interior write-names`) is the slice that consumes it.

Shape is an **enum, not an extra field**, so the invariant is structural rather than a runtime guard:

```rust
pub enum WriteHistory&lt;'a&gt; { Carried(&amp;'a [u8]), Cut(PrevEpochSeed&lt;'a&gt;) }
```

`CommittedSet.write_history_link` is removed entirely, so a cut has **no channel** to carry through — unrepresentable rather than checked. `Carried` preserves byte-for-byte behaviour on the paths that legitimately carry (sweep, cascade, read rotation, grant create — verified each was a real carry, not `&amp;[]`).

Sealing mirrors the read plane: `structure_key(fresh_write_scope_seed, history-link)`, AAD at the new write epoch, payload = the retiring seed/epoch. `blueprint/core.md`'s catalog row is "structure-key | nodeSeed **or scope seed**, structTag", so this is a *use* of a frozen edge, not a new one.

## What changed

- `reseal.rs`: `WriteHistory` on `ResealSeeds`; `mint_history_link` extracted and shared by both planes; `ResealError::HistoryLinkNotDescending` (release-active `Err`, never `debug_assert!`) refuses a cut that doesn't advance the write epoch.
- `rotate_write.rs`: `WriteSubtreeResolver::recover_wave` + `ResumedWriteWave{seed, root_name, write_epoch}` replace the faked `RotateScopeWritePlan::resume_write_scope_seed` (deleted). Two release-active refusals: `ResumedWaveAtAnotherEpoch`, `ResumedSeedNotAtItsRoot`.
- `net/rotation.rs`: production `recover_wave` — scope pointer → owner-verified re-point → `prevRoot` must be this rotation's root → gated moved root → owner-write blob opened at the owner-signed epoch. Deliberately does **not** raise the write-epoch floor (that would make the pre-wave root unreadable for the resume). `RootPlane` carries the retiring seed for the `Cut`.
- `net/adopter.rs`: extracted `open_write_scope_seed_at` so the owner-write-blob open exists once.

# Your question: is the two-slot layout now redundant?

**Yes — collapse it, and #1189 goes moot.** Evidence, point by point.

**1. Does the contract cover what two slots were protecting against?** Yes, for both shipping hosts. The hazard `SLOTS` names (`received_share_store.rs:35-41`) is "a host that truncates before it writes can be interrupted and leave a partial blob". That host was the browser one, and it no longer exists: the target is only ever touched by `move()`, a single atomic directory operation, so there is no window in which the key holds a partial blob. Desktop was already `atomic_write` (`fs_util.rs:48-53`: "A crash can only ever leave the old value or the new value — never a torn one"). Both are now pinned by `check_failed_put` against the real backing.

Two caveats, neither of which two slots fixes:
- The kit arms an **error return**, not a power cut. Process-kill atomicity rests on the rename in both hosts — structural, and asserted separately (desktop `fs_util` tests; the browser's `move()` is atomic by spec). A torn *target* is not reachable in either design.
- A **future** host could skip the kit case (the opt-in gap above). That is an argument for closing that gap, not for a per-caller workaround — and note two slots never protected against a host that fails to meet the contract in other ways either.

**2. What does two-slot still buy?** Only the `Slot::Unreadable` fallback at `received_share_store.rs:186-191` — and I agree with your read that it is a liability. With torn writes gone, a slot is unreadable only if (a) a newer build wrote a `v` this build rejects (`core/src/seal/received_shares.rs:110-112`), (b) it is another identity's bytes, or (c) host storage corrupted it. In case (a) the unreadable slot is *the newer one*, so `[Slot::Held(held), _] | [_, Slot::Held(held)]` serves the older list and silently drops what the newer session bookmarked — #1189 exactly. A single slot returns the `"the stored list did not open"` error the same file already reaches for when both slots are bad (`:159-161`, `:191-193`), which is the fail-closed behaviour the design itself prefers everywhere else. The "terminal" fear in the `SLOTS` comment — an unopenable list whose mailbox items are acked and gone — is real but unchanged by two slots in cases (a)/(c): a version bump makes *both* slots unreadable to the old build, and corruption is not selective by design.

**3. Anything else?** I checked and found nothing.
- *Concurrent writers / cross-tab:* no protection. Two writers both read revision N and write N+1 into different slots — one list is lost just as with one key. Safety comes from tab leadership and the single-writer engine, not the layout.
- *Partial reads:* `staged_bytes` is verbatim-or-error on both hosts (the browser rejects a short read rather than zero-padding).
- *Orphan GC:* the carve-out is by prefix (`sync/staging.rs:44` `key.starts_with(RECEIVED_SHARES_PREFIX)`), so key count is irrelevant.
- *Rollback:* the local staging store is trusted; there is no adversary the revision defends against here.
- Cost side: every `load` and every `persist` does two `staged_bytes` reads (`:93-99`).

**4. Migration.** Greenfield, no deployed data, and the blast radius is one file. `StoredList.revision` lives in the **engine** codec (`grants/accept.rs:220-229, 317, 343-348`), not in core; the received-shares KAT vectors cover the core seal envelope and its `v` gate, not the engine body. So collapsing means: `slot_keys()` → one key, delete `SLOTS`, `Slot::target_preference`, `TargetPreference`, and the two-slot arms of `persist`/`load` (~100 lines). The seam kit `conformance/received_share_store.rs` is trait-level and pins nothing about slots, so it is untouched. **Keep `revision` in the sealed body** — dropping it would turn an engine-only simplification into a codec change with fixture churn, and it stays useful as a monotonic write counter. Recommendation: collapse in `received_share_store.rs` only, close #1189 as moot, and tell #1164/#1165 to write a single key.</result>

<result>Done. Both findings resolved on PR #1194, pushed as `a8550a8c3`, PR left **draft**.

## Finding 1 (Major) — fresh-backing failed put — accepted, fixed in this PR

**Decision: a sibling entry point, not an extension of `check_failed_put`.** The two cases cannot share one backing: the fresh case needs the lever armed *before* the key's first put, while `check_failed_put` needs an *unarmed* put to establish the bytes it then defends — and the kit's lever contract explicitly allows a host's lever to stay armed (no disarm hook exists). Folding them into one function would have forced either a new disarm burden on every host or losing the Windows leg entirely.

- `crates/engine/src/testkit/conformance/staging_store.rs:200` — new `check_failed_first_put`: asserts empty backing, arms, requires `Err`, then through a **reopened** handle asserts `staged_bytes == None`, `staged_keys` excludes the key, `staged_bytes_total == 0`.
- All four existing call sites wired (no new opt-in gap): `crates/engine/tests/staging_atomic_put.rs:32` (fake), `crates/desktop-seams/tests/conformance.rs:66` (file store), `crates/wasm/src/conformance.rs:101` → `packages/client/test/browser/conformance.worker.ts:267` + `conformance.spec.ts:19` (OPFS, both short-write and throwing-write levers, staged-dir entry count 0).
- `crates/engine/src/testkit/fakes/staging_store.rs:82,165` — new `strand_staged_write_after` injector (lands half the bytes, then fails) + `crates/engine/tests/staging_atomic_put.rs:41` permanent `should_panic` negative control.
- **The desktop leg is `#[cfg(unix)]`**, documented at the test: the lever is a permission denial on `staged/`, and Windows honours no denial on a path that does not exist yet (the existing Windows lever marks the *sidecar* read-only, which requires it to already exist). The Windows CI leg keeps running the replacement case. The store's put path is identical code on all platforms; only the fault lever is platform-specific.

## Finding 2 (Minor) — `WriteDenial::drop` — accepted, mechanism confirmed first

`crates/desktop-seams/tests/conformance.rs:107,116`. A throwaway probe proved the abort: dropping a `WriteDenial` whose path was removed while a real panic unwound gave `panic in a destructor during cleanup` / SIGABRT with the real message lost. `set_denied` now returns `io::Result&lt;()&gt;`; `Drop` discards it. I deliberately did **not** apply the suggestion literally to `arm` — `arm` keeps `.expect(...)` because it runs outside any unwind, and a silently unarmed lever would turn a real violation into the misleading "an armed put must report the failure rather than succeed".

## Red-proofs (all four done, production code restored each time)

1. Removed the new `staged_bytes == None` assertion → the fake negative control failed (`panic did not contain expected string`). Restored.
2. `FileStagingStore::put_staged_bytes` made to swallow the error → both desktop failed-put tests red on "an armed put must report the failure rather than succeed". Restored.

## Changes

**Change 1 — hoist the descending guard** (`crates/engine/src/rotation/reseal.rs`)
- `reseal.rs:374-379` — new pre-seal guard `if let WriteHistory::Cut(prev) = &amp;seeds.write_history &amp;&amp; prev.epoch &gt;= seeds.write_epoch { return Err(HistoryLinkNotDescending) }`, placed immediately after the codec-bound guards and before `enforce_committed_ledger`, so it sits inside the same release-active pre-seal block as every sibling.
- `reseal.rs:549-559` — the `WriteHistory::Cut` arm is now a bare `mint_history_link(...)?`; link-generation behaviour is byte-identical.
- `reseal.rs:662-670` — `UndrawnEntropy` (panic-on-draw seam) hoisted from inside `a_committed_ledger_past_the_codec_bound_fails_closed_before_any_seal` to test-module scope so both guards share one probe.
- `reseal.rs:1800` — test renamed `a_write_cut_that_does_not_advance_the_write_epoch_is_refused_before_any_seal`, now drives `UndrawnEntropy` and builds an **interior** scope root (`Some(&amp;fx.parent_node_seed)`) so the ascent-link seal is also inside the pinned prefix.

**Change 2 — exhaustive terminal classification** (`crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:4325-4362`)
Two variants were missing from the hand-maintained list, not one: `HistoryLinkNotDescending` **and** `TagNotBoundToRecipient`. Replaced the array-of-terminals + separate entropy assertion with a single loop over one sample per variant, whose expected verdict comes from a `match` written independently of `reseal_verdict`.

## Did I make it compiler-enforced? Partly — and here is exactly how far it goes

Yes for classification: the test's own `match &amp;sample` is exhaustive, so adding a `ResealError` variant now fails to compile in the test as well as in production `reseal_verdict`. I proved this: after adding a probe variant and fixing only the production match, the build still failed with `E0004: non-exhaustive patterns: &amp;reseal::ResealError::ProbeVariant not covered --&gt; crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:4344`.

No for sample coverage: nothing in Rust forces the sample array itself to contain every variant without a derive (`strum::EnumIter`) — I did not add a dependency for one test. The residual hole is narrow: an author adding a variant is stopped by the compiler and must write a classification arm, at which point omitting the sample is a visible choice rather than the silent omission this nitpick found.

## Red-proofs (both done, both observed red, both restored)

1. Reverted the guard back into the `Cut` match arm → `a_write_cut_that_does_not_advance_the_write_epoch_is_refused_before_any_seal` FAILED with `panicked at reseal.rs:666: the guard must reject before any seal draws entropy` — i.e. it panicked on the first grant-blob HPKE draw, proving seals happened before the refusal under the old placement.
2. Re-classified `HistoryLinkNotDescending` to `NotLanded` → `a_reseal_seam_failure_stays_retryable_and_a_trust_failure_does_not` FAILED with `left: NotLanded, right: Rejected`, message `history-link-not-descending: deterministic on inputs the wave already gated`.

Both files restored from backups and re-verified green (`806 passed`).

## What shipped

**Commit 1 — `0c01b4ddc` `refactor: collapse the received-shares store onto a single staging key`** (Closes #1189)
One key holds the whole list. `Slot`, `TargetPreference`, `slot_keys`, `read_slots` and `StoredList.revision` are gone; the body is `encode_stored_list`/`decode_stored_list` over `{shares, v}` and the frozen at-rest byte vector is re-frozen. Also folded from review: `op_mark_key` → `owner_scoped_key` (it already implemented the prefix+owner-tag shape all three stores hand-rolled), key derived once at construction, and `TooLong`/`within`/`reject_unknown` shared instead of recopied.

**Commit 2 — `5fd933bfd` `feat: persist imported contacts…`** (Closes #1164)
`StagingContactStore` over `StagingStore` at the grants layer, no tenth seam. It stores the self-authenticating contact **code** and re-imports on every load, so host bytes never construct a `Contact`. Only the canonical re-encoding is stored (the offered spelling tolerates unknown fields, so storing it verbatim was an attacker-chosen byte channel). `Full` + `forget` replace the 1024-contact brick; a tampered book now surfaces as `TrustViolation`, not a retryable `Seam`.

### Tests removed in the collapse
Five slot-arbitration tests: `a_torn_slot_loses_to_the_readable_one`, `the_higher_revision_wins_a_load`, `a_persist_takes_the_free_slot_over_the_unreadable_one`, `a_persist_overwrites_the_unreadable_slot_before_a_readable_one`, `a_persist_refuses_when_neither_slot_is_readable` — all assert arbitration between two slots that no longer exist. `a_lost_write_never_destroys_the_readable_list` was reworked onto the seam's atomicity; the fail-closed load is now covered in both arms (does not open / opens but does not decode).

## The one thing you need to decide: #1165 is not closed

The review gates found that landing the invite store **as designed is a forgery**, so I held it back rather than ship a latent brick. `RecordedInvite.ephemeral_identity_pk` is bound by nothing — `convert_invite_claim`'s tag re-derivation covers only `ephemeral_enc_pk`. Making `RecordedInvite` host-supplied lets anyone who can write host storage pair a real grantee's public `enc_pk` (which re-derives to that grantee's committed tag) with an identity key they hold, and convert a claim into an owner-signed grant at that grantee's permission — up to `Write`, with no owner key material.

I checked the reviewer's cheap fix (embed the ephemeral half as a contact code) and it does **not** work: a contact code proves "identity A asserts enc E", which an attacker signs for any E. The authority must be the owner's, so the state has to be sealed HPKE-to-self — a new frozen structure tag plus KAT vectors, which is substantially bigger than #1165 describes and deserves its own review. The full store, kit, `mint_recorded_invite` fragment gate, and integration coverage are written and preserved at `scratchpad/invite-store-followup/`; the mechanism and required scope are written up as a comment on #1165, which stays open.

Two smaller things I deliberately did not do, both noted rather than silently dropped: replacing `is_bookkeeping`'s prefix list with a `cbx/`+`cipherbox/` namespace rule (correct, but changing the orphan sweep's matching rule is a data-loss-risk change deserving its own review), and stale `cbx/rs/&lt;tag&gt;{a,b}` keys from pre-collapse dev builds staying uncollected (greenfield; dies at the #653 cutover).

PR is draft; I did not run `gh pr ready`.</result>

<result>**PR [#1200](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1200)** — draft, base `main`, sha `9ddd1a7085f4ec2bf3aaf2da129843787a2dcf7f` (rebased onto `d10a97f6f`; `blueprint/engine.md` conflicted with #1198's citation restyle and was resolved by hand). Two commits: `cc8a06790` (the rebuild) and `9ddd1a708` (review findings).

## What changed, per scope item

**1. Re-targeted at interior nodes.** `sweep_pass` gates the scope root, walks its read body stopping at scope-root boundaries, and re-seals nodes whose envelope epoch lags the scope's. `SweepTarget` is gone — the pure pass now carries **no key material at all** (re-sealing moved behind the publisher seam). New `reseal::seed_at_epoch` walks the history-link ratchet backward to the epoch a lagging record was sealed at, sharing one `ratchet_step` with `walkable_chain_start`.

**2. Concrete resolver + publisher** on `OwnerRotationNet` over `RecordTransport` + `ApiClient`, reusing `gated_root`. Re-seal is `author_child_envelope` + `preflight` + `publish_record`.

**3. Superseded verdict.** `gated_scope_root` now returns a three-way `RootGateVerdict`; `EpochBelowFloor` becomes `Superseded`, routed through the scope-pointer consult, re-resolved at `currentRootName`, fail-closed if still below the floor. `OwnerRotationNet` gained `owner_pointer_seed` + `payload_version` (only constructed in its own tests today).

**4. Self-heal decoupled** — runs on the walk result before any epoch comparison is acted on, and repairs the full #38 D6 invariant (canonical *and* naming every scope root the walk saw).

**5. Convergence gate fixed.** New `converge_subtree` entry point; grant creation converges the granted folder's subtree (not the whole parent scope), and refuses on `dropped_lost_race` ∪ `unreachable`. Step 5b's descendant re-key moved from the sweep resolver to `CascadeResealResolver`, which is where that scope-root material belongs.

**Equivalence check** (inherited from #1014): `rotation/sweep/sim.rs` holds the simulation network and a `SCENARIOS` set; `the_sweep_simulation_and_the_production_seams_agree` runs the one `sweep_pass` over both and asserts identical outcomes. It blocks merge in the existing **Engine Tests** gate.

## Two real defects the reviews caught

- **Critical (security).** A child carrying a grant section was proved a scope root by its owner-signed commitment and node id alone. A vault root carries no ascent link, so the gate's ascent stage never runs on it — a committed writer could plant the owner's own vault root as a `ChildRef` and have the self-heal repair it into the swept scope's committed index, where a later cascade would re-key it under that scope's seed. Full vault read compromise from a write grant. **This was introduced by my diff** (the old self-heal only canonicalised). Fixed: the ascent link is now required, release-active.
- **High (crypto + security, found independently by both).** Nothing on the sweep's read path adopts an interior record, so no gate raised the name's sequence floor; `publish_record` mints `floor + 1`, so on any node this device hadn't browsed the sweep published at sequence 1 against a network at N and could never converge. Fixed: carry the observed sequence as `min_current_sequence`, and advance the floor on the confirmed unseal as `net/child.rs` does.

Also folded in: `unreachable` isolation (a node past the retained ratchet window, or claiming an epoch above the root's — a value a committed writer picks freely — no longer aborts the whole scope); C2 label binding; scope-keyed parked read + explicit scope parameter on both seams; both the seam invariant *and* the durable floor mirror in `publish_node`; and dedup of the frontier rule, ratchet step and sequence-floor strictness with the code that already owned them (`floor::check_sequence` + `Strictness::AtOrAboveFloor`).

## Red-proof — every new test reverted to red, then restored

18 mutations, each isolated, each naming the guarding test. Highlights: dropping the ascent-link requirement → `the_vault_root_planted_as_a_child_scope_root_is_refused`; removing the CAS basis + floor advance → `a_swept_node_lands_above_the_sequence_the_network_already_holds` + `an_interior_unseal_advances_the_names_sequence_floor`; vacuous epoch predicate → 17 tests; no boundary stop → `an_indexed_scope_root_is_not_resolved_as_an_interior_node`; no superseded route → 3 tests; self-heal gated on a re-seal → 5 tests; unkeyed park → `a_node_read_under_a_scope_this_net_never_gated_is_refused`; `converge_subtree` ignoring its start node → 2 tests.

Two honest calls: the C2-boundary test alone did *not* go red when I removed the boundary check (the grant-section marker is a second line of defence) — `an_indexed_scope_root_is_not_resolved_as_an_interior_node` is the test that actually pins it. And the CAS basis and the floor advance overlap: each alone closes the bug, so only removing both goes red; they're kept as belt-and-braces matching `child.rs` + `revival.rs`.

## Gate exit codes

`cargo fmt --all -- --check` = 0 · `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` = 0 · `cargo test --workspace` = 0 (39 suites) · `cargo check -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` = 0. **No TypeScript in the diff**, so `pnpm typecheck/lint/test` don't apply; I ran `lint:tracker-refs` anyway (clean, 682 files) plus prettier and markdownlint on the blueprint.

## Residuals I did not fix — worth filing

1. **The interior read skips the read-epoch floor by design.** The stage it skips carried *authorship*: a party holding an old epoch's seed and the node's write key can author a body the sweep promotes to the current epoch. That is `CONTEXT.md`'s documented "Forgery window", closed by `rotateScopeWrite` — but the sweep extends its lifetime past the wave. It's inherent to the lazy wave the blueprint mandates, so I documented it at the skip rather than blocking the PR.
2. **`record_scope_boundary` in `WriteWaveNet` has the same missing ascent-link check** — untouched code, and it only reads an owner-authored index, but it's the same shape as the critical finding.
3. **A `Rejected`/`Unavailable` node still aborts the whole scope's pass** — one revoked writer holding one node's write key blocks the lazy wave for every other node. Inherited from the eager-set walk's posture; converging the clean nodes first would be strictly safer.
4. **`ScopeRootPublishError` now serves interior-node publishes**, so its docs and `Display` say "scope root" at half its call sites. A rename is blocked by the existing `RecordPublishError` in `net/record_publish.rs`.

**ADR 0003's `Status:` line** still says `Proposed`. That's a follow-up commit on `FSM1/cipher-box-next` after this merges — cross-repo, not attempted here.

## The headline

**#1096 cannot be delivered as written.** The pin store is not what a write hits first. Nothing in production provisions a first-run vault, so no write in this suite reaches any endpoint at all — a Kubo service and write specs would both have been dead weight.

Evidence, driven against a real stack (API on a fresh DB with `KUBO_API_URL` set, real Kubo, someguy, mock `/routing/v1`), through the shipped e2e bundle:

- Cold start settles clean, staleness `fresh`.
- `[+ NEW FOLDER]` is accepted, row renders, `pending: "metadata"`.
- 100 s later the snapshot is byte-identical, staleness decayed to `stale`, and the tab issued **zero** requests since `/auth/login` — no `POST /content/upload`, no `POST /registry/register`, no `PUT /routing/v1/ipns/*`. The only two requests ever sent are the vault-pointer `GET`s that 404.

The chain:
1. `crates/engine/src/sync/pointer.rs:161` `seal_repoint` has no caller outside tests — `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:4529` and `:5039` are inside the `#[cfg(test)]` module opening at `:3424`. Nothing ever authors a vault pointer.
2. Empty chain → cold start takes `crates/engine/src/sync/boot.rs:191-207`, yielding `write_scope_seed: None`, no root name.
3. Both `deposit_write_seed` sites (`facade.rs:1796`, `:2132`) are fed from a resolved pointer, so neither fires.
4. `facade.rs:2016-2018` — `spawn_resolve_tick_loop` returns without spawning when the root name is `None`, and the drain is constructed inside that loop (`facade.rs:2183`).

The seed is not owner-re-derivable by design (`facade.rs:2096-2098`). Every write suite plants the pointer by hand (`crates/engine/tests/write_plane.rs:495`, `crates/fuse/tests/fuse_op_core.rs:1324`), which is why no existing gate catches this.

## What shipped

**PR #1206 (draft), sha `f848be0c0`** — `tests/web-e2e/README.md` only.
- Deleted the "No affordance to drive" bullet (false since wave 4).
- Kept the pin-store bullet, demoted to second, with "once ops do drain".
- Added the provisioning blocker ahead of it, with checkable call sites.

**Issue #1205** filed for the provisioning gap; `#1096 blocked_by #1205` set via the native dependency API. Comment left on #1096 with the finding.

## What I deliberately did not ship, and why

- **The Kubo service.** Written and correct (`ipfs/kubo:v0.42.0`, `KUBO_API_URL: http://localhost:5001`, plus `VITE_READ_ACCELERATOR_URL: http://127.0.0.1:8080` — note `127.0.0.1`, since Kubo redirects a path-gateway request with Host `localhost` to its subdomain gateway). Reverted: nothing in the suite exercises it until ops drain, and an unused service on a merge-blocking job is cost with no gate.
- **The five specs.** Written, page objects extended against shipped testids only (`new-folder-button`, `create-folder-*`, `rename-*`, `move-*`, `delete-*`, `file-list-item`, plus the `actions for &lt;name&gt;` / `select &lt;name&gt;` / `Choose files to upload` accessible names — **no testid added to `apps/web`**). All five fail at the settle after the mutation. Reverted; a red spec is not a gate.

## Root cause — confirmed, your diagnosis was correct

I verified it end to end rather than assuming it:

- `crates/engine/src/grants/create.rs:291` now calls `converge_subtree(...)` instead of a whole-scope sweep.
- `converge_subtree` (`crates/engine/src/rotation/sweep.rs:462`) delegates to `walk_and_converge(..., Some(node))`, and the frontier seed is `match from { Some(node) =&gt; vec![node.clone()], None =&gt; swept.children }`. So the granted node itself lands on the frontier and gets `resolve_child`'d — under the old whole-scope path the frontier was `swept.children`, which the fake returned empty, so `resolve_child` was never called.
- The walk loop treats any non-`Unreadable` resolve error as a hard `SweepError::Node`, which is exactly the `Converge(Node { node_id: [92 x16], reason: Rejected })` panic. `0x5c == 92` is the `GranteeScopePlan.scope_id`.

Production code is correct; the test fake was stale. I changed no production code.

## The change

Test-only, `crates/contract/tests/contract.rs`:

- `LocalNet::resolve_child` returned an unconditional `Err(SweepResolveFailure::Rejected)`. It now returns `SweptChild::Interior(SweptNode { ... })` at read epoch 3 with an empty folder body. `node.current_read_epoch &gt;= swept.current_read_epoch` puts it in `already_converged`, and `body_children` on an empty folder yields nothing, so the walk terminates immediately.
- Extracted `const LOCAL_NET_READ_EPOCH: u64 = 3` so `resolve_scope` and `resolve_child` cannot silently drift apart — that divergence is the exact failure class being fixed.
- Corrected the stale `LocalNet` doc comment ("The swept parent scope is empty, so the convergence gate passes with nothing to advance") to describe the current behavior in two lines.
- Added `ReadBody` and `SweptNode` imports.

## Finding 1 — contact book plaintext at rest (`crates/engine/src/grants/contact_store.rs:16-25`)

**Mechanism real, but the ask was tracking, not code.** The disclosure is genuine: the received-shares store seals HPKE-to-self before the host sees it (`received_share_store.rs:70`), while the contact book writes det-CBOR in the clear under the same seam, exposing the owner's whole contact graph to a local reader. It is a *new* disclosure — the grant ledger that would otherwise carry those identity keys is sealed under the scope write key.

CodeRabbit asked only that this be tracked rather than living in a doc comment. I filed **https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/issues/1207**, attached as a sub-issue of #635 (same parent as #1164 and #1165, so it can't orphan), labels `v2-build`/`comp:engine`/`comp:core`, milestone v2.0.0. The issue folds in the three further residuals sealing closes that the per-entry binding verify can't reach (third-party insertion, subkey rollback, entry deletion), and notes #1165's invite-record store is blocked on the *same* missing structure tag — I cross-commented on #1165 so one tag lands both consumers.

I deliberately did **not** put the issue number in the module doc: AGENTS.md forbids tracker references in code and `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` enforces it. The header already states the checkable condition (closing it needs a frozen structure tag), which is the form the rule requires.

## Finding 2 — "no read path for previously persisted received shares" — **FALSE POSITIVE**

Both halves (legacy slot-key fallback, and bumping `STORED_LIST_V` with a compat decode arm) assume a population that does not exist.

- The two-slot layout and revision-bearing body were introduced by `2d54ddc7f` (#1185), merged to `main` **today at 17:30**. `git tag --contains 2d54ddc7f` is empty; newest tag `v0.9.2` is from February and predates the v2 engine.
- v2 has never been deployed. `blueprint/deploy.md` gates staging behind a single scheduled destructive cutover that hasn't happened, and states the clean break (FSM1/cipher-box-next#6) "removes all data-continuity obligations… cutover is a redeploy, not a migration."

The only tree that can hold such bytes is a dev checkout straddling two same-day merges, and that already fails closed: stale slot keys read as empty, and a `revision`-bearing body hits `reject_unknown(map, &amp;["shares","v"])` and surfaces as a decode error — never a silent empty list. Adding the fallback would be dead code; CodeRabbit's alternative ("state that in both docs") is exactly the absence-justifying comment AGENTS.md rules out by name. I also verified the collapse is internally complete — no residual `slot_keys`/`TargetPreference`/`read_slots`/`StoredList`/`revision` references anywhere in `crates`, `packages`, or `apps`.

## Verdicts

**1. `drain.rs` 98-114 — applied.** Real. `owner_scoped_key` (`crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs:116`) appends the tag directly; `StagingRetireLedger::scope` (`crates/engine/src/net/retire.rs:153-160`) writes `prefix || len || owner_tag`. The "shape every per-identity durable record takes" claim was false. Narrowed the doc to the direct layout with a cross-reference to the ledger. Left the `owner_tag` doc below alone — it is accurate.

**2. `contact_store.rs` 220-223 — applied.** Real double decode + double secp256k1 verify per code, paid once per entry on every book load. Rather than adding a raw field constructor (which would break `Contact`'s "proof the binding verified" invariant), added `impl From&lt;&amp;ContactCode&gt; for Contact` — a `ContactCode` is itself only obtainable from a passing verify, so the invariant holds by the same argument. Both `import_contact` and `import_recorded` now route through it.

**3. `contact_store.rs` 246-251 — declined.** The cost is real but a per-instance memo buys nothing. `StagingContactStore` has exactly one production construction, `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:2348`, built inline for a single `record` and dropped — the cache would be populated and discarded inside one command. `resolve_recipient` has no production caller yet (the grant-mint path hasn't landed). Nitpick 2 already halves the cost. Not filed as a follow-up either: the useful version is a session-scoped store, a different change, and there is no caller to motivate it.

**4. `contact.rs` 44-51 — applied.** Real. `import_contact` does no length check; enforcement is `facade.rs:2342` and `within("contactCode", …)` at `contact_store.rs:319`/`:354`. Reworded as a positive obligation on callers, not as prose about what `import_contact` omits.

**5. Conformance test 118-129 — applied, but CodeRabbit's mechanism is wrong.** I probed it: canonical key order puts `identityPk` last, and for the fixed input `code(0x23, 0x23)` the flipped byte still yields a valid curve point, so the old test *did* reach the verify (`class=trust`, `check=subkey-binding-invalid`) deterministically. The actual defect is that reaching the verify was incidental to byte layout and `Err(Import(_))` accepts a decode failure equally — nothing pinned the property. Replaced with a `substituted_subkey` helper (same-length X25519 substitution, so only the verify can reject) and asserted `check() == "subkey-binding-invalid"`. **Proved it goes red**: stubbing `crates/core/src/suite/contact.rs:142` to `if false &amp;&amp; !verify_subkey_binding(…)` fails the kit at the new panic arm; restored before committing, and that file is not in the diff.

**6. `tests/facade.rs` 213-214 — applied.** Real latent coupling. `crates/engine/tests/write_plane.rs:62` already uses `const SECRET: [u8; 32]`; adopted the same in `facade.rs`, with `secret()` built from it and both sites using `kdf::enc_subkey(&amp;SECRET)`.

<result>## Bottom line

#1205 is real and is the only place it is tracked. The gap is not one missing call — it is **one missing orchestrator plus two genuinely missing primitives**. Everything else already exists in production code, most of it wired and load-bearing for other paths.

---

## 1. What provisioning must do — ordered, with exists/missing

The exact recipe is already written down, twice, in test fixtures: `crates/engine/tests/write_plane.rs:463-527` (`seed_account`) and `crates/fuse/tests/fuse_op_core.rs:1324-1347`. Those are the specification. Steps:

| # | Step | Primitive | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Draw a random **read scope seed** (32B) from the entropy seam | `crate::entropy::Entropy::fill`, pattern at `crates/engine/src/rotation/rotate_write.rs:544-550` | **exists, wired** |
| 2 | Draw a random **write scope seed** (32B) | same | **exists, wired** |
| 3 | Derive the owner's **writer pseudonym** for the root scope | `kdf::pseudonym_sign` (`crates/core/src/kdf/mod.rs:294`); owner-side wrapper `SessionIdentity::writer_pseudonym_signer` (`crates/engine/src/session.rs:159`) | **exists, unwired** — and its *input* is missing, see §2 |
| 4 | Build the **grant-set commitment** (empty entries, `ipnsName` = the root's derived name, `ownerPseudonymPk`) and owner-sign it | `GrantSetCommitment` (`crates/core/src/seal/grant.rs:741`), `sign_grant_set` | **exists**; only test callers in engine (`grants/invite.rs:784`, `net/rotation.rs:1326` is prod but inside the unwired rotation arm) |
| 5 | Derive the root's `ipnsName` = `ipns_keypair(write_seed(writeScopeSeed, rootId))` | `derive_write_name` (`crates/engine/src/rotation/`, used at `sync/drain.rs:939`) | **exists, wired** |
| 6 | Author the **root grant section**: owner blob (wraps read scope seed), owner-write-blob (wraps write scope seed, at the write epoch), no ascent link (vault root), no history links, empty grant blobs, sealed write body | `reseal_scope_root` (`crates/engine/src/rotation/reseal.rs:347`) — takes `parent_node_seed: None`, `prev: None`, `write_history: WriteHistory::Carried(b"")` (`reseal.rs:551`), exactly the vault-root shape | **exists, production, but no production caller** — its only caller is `rotation/rotate.rs` / `grants/create.rs`, both themselves unwired |
| 7 | Author the root **envelope**: empty folder read-body + the grant section, at read epoch 1 | `author_scope_root_with_section` (`crates/engine/src/net/author.rs:204`) | **exists, wired** (used by `net/rotation.rs:657`) |
| 8 | Upload head block (`POST /content/upload`), register-first (`POST /registry/register`), CAS PUT the record at sequence 1 | `publish_record` (`crates/engine/src/net/record_publish.rs:183`) → `publish` (`net/publish.rs:217`); first publish embeds sequence 1 automatically (`net/publish.rs:286`) | **exists, wired** |
| 9 | Build the `RepointObject` (`scope_id`, `current_root` = the name from step 5, `write_epoch`, `min_read_epoch`, `prev_root: None`) | `RepointObject` + `build_repoint_object` (`rotation/rotate_write.rs:596`) | **exists**, prod caller is rotation only |
| 10 | **Seal** the pointer block, owner-signed | `seal_repoint` (`crates/engine/src/sync/pointer.rs:161`) | **exists, unwired** — confirmed: only prod caller is `rotate_write.rs:605`; all others are `#[cfg(test)]` or `tests/` |
| 11 | **Publish** the pointer block as the raw IPNS value at `vault_pointer_name(loginSecret, 0)`, signed by `kdf::vault_pointer_index(loginSecret, 0)` (`crates/core/src/kdf/mod.rs:317`) | `publish_inline` + `InlineRecordRequest` (`net/publish.rs:179`), pattern at `net/rotation.rs:1493-1529` (`publish_scope_pointer`) | primitives **exist, wired**; the **vault-pointer channel has no production publish path at all** — `publish_scope_pointer` publishes the *scope* pointer (`kdf::scope_pointer`), a different name |
| 12 | Deposit both seeds into the in-memory cells and spawn the resolve-tick loop with the new root name | `deposit_seed` / `deposit_write_seed` (`facade.rs:1776`, `:1796`), `spawn_resolve_tick_loop` (`facade.rs:2002`) | **exists, wired** — just never reached on an empty chain (`facade.rs:2016-2018`) |
| 13 | Persist locally | Nothing new: `publish` advances the sequence floor; the next boot cold-seeds the epoch floors from the pointer (`sync/boot.rs:212`); seeds are **never** persisted (`facade.rs:127-134`) | **exists** |

There is **no server-side step**. `blueprint/api.md:32` explicitly removed v1's vault-init endpoint and the `vaults` table: *"bootstrap is the derived vault pointer"*. The only API calls are `/content/upload` and `/registry/register`, both inside step 8.

---

## 2. The two things that genuinely do not exist

**(a) `owner-pseudonym-seed` — a specified but unimplemented frozen KDF edge.**
`blueprint/core.md:279` lists it: `login secret → ownerPseudonymSeed`, and `core.md:280` says `pseudonym-sign` takes `ECDH(ownerEnc, writerEnc) ‖ scopeId` **(owner: ownerPseudonymSeed)**. `crates/core/src/kdf/mod.rs:32-46` has no `CTX_OWNER_PSEUDONYM_SEED` and no `owner_pseudonym_seed()` function. Every production site that needs the owner's pseudonym today either fakes it (`net/rotation.rs:1771` `const OWNER_ROOT_SECRET: [u8; 32] = [0x6b; 32]`, `#[cfg(test)]`) or takes it as an injected parameter (`grants/create.rs:116`).

This is the highest-risk item: the pseudonym key is baked into the published `ownerPseudonymPk` of the commitment and into every structure signature. Choosing it wrong at provisioning time bricks all later rotation with `ResealError::SignerNotCommitted` (`rotation/reseal.rs:~180`), permanently, because the commitment is already published.

**(b) `OwnerScopeKeys` has zero production implementations.**
`crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs:81` defines the trait; the only two `impl`s are at `:1778` and `:3240`, both inside `#[cfg(test)]`. So the whole owner rotation arm is unwired for the same reason — this is the same defect class as #1205, one layer up.

Everything else in the table is a real, compiled, production function.

---

## 3. Where it belongs

**Module:** a new `crates/engine/src/sync/provision.rs`, sibling to `boot.rs`. It is a pure composition over injected seams (entropy, transport, floors, api, gateway) with no crypto of its own — the same shape as `sync/boot.rs::cold_start` (`boot.rs:161`) and `rotation/rotate_write.rs`. Put the seam-touching publish edges behind a trait as `rotate_write.rs` does, so the deterministic-simulation suites can drive it.

**Facade entry point:** inside `Engine::start`, immediately after the cold-start outcome at `facade.rs:1757-1765`, on the `outcome.vault_pointer.is_none()` branch — before `deposit_seed`/`deposit_write_seed` (`:1776`, `:1796`) and before `spawn_resolve_tick_loop(root_name, …)` (`:2018`). Provisioning then feeds `root_name` and both seeds into the existing plumbing and the loop spawns normally.

**Not a `Command`.** Three reasons, all verifiable: `Command` (`facade.rs:389-523`) has no provisioning-shaped variant and hosts have no reason to sequence it; the login secret needed for `vault_pointer_index` lives only in `SessionIdentity.login_secret` (`session.rs:44`, retained explicitly "for the runtime vault-pointer index probe"); and `spawn_resolve_tick_loop` is called once from `start` and takes the root name by value, so a later command could not retro-spawn it without restructuring.

---

## 4. v1 reference implementation — yes, and it is directly instructive

v1 provisioned **eagerly, on the login path, client-side, in both clients**, with the server told last.

- Web: `v1:apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts:432` — `completeBackendAuth()` awaits `initializeOrLoadVault()` after `/auth/login` and before `setAuthenticated()` (`:436`). New-account detection is a 404 from `vaultApi.getVault()` and only a 404 (`:133-139`); the provisioning branch is `:172-231`.
- Ordered steps (`useAuth.ts:172-231` + `v1:packages/core/src/vault/init.ts:37-52`): random root folder key → deterministic root IPNS keypair → publish the ECIES-wrapped key blob at a second derived IPNS name at **sequence 1** (`:187-193`) → publish the empty root folder metadata at **sequence 1** (`:204-210`) → `POST /vault/init` **last** (`:216-219`) → keys held in memory only (`:225-231`).
- Desktop mirrored it in Rust: `v1:apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/vault.rs:79-200`, triggered at `v1:apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/auth.rs:127-137`.
- Server did nothing at signup — `POST /vault/init` only records what the client already published (`v1:apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts:66-111`), and its body carries no crypto material (`v1:apps/api/src/vault/dto/init-vault.dto.ts:9-26`).

Three v1 lessons that transfer:

1. **Concurrency is a real bug, not a theoretical one.** v1 shipped a module-level `vaultInitPromise` dedupe (`useAuth.ts:45-50`, `:117`) *because* double-init hit a duplicate-key on `folder_ipns`, and separately handled `23505` server-side (`vault.service.ts:101-111`, from commit `a955f86f1`). v2's equivalent is two devices/tabs both minting a root and both PUTting index 0 → `PublishOutcome::LostRace`.
2. **Partial failure aborts.** v1 threw and stopped if either publish failed (`useAuth.ts:194-196`, `:211-213`; desktop `vault.rs:139-143`, `:184-188`) rather than proceeding to registration.
3. **The structural difference:** v1 had *no pointer indirection* — the root IPNS name was HKDF-derived from the identity key (`v1:packages/crypto/src/vault/derive-ipns.ts:29`, `:44-58`), so re-login re-derived it and the only non-derivable thing was the root folder key, which got its own published blob. v2 deliberately inverts this (`facade.rs:2096-2098`: the write scope seed is not owner-re-derivable), which is exactly why v2 needs a provisioning step where v1 needed only a key blob.

---

## 5. Tracker

**#1205 is the only issue covering this.** Verified: `parent_issue_url: null`, zero sub-issues, not a child of #655 / #991 / #992. Searches for `seal_repoint` and `deposit_write_seed` across all open and closed issues return only #1205.

Nearest relatives, none a duplicate:

| # | State | Relation |
|---|---|---|
