---

## 6. Minimal demo vs. production-correct

**They differ, but less than you'd hope — there is no shortcut around steps 1-11.** The drain cannot bootstrap its own root: `Drain::load_scope_root` (`sync/drain.rs:834-844`) reads the root record from the snapshot cache and returns `Halt::Unclassified` when absent. And `OwnerRotationNet::publish_scope_root` (`net/rotation.rs:622-634`) resolves the node's *current* record first, so it is a republish path and cannot mint a first root either.

**Smallest slice that gets login → create folder → survives reload:**

- Pick the owner pseudonym material and implement it as a real `kdf` edge (do **not** hardcode or improvise — see the brick risk below).
- One `provision_vault()` in `sync/provision.rs` doing steps 1-11 straight-line, no resume, no idempotence, no cross-device coordination, called from `facade.rs:~1760` on the empty-chain branch.
- Empty grant set (`entries: []`), single root scope, read epoch 1, write epoch 1, `WriteHistory::Carried(b"")`.
- On any failure: return `Err` from `start` and leave nothing published-and-forgotten — v1's abort discipline.

**What that owes later:**

1. **Idempotence/resume.** A crash between step 8 (root published) and step 11 (pointer published) leaves an orphaned root record; the next boot sees an empty chain and mints a *second* root. Needs either a probe-before-mint or a durable "provisioning in flight" marker.
2. **Concurrency.** Two devices provisioning the same account both PUT pointer index 0. One gets `PublishOutcome::LostRace`; the loser must adopt the winner's pointer rather than retrying its own. This is v1's `vaultInitPromise` bug in distributed form and it is worse — the loser has already published a root record and burned a write scope seed.
3. **The pseudonym decision is not deferrable.** Steps 3/4 publish `ownerPseudonymPk` into an owner-signed, epoch-free commitment. If provisioning derives it one way and a later `OwnerScopeKeys` production impl derives it another, every rotation fails `SignerNotCommitted` forever on accounts provisioned before the fix. **This is the one item that cannot be shipped "minimally and fixed later"** — it must be the real `owner-pseudonym-seed` edge from `blueprint/core.md:279`, landed in `crates/core/src/kdf/mod.rs` with a KAT, before any account is provisioned.
4. **Settings record** (#1144) and the **scope-pointer channel** (#1137) stay unpublished; both degrade cleanly today.
5. **Test regime.** #1205's "done when" demands an e2e over a real stack, not a unit test over fakes. The existing `seed_account` fixtures (`write_plane.rs:463`, `fuse_op_core.rs:1324`) should be *replaced by* a call to the real provisioning path, which is also the cheapest proof it works — and it makes #1121 mostly moot.</result>

## 3. WASM boundary

**No dangling TS calls. No unhandled worker message kinds.** `WorkerRequest` has 12 kinds (`protocol.ts:185-197`), `serve.ts:48-100` dispatches all 12; `buildCommand` covers all 16 descriptors with a `never`-bound exhaustiveness check (`commandCodec.ts:77-79`). WASM is genuinely built and consumed (`apps/web/scripts/build-wasm.mjs`, wired into both `dev` and `build`).

Rust-side orphans (exist in Rust, no TS path): `Command::Move` (`facade.rs:2306`) has **no wasm-bindgen builder** — web can only `relink`, not move-with-rename or conditional-replace; that path is desktop-only (`crates/fuse/src/ops.rs:246`). Also `Engine::view`/`EngineView::*`, `stream_version_cid`, `profile` — all FUSE-only, fine.

One latent risk: `EngineWasm` (`packages/client/src/worker/engineWasm.ts:82-174`) is a hand-written mirror of the generated `.d.ts` and **nothing typechecks it against the real artifact** — the only meeting point is a double cast at `packages/client/test/browser/engine.worker.ts:21`. A rename in `crates/wasm` yields a green typecheck and a runtime `undefined is not a function`.

## 4. Download / read-back

Complete end to end in the web path — `useFileDownload.ts` → `facade.download` → `host.rs:365` → `Engine::read_content` (`facade.rs:3004`) → CID-verified fetch. **But** the recently-landed ranged-read commit `77ad6a71e` is titled and scoped to **FUSE**, not web; the web ranged path (`openContentStream`/`readStream`) was already there and is consumed only via `MediaService`, and the plaintext chunk cache is in `crates/fuse`, not on the web read path.

The real download blocker is config — see below.

---

## 5. Ranked blockers, with tracking

**T0 — nothing in the demo works without these (configuration, not code)**

1. **The web bundle's content gateway is dormant by default, and an empty gateway fails every read closed.** `apps/web/src/engine/config.ts:88-93` gives `acceleratorBaseUrl`/`publicGateways` no default; `GatewayConfig::disabled` semantics at `content/read.rs:98-101`; `read_block` returns `ReadError::Unavailable` when the source list is empty (`read.rs:236-241`). That breaks **cold-start root adoption** (`net/adopter.rs:503` fetches the root record head block over the gateway), download, and therefore "reload and still see them" — not just download. Staging's `docker/Caddyfile` exposes only `api-staging` and `app-staging`; there is **no ingress route to Kubo's :8080 gateway**, so the var has nothing to point at. → tracked by **#653** ("Caddy in front of Kubo's gateway port, forward_auth to the API token-verification endpoint").
2. **The staging API is not configured for the v2 pin store.** `deploy-staging.yml:400-426` writes v1-era `IPFS_PROVIDER` / `IPFS_LOCAL_API_URL` / `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` into `.env.staging` and **never sets `KUBO_API_URL`**, which is the only name `KuboPinStore` reads (`apps/api/src/registry/pin-store.ts:73`). Unset → `POST /content/upload` answers 503 → **folder create also fails**, since the record head block goes through the same endpoint (`net/record_publish.rs:199`). → the env-name mismatch specifically is **untracked**; #653 covers the surrounding cutover, #1096 documents the same 503 for CI.
3. **No local demo recipe exists.** `tests/web-e2e/README.md:55-80` starts only postgres + `mock-ipns-routing` — no Kubo, no `KUBO_API_URL`, no `VITE_READ_ACCELERATOR_URL`. `docker/docker-compose.yml` does ship Kubo on :5001/:8080 and someguy on :8190, so a working local demo is assemblable, but the env wiring is not written down anywhere. → **untracked**.

**T1 — will make the demo look broken even when configured**

4. **A non-413 upload failure retries forever with no dead-letter** — `classify_upload` returns `Halt::Unclassified`, `apply_valve` charges no attempt. Any 503 from (1)/(2) hangs the upload row at "retrying" indefinitely rather than surfacing an error. → **#1208**.
5. **There is no way to force a sync pass.** `Command::ManualRefresh` falls to `Unimplemented` (`facade.rs:2372`); `snapshot()` is a local render only; and the `RefreshHintSource` seam is dead on both sides (see §1). The production poll cadence is **30 s** (`crates/engine/src/profile.rs:56`), so every write takes up to 30 s to publish and up to 30 s to reappear after a reload, with no override. → **untracked** — #1016 explicitly excludes `ManualRefresh` and `Logout` from its scope; #642 covers only the web-side chrome.
6. **Zero e2e coverage of the write path.** `tests/web-e2e/tests/smoke.spec.ts` asserts login, an **empty** cold start (`children: []`, line 42), and sign-out. Nothing exercises create/upload/reload/download. → **#1096**.

**T2 — smaller code holes on the demo path**

7. `Command::Move` has no wasm builder → web cannot move-with-rename or conditional-replace. → **untracked**.
8. `Command::Logout` is `Unimplemented` → the engine-side zeroize never runs; only worker teardown frees the material. → **untracked**.
9. `acceleratorBearer` exists in the Rust constructor and `engineWasm.ts:112` but `EngineHostConfig` has no such field — `engineHost.ts:76` passes `undefined` unconditionally. Correct positionally, just unwired. → **#1033**.
10. `EngineWasm` interface never typechecked against the generated bindings. → **untracked**.

**Not on the demo path (noted for completeness):** grants/sharing/rotation all `Unimplemented` (#1016, blocked on #1163's missing production `OwnerScopeKeys`); vault-settings write plane has no facade caller (#1144); repoint channels 2 and 3 never publish (#1137) and the one that does never renews (#1136); desktop is a scaffold (#650/#648/#649); sidebar Shared/Bin/Settings inert (#643).

**Unverified:** whether the repo variables `VITE_READ_ACCELERATOR_URL` / `VITE_PUBLIC_GATEWAYS` are actually set on the staging environment (they are referenced but their values are not in the repo); whether `https://delegated-ipfs.dev` — the default `VITE_ROUTING_ENDPOINTS`, and what staging would use since the deploy workflow never sets that var — accepts browser-origin `PUT /routing/v1/ipns/*`; and whether Kubo's gateway CORS defaults suffice for the cross-origin `?format=raw` fetches (`content/read.rs:290`).</result>

<result>Engine half added to the same branch. **PR #1211** now covers both halves and carries `Closes #1163`. Still a draft.

## Where the impl lives

`/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-myankelev-Code-random-cipher-box/2b9a962d-e9c8-4465-b4ef-93584f21a390/scratchpad/wt-1163/crates/engine/src/owner_keys.rs` — new file, registered as `mod owner_keys;` in `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`.

`OwnerSessionKeys&lt;'a&gt;` borrows `&amp;'a SessionIdentity` rather than copying seeds out of it — a cascade discovers its scope ids at runtime, so the derivations must stay lazy and the session stays the terminal owner of the secrets.

I put the module registration in `lib.rs`, **not** `rotation/mod.rs`: you listed `rotation/mod.rs` as contested, and #1200 does change it (13+/10−, it adds `sweep/sim.rs`). `lib.rs` is not in #1200's file list at all. I verified that list directly rather than assuming — #1200 touches exactly 10 files, and this branch's 8 files intersect it in zero places.

## How `writer_pseudonym` derives

`OwnerSessionKeys::writer_pseudonym(scope_id)` → `SessionIdentity::owner_writer_pseudonym_signer(scope_id)` → `kdf::pseudonym_sign(self.owner_pseudonym_seed.as_bytes(), scope_id)`.

`SessionIdentity` now carries `owner_pseudonym_seed: SecretBytes` beside `owner_pointer_seed`, derived at cold start via `kdf::owner_pseudonym_seed(bytes)` — satisfying the issue's checkable condition literally. `pointer_read_key` delegates to the session's existing per-scope derivation.

## The type boundary — I narrowed it

Yes, and it stayed entirely inside `session.rs`, so nothing rippled.

`owner_writer_pseudonym_signer(&amp;self, scope_id)` takes **only a scope id**. The owner's pairwise input is read from the session field, so a caller structurally cannot supply it — which is the failure mode you were worried about, closed at the type level rather than by convention.

I also renamed the old `writer_pseudonym_signer(pairwise_material, scope_id)` to `grantee_writer_pseudonym_signer(grant_ecdh, scope_id)`. That is what it always was; the doc had said "owner root secret or grant ECDH", which is now false for the owner. This was free: the method had **no production caller anywhere** — only its own in-file tests — so the rename touched 5 lines in one file. `pseudonym_sign`'s signature and the grantee derivation are unchanged.

What I did **not** do: wrap the grantee's pairwise material in a newtype. With no production caller to constrain, that would be speculative generality, and the ECDH source sits behind `grants/create.rs`, which is contested. Not filed as an issue either — there is nothing to fix until a caller exists, and the narrowing that mattered is done.

## Red-proof — confirmed

Swapped `owner_writer_pseudonym_signer` to use `self.owner_pointer_seed`. Two tests went red:

- `writer_pseudonym_is_the_owner_pseudonym_seed_edge` — `assertion left == right failed`
- `writer_pseudonym_is_none_of_the_rejected_owner_inputs` — `assertion left != right failed: the owner pointer seed stood in for the edge`

Restored from a file backup (no destructive git command anywhere in this task), re-ran, 4/4 green. The negative test covers all three inputs ADR 0005 rejected: the login secret, the owner pointer seed, and self-ECDH over the enc subkey. `X25519Secret` deliberately exposes no scalar getter, so I modeled the enc-subkey case as the actual rejected design — `enc.diffie_hellman(enc.public())` — which is a better test than comparing raw scalar bytes would have been.

## Gate exit codes — all 0

`cargo fmt --all -- --check` 0 · `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` 0 · `cargo test --workspace` 0 · `cargo check -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` 0 · `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` 0.

Rebase was a no-op — #1196 and #1206 were already in the fork base at `2a820e845`.

## Review gates on the engine half

- **crypto-privacy**: clean, 0 findings at every severity. It specifically verified the zeroization question I'd have flagged myself — `Zeroizing::new(*session.pointer_read_key(id).as_bytes())` copies out of an owned temporary that zeroizes on drop at end-of-statement, and `OwnerSessionKeys` never zeroes the session it borrows. Terminal-owner rule holds.
- **simplify**: one real finding, applied — the negative test's doc comment restated the `SignerNotCommitted` rationale already stated at its home on `owner_writer_pseudonym_signer`. Trimmed to a cross-reference (commit `055bc5f3b`).

## One thing for whoever picks up the wiring

`OwnerSessionKeys` carries `#[allow(dead_code)]` because the facade's rotation commands still don't construct it — `RotateNow`/`Revoke`/`Downgrade` reaching a rotation primitive is a separate, larger job than this trait. The PR body says so plainly rather than implying the owner arm is live.

The seam worth a test when that lands: assert `OwnerSessionKeys::writer_pseudonym(scope_id).verifying_key()` equals the `ownerPseudonymPk` the #1205 provisioning path actually commits. That is the exact join where a mismatch produces the permanent brick, and nothing in this PR can check both sides of it.</result>

## Verdicts

**1. Actionable — `grants/create.rs:1212` comment polarity — VALID, fixed.** The comment called `the_same_lagging_node_lets_the_grant_through_once_it_converges` "the negative direction of the same gate", but it asserts success (`outcome.expect("a converged subtree grants")`, 2 records published). Reworded to "The passing direction". Thread replied and resolved: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1200#discussion_r3741675621

**2. Nitpick — dead `sim::folder` — VALID, deleted rather than wrapped.** No call site in the workspace; the other `folder(` hits are a distinct local helper at `net/rotation.rs:3856` taking `Vec&lt;ChildRef&gt;`. CodeRabbit offered a wrapper, which would have kept a dead function alive behind its `#[allow(dead_code)]` — deletion is the root-cause fix.

**3. Nitpick — equivalence gate is depth-one — VALID, and materially worse than "optional".**

This is the finding worth the weight. The mechanism was as claimed, but the blast radius was larger: `Scenario` had no per-node children, `Scenario::fake` passed `&amp;[]`, `stage_scenario` passed `Vec::new()` — **and every other production-seam sweep test in `net/rotation.rs` also passed `Vec::new()` to `interior_record`**. So `walk_and_converge`'s frontier expansion (`body_children` → `next` → `canonicalize_frontier`), the `visited` set, `bind_labels` over grandchildren, and boundary detection below the root had *never* executed against `OwnerRotationNet` at all — only against `FakeNet` in `sweep.rs`, which does nest (diamond at `sweep.rs:1051-1053`, back-edge at `784-785`). For a PR whose central claim is that one `sweep_pass` behaves identically over sim and real seams, the claim was depth-one-shaped. No scenario reached depth two.

Fix: `Scenario.nodes` is now `(byte, epoch, children)`, threaded through both sides. `interior_name` was split out of `interior_record` (a node's write name is a pure function of its node id), so all names bind before any body seals and a parent can cite a child staged later. Four scenarios added — nested three-level convergence, a diamond, a mixed-epoch level with the root body listed out of node-id order (canonicalisation load-bearing, converged parent descended through to a lagging child), and a scope-root boundary nested below the root with the D6 self-heal driven from depth two. The gate additionally asserts the walk reached every node the scenario describes, so flattening fails loudly instead of agreeing trivially.

## Red-proof (both reverted)
- Reverting `stage_scenario` to childless bodies: `scenario "a nested subtree converges at every level" diverged` — sim `converged: [01, 02, 03]` vs production `converged: [01]`. The pre-existing flat scenarios stayed green, which is exactly the gap.
- Dropping `0x02`'s child: `left part of its subtree unwalked` — `[01, 02]` vs `[01, 02, 03]`.

The gate is `cargo test --workspace` (the required **Test** gate) via `net::rotation::tests::the_sweep_simulation_and_the_production_seams_agree`.

<summary>Agent "Implement ManualRefresh" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>Done. **PR #1212** (draft, `feat/manual-refresh` → `main`), 2 commits.

## What `ManualRefresh` now does

It files a request on the resolve tick loop's second wake source and awaits the verdict of the pass that answers it.

- **One pass implementation, reused.** `run_tick_loop` now sleeps the cadence but wakes early on a filed request, and the facade's resolve-tick driver moved onto it from `run_liveness_loop`. The loop is the only pass executor, so a forced refresh never runs beside the poll leg — no second pass to drift. **The poll cadence itself is unchanged.**
- **Nocache with teeth.** `ResolveMode` is threaded through `resolve` / `resolve_gated` / `resolve_and_hold` / `resolve_child` / `FolderRefresh`. Under `NoCache` the snapshot cache is never read, so only what the record plane serves this pass can be rendered or reported; a gate pass still writes back. Every other caller (cold start, drain, on-access refresh, content read) stays `CacheFirst` explicitly.
- **Resolve *and* drain**, which is what makes a queued op publish on the click instead of a cadence later. The command settles on the read legs; the drain reports through its own op events.
- **Failure is typed.** Unreachable plane → `EngineError::RefreshFailed`; a gate-rejected record → `EngineError::TrustViolation`.

## Coalesce, not queue

**Coalesce.** Requests outstanding when a pass starts share that pass; a request filed while a manual pass runs joins it. Two clicks = one network pass. Rationale: the loop is the sole driver of the durable op queue, so two passes would double-drive the drain; and requests filed before a pass starts still get a pass that starts after them.

## The `RefreshHintSource` seam: deleted

No host ever implemented a producer — desktop-seams explicitly declined it, the web built a queue nothing pushed to, and `LeaderRelay` already routed its cross-tab focus signal through `manualRefresh`. Its only consumer, `run_tick_loop`, was itself unreached. With the command implemented it is strictly better: host-agnostic, coalesced in one place, and able to report failure — which a fire-and-forget hint queue structurally cannot, and which is requirement 3. The designed-for push overlay keeps its extension point (a push handler calls the same command). Blueprint seam tables and `CONTEXT.md` updated.

One deliberate consequence, called out in the PR: `jittered_cadence` went with the loop that used it. It was never live (the facade drove the tick through `run_liveness_loop` at a flat cadence), so this deletes dead code, not behavior — swept through all nine doc sites that asserted jitter.

## Chrome

`snapshotStore.refresh()` commits the rejection as a `SnapshotError` instead of `.then(again, again)`; `refreshFailed` joins `tooManyStreams` as recoverable, so it renders through the existing `file-browser-notice` banner over the listing it left standing, with the retry already there. `trustViolation` stays fatal.

## Red-proof

Every new test broken, confirmed red, restored — 13 total: 4 in `write_plane.rs` (forced publish without cadence, no cache read, unreachable→failure, rejection→trust violation), 1 in `facade.rs`, 1 in `net.rs`, 4 in `refresh.rs`/`tick.rs` unit tests, 3 in TS. Two of them fail by hanging rather than asserting (removing the armed guard parks the command forever) — noted as such.

## Gates (all exit 0)

`cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo check -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:md`, `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`, `pnpm test`.

## Review gates

`/simplify` and `/security-review` both ran against the diff. The security review found one real fail-open — the failure verdict collapsed a gate rejection into retryable availability, which both hosts then treated as transient — fixed in the second commit by splitting the verdict, plus a test. It also flagged that the leader relay's per-focus-change forced pass would stack behind the engine's command serialization; that now collapses onto one trailing pass. Simplify's findings (stale "nine seams"/jitter/hint docs, `useRefreshHints`→`useRefreshOnWake`, `ceiling`→`recoverable`, `pub(crate)` demotion, duplicated rejection continuation, weak browser-suite assertion, thrice-stated invariant) are all folded in. One latent item was recorded and not fixed: `arm`/`close` are not refcounted, which would matter only if a re-login-in-place path ever spawned a second tick loop — unreachable today.</result>

## 1. Compose services

`docker/docker-compose.yml` (project `cipherbox-infrastructure`), 4 services:

| Service | Image | Ports | Line |
|---|---|---|---|

| `someguy` | `ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy:v0.11.1` | `8190:8190` (delegated routing HTTP), `4004:4004/tcp+udp` (libp2p swarm) | `docker/docker-compose.yml:60-88` |

- Yes, there is a Kubo service: `ipfs`, image `ipfs/kubo:v0.42.0` (`docker/docker-compose.yml:22`), API on `5001`, gateway on `8080` (`docker/docker-compose.yml:44-46`).

## 2. `KUBO_API_URL`

Confirmed: `apps/api/src/registry/pin-store.ts:73` (`configService.get&lt;string&gt;('KUBO_API_URL')`) is the only place in application source that *reads* it. Repo-wide grep across `apps/`, `packages/`, `crates/`, `tools/` finds no other reader; the only other source hits are the docstring at `apps/api/src/registry/pin-store.ts:57` and the unit-test helper `apps/api/src/registry/pin-store.test.ts:16`.

Where it is *set*:
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml:794` — `KUBO_API_URL: http://localhost:5001` (the `contract-suite` job).
- `.github/workflows/load-test.yml:136` — `KUBO_API_URL: http://localhost:5001`.
- **Nowhere** in `docker/docker-compose.yml` or `docker/docker-compose.staging.yml` (neither file sets `KUBO_API_URL` as an env var for any service, including `api` in the staging file, which only takes `env_file: .env.staging`, `docker/docker-compose.staging.yml:13`).
- **Nowhere** in an `.env.example`: no `.env.example` file exists anywhere in the repo (repo-wide `find -iname "*.env.example"` returns nothing, excluding `target/` build artifacts and the stray `.claude/worktrees/` copy). `docs/GETTING-STARTED.md:71` and `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md:40` both instruct `cp apps/api/.env.example apps/api/.env`, but that source file does not exist in this repo.
- **Not set** in `tests/web-e2e/README.md`'s local recipe (its exported env list at lines 52-55 omits `KUBO_API_URL`).

Effect of it being unset (`apps/api/src/registry/pin-store.ts:69,73-74`): `apiUrl` is `undefined`, `unpin()` no-ops and returns `false` (`pin-store.ts:100-103`), and `pin()` (the upload byte path) throws `ServiceUnavailableException('Hosted pin store not configured')` via the shared `rpc()` helper (`pin-store.ts:158-161`) — i.e., uploads fail closed with a 503 when Kubo isn't configured.

`apps/api/src/registry/registry.module.ts:37` wires `{ provide: PinStore, useClass: KuboPinStore }` — `KuboPinStore` is the only production `PinStore` implementation; there is no in-process fake pin store wired in the app module.

## 3. `apps/web/src/engine/config.ts` — environment variables read

Full file read (133 lines). Every `env.VITE_*` reference:

| Variable | Default | Behavior if absent | Citation |
|---|---|---|---|
| `VITE_API_URL` | `'http://localhost:3000'` | Falls back to localhost; empty string also falls back (rejected as `''` is treated same as undefined) | `config.ts:6,18-23` |
| `VITE_ENVIRONMENT` | `'local'` | Falls back to `'local'`; a value present but not in `['local','ci','staging','production']` throws | `config.ts:62-69` |
| `VITE_ROUTING_ENDPOINTS` | `'https://delegated-ipfs.dev'` (`DEFAULT_ROUTING_ENDPOINTS`) | Comma-split list; if the resulting list is empty, throws `'VITE_ROUTING_ENDPOINTS must list at least one routing endpoint'` | `config.ts:7,79-83` |
| `VITE_READ_ACCELERATOR_URL` | **none** | `configured()` returns `undefined` when unset/blank; becomes `acceleratorBaseUrl: undefined` | `config.ts:92` |
| `VITE_PUBLIC_GATEWAYS` | none (empty-list default via `list()`) | Comma-split list; empty array is accepted (no throw) | `config.ts:93` |
| `VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID` | none | Required only via `loginEnv()`; throws if missing when a login is attempted | `config.ts:31,104-111` |
| `VITE_WEB3AUTH_VERIFIER` | none | Same as above | `config.ts:31,104-111` |
| `VITE_E2E_HOOK` | none (boolean-ish "configured or not") | Determines `shipsE2eHook()`; refused (forced `false`) unless build is `staging`/`production` **and** it's configured — i.e. in `local`/`ci` this function is moot for gating | `config.ts:130-132` |

Your belief about the accelerator gateway is correct, per the code's own comment: `config.ts:88-91` — "The content gateway has no default: unset reads nothing rather than reaching for an endpoint nobody chose. Dormant is the fail-closed state, so a blank value must land there rather than configuring a gateway source whose every request fails." `acceleratorBaseUrl` is set via `configured(env.VITE_READ_ACCELERATOR_URL)` (`config.ts:92`), which returns `undefined` for absent/blank (`config.ts:45-47`) — there is no default constant for it anywhere in the file (only `DEFAULT_API_URL` and `DEFAULT_ROUTING_ENDPOINTS` exist as defaults, `config.ts:6-7`). Confirmed, not corrected.

Separately, `missingDeployEnv()` (`config.ts:113-122`) enforces that a `staging`/`production` build has `VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID`, `VITE_WEB3AUTH_VERIFIER`, and `VITE_API_URL` configured (`DEPLOY_ENV`, `config.ts:38`); this build-time gate does **not** include `VITE_READ_ACCELERATOR_URL` or `VITE_ROUTING_ENDPOINTS`, consistent with the accelerator being allowed to stay dormant even in a deployed build.

## 4. Existing local-run recipes

Repo-wide README search (excluding `node_modules`, `.git`, `target`, vendored `fuser`, and the stray `.claude/worktrees` copy) finds exactly 5 READMEs: `README.md`, `tests/web-e2e/README.md`, `crates/load/README.md`, `tools/hw-gates/fskit-spike/README.md`, `tools/hw-gates/vendor/fuser/README.md`. There is **no** README under `apps/api/`, `apps/web/`, `packages/`, or `crates/contract/`.

- **`README.md:107-132`** ("Getting started"): `docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml up -d` (no service filter — starts all 4: postgres, ipfs, someguy, mock-ipns-routing), `pnpm install`, `cp apps/api/.env.example apps/api/.env` and `cp apps/web/.env.example apps/web/.env` (both source files **do not exist** in the repo, per §2 above), then `pnpm dev`. `README.md:130-132` itself flags that this only boots "the v2 skeleton (a stub API and web shell)" and that `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` is v1-legacy. This is the only recipe in the repo whose `docker compose up -d` invocation (as written) would bring up Kubo — but it depends on `.env.example` files that are absent, so it cannot be followed as written.
- **`tests/web-e2e/README.md:38-81`**: explicitly brings up only `docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml up -d postgres mock-ipns-routing` (`:46`) — Kubo and someguy are deliberately **not** started. It boots the API directly via `node apps/api/dist/main.js` with manually exported env vars (`:52-58`) that do **not** include `KUBO_API_URL`, builds two web bundles, and runs Playwright. `tests/web-e2e/README.md:20-25` states the suite covers no write path (no upload/download) yet, consistent with Kubo/pin-store being absent from this recipe. This recipe uses the mock `/routing/v1` record store; it leaves out Kubo entirely (no real content store), so uploads would fail closed per §2/§3 if attempted.
- **`crates/load/README.md:32-40`**: says "The harness needs a live API. With the docker stack up:" (`:34`) but does not specify which services or file, nor set `KUBO_API_URL` in its example (`:37-39` sets only `LOAD_TEST_SECRET`). It does not itself boot anything — it assumes a stack is already running.
- **`docs/GETTING-STARTED.md`** (root-README-flagged as v1 legacy, `README.md:83-84`): a full recipe at `docs/GETTING-STARTED.md:41-185` that runs the whole `docker/docker-compose.yml` and would bring up Kubo, but it documents services (`redis`, port `6380`, a `tee-worker`) that do not exist in the current `docker/docker-compose.yml` (confirmed absent from the file read in §1), and references `apps/desktop/.env.example`, which also does not exist. This doc is stale relative to the current compose file.
- **`docs/DEVELOPMENT.md:1-40`**: same v1-legacy status; table at `:19-24` also lists a `redis` service and a "staging stack... adds `api`, `tee-worker`, `caddy`, `alloy`" note (`:26`) that doesn't match the current `docker-compose.staging.yml` (no `tee-worker` there, per §1).

**Answer to your specific question**: none of the recipes that are actually executable as written starts Kubo. `README.md`'s recipe nominally would (full `docker compose up -d`), but it is broken by missing `.env.example` files. `tests/web-e2e/README.md` explicitly starts only `postgres` and `mock-ipns-routing`, deliberately excluding Kubo/someguy. No recipe in the repo demonstrates a complete, working, end-to-end local boot that includes a live Kubo and exercises the pin-store's Kubo path.

## 5. Contract suite's stack

`blueprint/testing.md:127-134` corroborates: "A Rust integration-test crate constructs real engines with production seam implementations pointed at the CI stack (real NestJS app + Postgres + Kubo + the `/routing/v1` store)..." and `blueprint/testing.md:226-232` states explicitly: "The CI stack: Postgres, Kubo, the API under test, and a local `/routing/v1` record store... someguy itself runs in staging, not CI... ## Findings

**1. `crates/engine/src/net/focus.rs` — focused-folder failures dropped from the verdict (Major). VALID — real correctness defect.**
`FolderRefresh::run` returned only `bool` (changed), swallowing `Unavailable`, `Gate(Seam)`, gate rejections, and the kind-transplant case. `facade.rs:2201` derived the settled verdict from the root result alone, so a forced pass whose root resolved `Current` while the folder in view answered nothing settled `Reconciled` and `Command::ManualRefresh` returned `Ok(Done)` over a listing that never moved.
Fix: added `RefreshVerdict::worst` (worst-wins lattice, `Rejected &gt; Unreachable &gt; Reconciled`) in `crates/engine/src/sync/refresh.rs`; `run` now returns `FolderRefreshReport { changed, verdict }` folding each per-folder failure (`EpochBelowFloor` stays sweep-pending staleness → `Unreachable`; other rejections and the kind transplant → `Rejected`); the tick loop settles `root_verdict.worst(folder_verdict)`.
**Deliberate departure from the suggestion:** the staleness ladder's `last_success` stays bound to the root leg. The ladder measures the record plane, which the root leg alone proves answered — folding one dark focus folder into it would escalate the rung on a demonstrably reachable plane.
Proof: `a_manual_refresh_reports_an_unreachable_focus_folder_as_a_failure` (`crates/engine/tests/write_plane.rs`) — authors the folder on device A, focuses it on device B, darkens only that folder's record, asserts `Err(RefreshFailed)`. **Verified as a true negative control**: reverting the settle to the root verdict makes it fail with `Ok(Done)`. Lattice precedence covered by `the_worst_leg_settles_the_pass`.

**2. `packages/client/src/leaderRelay.ts` — trailing refresh after close (Minor). VALID.**
`forceRefresh` is never *initiated* after close (all callers sit behind a `closed` check), but a pass in flight when `close()` lands settles afterwards and its `finally` re-enters `forceRefresh` on a set `refreshTrailing`, dispatching from a relay that already stepped down. Applied **one of the three** suggested hunks — the `if (this.closed) return;` guard. The other two are redundant behind it: `closed` is a one-way latch, so the `finally` re-entry is already a no-op and clearing `refreshTrailing` in `close()` only scrubs state nothing reads again.
Proof: `drops the trailing pass a step-down overtook` (`packages/client/src/broadcastTransport.test.ts`) — also verified as a true negative control.

**3. `CONTEXT.md:53` — nocache wording (Minor). VALID.** "only what the record plane serves that pass is rendered" read as forbidding the retained listing `snapshotStore.test.ts` asserts. Reworded tersely rather than taking the proposed text verbatim (glossary entry), and folded in the finding-1 verdict change.

**4. `apps/web/src/engine/snapshotStore.test.ts:343` — absence-justifying comment (Minor). VALID.** Replaced with the invariant: `// A failed refresh must not trigger a follow-up pull.` It is not mere restatement — `toHaveLength(1)` alone doesn't tell a reader the one pull is the setup pull.

**Review-body nitpick (no inline thread, `crates/fuse/src/error.rs:69-70`):** handled — added `EngineError::RefreshFailed` to `availability_failures_stay_availability`. Nothing to resolve since it has no thread; accounted for here.

<result>Settled — and investigating it turned up something that changes the picture.

## Decision: option 1 — `EpochBelowFloor` does not fold at all

Three pieces of engine evidence, not just the tidier reading:

1. **`CONTEXT.md` "Epoch lag"** — a lagging node is *"readable via history links, queued for the lazy wave"*, and the wave is *"carried by ordinary writes and/or a background sweep rather than an eager cascade"*. It is specified to lag.
2. **`crates/engine/src/gate/floor.rs`, `Strictness::AtOrAboveFloor`** — documented as *"the one read that must reach records the **epoch** stage refuses"*, taken by `rotation::sweep`'s interior nodes. Epoch-lagged interior records are a designed-for class the **sweep** handles; the child gate refusing them is the intended split.
3. **Decisive one — `crates/engine/src/rotation/sweep.rs`** — the engine *already* treats this as neither availability nor trust: `SweepResolveFailure::Superseded` is a third verdict (per ADR 0003) explicitly *"neither a trust violation the caller must not retry nor a transport stall"*, and `is_retryable()` excludes it.

Your framing was right for the wrong reason, and the sharper argument is retryability: `Unreachable` → `EngineError::RefreshFailed` → `VfsError::Unavailable` is an explicitly **retryable** verdict, but no retry of the pass clears epoch lag — only the sweep does. Folding it advertised a retry that provably cannot work. That contradiction with `is_retryable` is what settles it, and it holds without needing option 3.

The rationale now lives in one place, `rejection_verdict` in `focus.rs`, which also made it directly testable.

## The test — and a problem I found

My first attempt (`a_manual_refresh_does_not_fail_on_an_epoch_lagged_focus_folder`, on the existing `DeepCreate` harness) **passed the negative control, i.e. it was vacuous**, so I deleted it rather than ship it. Probing found why: `raise_epoch_floor` makes `refresh_seed_floors` revoke the cached scope read seed, so `read_seed` is `None` and the **focus-folder leg is skipped entirely** — the folder is never resolved and no `EpochBelowFloor` ever occurs. I verified this with a discriminator: blanking the `photos` record alone yields `RefreshFailed`, but blanking it *with the floor raised* yields `Ok(Done)`.

**New true-negative control:** `only_an_epoch_lagged_folder_costs_the_pass_nothing` (`crates/engine/src/net/focus.rs`) — asserts `rejection_verdict` returns `None` for `EpochBelowFloor` and `Some(Rejected)` for `SequenceNotNewer`. Confirmed: changing the `EpochBelowFloor` arm to `Some(..)` fails it. This pins the classification, not the end-to-end path — I'm being explicit that it is the narrower guarantee.

Two things worth your attention, neither fixed here:

- **`an_epoch_lagged_focus_folder_rejects_without_raising_abuse` (`crates/engine/tests/write_plane.rs:3380`) is pre-existing and vacuous** for the same reason — the folder it claims "rejects" is never resolved, and both its assertions hold trivially. Reaching that branch honestly needs a real rotation that republishes the root at the new epoch (so a fresh seed is deposited) with an interior folder still lagging. That is a real chunk of harness work, so I did not fold it in.
- The skipped-leg case (`read_seed` `None` while focus folders are open → `Reconciled` over a window that never refreshed) is the same *shape* as the bug I fixed, but I do not believe it is reachable in production: the floor law only raises the read-epoch floor on an AAD-confirmed root unseal, which deposits the new seed in the same step. The test harness raising the floor directly is synthetic.

<result>Done. PR **#1219** (draft): https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1219

## Verified step list, with corrections to yours

Provisioning lives in **`crates/engine/src/sync/provision.rs`** (`provision_vault`, pure composition over seams, sibling to `boot.rs::cold_start`), with the production network arm in **`crates/engine/src/net/provision.rs`** (`VaultProvisionNet`). It is called from **`Engine::start`** (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs`, `provision_first_run_vault`) on the `outcome.vault_pointer.is_none()` branch, before the seed deposits and before `spawn_resolve_tick_loop`.

Your list was right on 1–8 and 11–13. Four corrections:

1. **Step 9 is wrong: `build_repoint_object` cannot mint the genesis re-point.** It takes `prev_root: IpnsName` by value and enforces `new_root != prev_root` plus a strictly advancing write epoch — the invariants of an *advance*, not of a first publication. The object is built directly with `prev_root: None`, as both fixtures do.
2. **A step was missing: cold-seed the floors.** Without the write-epoch floor, `RootAdopter::recover_write_scope_seed` returns `None` ("no known write floor ⇒ held keyless"), so every tick in the first session surfaces no write seed. It runs *before* any publish, so an account whose floors already exceed the genesis epoch is refused rather than pointed at a root its own floor law rejects.
3. **The publisher must NOT advance the per-name sequence floor** (I tried; it broke the first write). Advancing it turns the session's first resolve of the genesis root from `Adopted` into `Current`, which writes no snapshot cache — and `Drain::load_scope_root` loads the root it re-authors *from that cache*, so `open_pass` halted `Unclassified` and the folder never published. The floor moves on the adopt, exactly as for every drain publish.
4. **`vault_pointer.is_none()` is not evidence of a new account** — see below. Also: provisioning is gated on a configured API base URL, for the same reason login is (register-first has no offline form); `ApiBaseUrl::offline` is `test-kit`-only, so a shipped host cannot reach the skip.

There is no server-side step; the only API calls are the recovery probe, the upload, and two register-firsts.

## The one change I'd flag hardest

Both review gates independently found the same **critical** bug in my first cut: `fanout_get_verify` tolerates a per-endpoint transport failure as staleness, so `resolve_vault_pointer` returns `None` for a **total outage** exactly as it does for a new account. Inferring "new account" from that would mint a second genesis vault at the one *fixed, account-derived* pointer name that names the first — whose owner-write blob holds the only copy of a write scope seed nobody can re-derive. Silent, irrecoverable vault loss.

Fixed by making the mint's precondition positive: `VaultProvisionPublisher::require_vacant_vault_pointer` requires the API's recovery cache (which outlives an IPNS EOL lapse) to 404, then asks every routing endpoint individually — any record is `AlreadyPublished`, and if not one endpoint affirmatively answered, the probe is `Indeterminate` and nothing is minted or even drawn. Both oracles are used only to *refuse*, so a hostile or lagging answer can block a mint, never cause a wrong one.

## What a failure leaves behind

- Before the root publish, and on either probe refusal — nothing, not even a drawn seed.
- At the root publish — a registered name and a pinned head block, possibly a root record; **no pointer names any of it**. Unreachable, never authoritative.
- At the pointer publish — the same, plus a pointer that may be durable (an `Unconfirmed` verdict means an endpoint acked). The next boot resolves it and cold-starts normally off the root's owner-write blob.
- The floor cold-seed is the one durable local effect; re-running seeds the same epochs.

## The join test and its red proof

`the_committed_pseudonym_is_the_one_the_owner_arm_derives` drives real `provision_vault` with a real `OwnerSessionKeys` over a real `SessionIdentity`, decodes the grant section back out of the published head block, and compares `commitment.owner_pseudonym_pk` against `OwnerSessionKeys::writer_pseudonym(scope_id).verifying_key()`. Its mirror `..._is_none_of_the_rejected_owner_inputs` rejects the login secret, `owner_pointer_seed`, and self-ECDH over the enc subkey.

**Red proof:** swapping provisioning's pseudonym source to `pseudonym_sign(pointer_read_key(scope), scope)` — the join test failed and **every other test in the crate still passed**, which is exactly the issue's claim. Restored, green. Also red-proofed: publishing the pointer before the root (2 tests red), dropping the floor cold-seed (2 red), ignoring the vacancy probe (2 red), and disabling the `start` branch entirely (the write_plane integration test red at "a vault pointer is published"). All restored, all green.

## Other tests (all in the merge-blocking `Test` gate)

14 in `sync/provision.rs`, 1 in `facade.rs` pinning the refusal-vs-stall classification, and `a_first_run_account_provisions_its_vault_and_publishes_a_write` in `crates/engine/tests/write_plane.rs` — no account fixture at all: `start` mints the vault, a created folder publishes, the root advances past its genesis sequence, and a second device cold-starts off nothing but what provisioning published.

## Review-gate outcomes

- **`/simplify`** (manual pass): tightened both module headers, dropped the now-dead `#[allow(dead_code)]` on `OwnerSessionKeys`, collapsed the floor advance out of the publisher.
- **`/security-review`**: 1 critical (fixed, above), 1 high — provisioning verdicts were all one error class, so a `FloorRegression` through provisioning surfaced as availability while the identical condition through cold start is a trust violation; fixed with `ProvisionError::is_retryable` splitting the facade mapping. Plus a false "nothing left behind" doc claim (corrected). Clean on owner gating, encode/decode symmetry, the auth gate, register-first ordering, determinism, RefCell-across-await, and key handling.
- **`/crypto-privacy-review`**: same critical, plus a medium — `draw_seed` and `reseal.rs`'s four HPKE ephemeral draws bypassed the silent-seam guard, and the genesis section's owner blob and owner-write blob seal to the **same** recipient key, so a seam returning `Ok(())` having written nothing would give both the identical HPKE context (a key/nonce reuse XORing out both scope seeds). Fixed: `draw_seed` refuses an all-zero draw, and `reseal.rs` now routes all four ephemerals through the existing `fresh_ephemeral`. Confirmed correct on seed independence, nonce uniqueness, the pseudonym join, `GENESIS_EPOCH = 1`, name/signer pairing, privacy, and zeroization.

## Gates (each exit code captured directly)

`cargo fmt --all -- --check` → 0 · `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` → 0 · `cargo test --workspace` → 0 · `cargo check -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` → 0 · `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` → 0

## Contract suite

Ran against a live stack (built + migrated the API, booted test:3000 and prod:3100 with the CI env; no stale processes were holding either port): **21 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**. None of them touch provisioning — its only API-plane surface is upload + register-first, both already covered. Provisioning also needs a `/routing/v1` record transport, which the contract crate does not wire, so **the end-to-end "fresh account creates a folder and it publishes over a real stack" proof belongs to #1096** (`ci: extend the web e2e smoke slice to the write path`), not here. I stopped only the two API processes I started; the docker stack is still up.

## Follow-ups to file

1. **Resume / idempotence.** A crash between the root publish and the pointer publish orphans a root record; the next boot mints a second one. Nothing reads the orphan and nothing reclaims it, so each retry adds a registered, pinned name against the account's quota with no retirement path.
2. **Concurrent-provision adoption.** Two devices provisioning at once both pass the vacancy probe (neither pointer exists yet) and both publish at sequence 1, so the loser's verdict is `Unconfirmed`, not `LostRace` — a same-sequence fork, not a CAS loss. Its `start` fails having already published a root and burned a write scope seed; recovery is a restart.
3. **Partial-outage clobber (residual of the critical fix).** The probe closes the total-outage and empty-endpoint-set cases. It does not close a partial outage where a live endpoint answers "no record" for a name a down endpoint holds and the recovery cache has not yet seen. Fix is a quorum rule, or conjoining the `is_new_user` flag `/auth/login` already returns and `Engine::start` currently discards — both cross-device-coordination decisions this slice scoped out.
4. **Genesis root sequence floor rests on tick ordering.** Left at 0 deliberately (raising it breaks the cache write, correction 3 above), so the first write is correct only because the tick body resolves before it drains. A self-adopt of the publish receipt would make it independent of that ordering.</result>

## PR

**#1217** — https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1217 — **draft**, no tool attribution in title, body, or either commit. Branch `fix/upload-terminal-state-and-rotation-gates`, forked from and still current with `origin/main` @ `21b78a13f` (PR #1212 had not merged, so no rebase was needed).

## Per issue

**#1208** — `classify_upload`'s `let-else` dropped every non-413 failure into `Halt::Unclassified`, so the valve charged nothing. Both classifiers are now exhaustive on one rule: a refusal that judged *these bytes* is charged; one that judged the transport, the session, or the request rate is not. The identical hole in `classify_register` is closed too. Proof: `a_standing_server_refusal_dead_letters_instead_of_cycling_forever` drives a persistent 503 through the real drain to `AttemptsExhausted`, plus two unit tests over both call paths.

**#1201** — `record_scope_boundary` now requires a present ascent link. Rather than write the check twice, the two-binding proof became one function, `gated_child_root`, shared with the sweep's `gated_child_scope_root`. Proof: `the_vault_root_named_by_the_child_scope_index_is_refused`.

**#1202** — `Rejected` and `Unavailable` now join `unreachable` behind an `isolates_the_node` predicate. I **verified** `create_read_grant` refuses on `dropped_lost_race` ∪ `unreachable` before relying on it — it does, in one check.

## Gates (bare, exit codes captured directly)

`cargo fmt --all --check` **0** · `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` **0** · `cargo test --workspace` **0** (39 suites) · `cargo check -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` **0** · `lint:tracker-refs` **0**. No TS touched. All 27 CI checks pass, including the Contract Suite — which really ran in CI, so the local skip-when-`CONTRACT_API_URL`-unset trap did not mask it.

## What the review gates found — and this matters

The first commit was wrong in ways I did not catch myself. Six review agents converged on three defects, and I fixed all three:

1. **I introduced a data-loss path.** Bare `400` → `Permanent` dead-lettered with zero attempts and released the staged blocks. The API stamps no `code` on its 400s, so a proxy or WAF 400 was indistinguishable — one would have destroyed the user's unpublished file. Now charged.
2. **429 charged would have been self-inflicted.** The API throttles every route; a client draining a backlog would rate-limit itself into dead-lettering it. Now uncharged.
3. **I silently removed the sweep's retry.** Bucketing `Unavailable` into `unreachable` meant `run_sweep` returned `Ok` on pass 1 — one unfetchable name would end the sweep with the node epoch-lagged and the rotation reporting success. `unreachable` now carries its verdict and `worth_another_pass` restores the retry; that also keeps a trust rejection distinguishable from an availability blip.

## Where the issues were wrong

- **#1208's "give the op a terminal state the UI can render as failed" needed no work.** `Event::DeadLetter` and `SnapshotView::dead_letters` already reach `apps/web`, which renders three surfaces. Only the classification was missing.
- **#1208's "classify the statuses that are terminal" is unsafe as literally written.** Concluding `Permanent` from a bare status is what the file's own positive-evidence rule forbids. Terminal-on-sight rests only on a stamped discriminator or `MalformedContentCid`; a standing refusal still settles, via the budget.

## Deliberately not fixed — four issues filed, each with a dependency edge

- **#1224** — the cascade reads the same child-scope index without requiring an ascent link. **Both** security reviewers found this independently, HIGH. Pre-existing, but it means #1201 closes the hole at two of three gates. The fix needs a child-vs-own-root distinction inside `gated_root` I was not willing to guess at.
- **#1225** — a re-seal can strip an ascent link its own gates then reject (the rule-8 encode-side mirror).
- **#1226** — `AttemptsExhausted` releases the staged version where CONTEXT.md says a dead-letter preserves it. Pre-existing, but this PR widens what reaches it. I chose not to redesign the escalation policy unasked.
- **#1227** — the sweep reports a newer-build node as a trust rejection.

**#1224 is the one to look at first** — it is the same invariant as the issue you asked me to fix, still open at a third gate.</result>

## What changed

**#1209 (staging 503):** `.env.staging` now sets `KUBO_API_URL=http://ipfs:5001`. I did **not** restore `.env.example` — the environment is inline `export` blocks instead, matching the only recipe in the repo that works (`tests/web-e2e/README.md`), and avoiding `apps/web`, which another PR owns. Worse than the Kubo case: `runOnce` returns *before* `alerter.walkComplete`, so there's no error, no warn, no zero-count — silent until IPNS names hit EOL. Fixed, plus a mirrored boot report.

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**PR: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box-next/pull/62** — `decisions/0007-derived-idempotent-first-run-mint.md`, Status Proposed, dated 2026-08-09. Body and commit verified clean of tool attribution.

## The decision, in three sentences

The genesis scope seeds become derived rather than drawn — two new KDF catalog edges over the login secret — so every attempt by one account mints the same root name, the same keys and the same re-point object. The mint's success condition moves from "my PUT landed" to "a re-point I can open under my own pointer read key names my derived root," and the root publish likewise completes on any adoptable record at the derived name, whether this run or a crashed earlier one published it. One decision, because both issues reduce to "what is authoritative when a mint did not finish," and both answers fall out of removing the entropy draw.

That answers the three required questions: the orphaned root is neither reclaimed nor leaked but **ceases to be an orphan** (the retry re-derives its name and adopts it); the fork has **no winner because there is no fork of identity**, only of bytes at one name; and the loser's recovery is neither adoption nor hard failure — it completes normally.

## How I resolved the D8 tension

I did not claim an exemption. The journal is rejected **first on its own terms** — it is per-device, so it is structurally blind to #1222's cross-device half and would still need the adoption rule that provably cannot exist — and **then on D8**. The exemption argument ("crash recovery only names bytes for re-checking, so it is not an authority") fails on the journal's *absence*: an empty journal routes the mint down the "nothing was published" branch, so its absence decides behaviour, which is authority in exactly the leaking direction, at exactly the moment it is least likely to exist (first run, fresh device, cleared store). ADR 0006's structure is evaluated as the container it would use and rejected with it — a new kind would be correct if a store were needed, and none is.

## Rejected, each in strongest form

Registry serialization on the pointer name (registration is idempotent by contract and both devices are the *same account*, so manufacturing a conflict means the server issues mint tokens — constraint 1 inverted); a deterministic fork winner with the loser adopting (unevaluable: a device chooses over what it sees, and one that sees only its own record concludes it won; a seq-2 collapse forks at seq 2 for the same reason); a registry listing plus retire sweep (serves a population #6's clean break makes empty, at the cost of a new API surface with a server-authored work-list); and simply accepting the leak (right about #1221, blind to #1222).

## Where the brief was wrong

1. **The loser's verdict is not necessarily `Unconfirmed`, and there may be no loser at all.** `net/fanout.rs:119-124` retains a record only on a **strictly greater** sequence, so a same-sequence tie is settled by endpoint iteration order. The interleaving `PUT(A), GET(A), PUT(B), GET(B)` gives **both** devices `PublishOutcome::Published`: A reads its own bytes back before B's PUT lands. Both complete `start`, both deposit seeds, both write, and neither is told. #1222's framing (loser gets `Unconfirmed`, `start` fails, restart recovers) describes the benign interleaving only.
2. **"The losing device may already have written" is not reachable the way the brief implies.** Inside `provision_vault` no writes happen — a failed pointer publish returns `Err` and `facade.rs:1809-1813` clears the session before any loop spawns. Writes are at stake only in the both-`Published` case above. The substance of your instinct was right; the mechanism is different, and it is worse.
3. **The orphaned root's IPNS record does self-expire** — 90-day client-signed EOL, and the republisher is a keyless re-PUT that cannot extend one. So your "maybe the real cost is only the pin and the registry entry" hypothesis is correct on the facts. I did not make the ADR smaller on that basis, because the same root cause (a discarded random seed) is what makes #1222 unadjudicable, and one fix covers both.
4. **Registration cannot serialize anything.** `net/register.rs:9-11` states it is idempotent server-side, and v1's duplicate-key precedent that #1222 cites cannot be reused in v2 — worth knowing before anyone reaches for it.
5. Minor: the catalog's non-edge list (`blueprint/core.md:296-297`) says "scope override seeds (random at rotation), scope seeds at grant cuts (random)" — it never actually states the genesis seed is random, so D1 narrows a phrasing rather than overturning a stated rule. `CONTEXT.md:8` ("keyed from one random scope seed") is the text that genuinely has to change.</result>

## 1. `desktop-e2e.yml` — folded in

Swapped all three env blocks (one `.env` heredoc, two `env:` maps): the four dead names out, `KUBO_API_URL=http://localhost:5001` and `ROUTING_V1_URL=http://localhost:3001` in. **Two things there I did not fix, deliberately.** It still installs and health-checks Redis on all three OSes to feed `REDIS_HOST`/`REDIS_PORT` that nothing reads — removing those is install-step surgery, not a name swap, so per your rule I stopped. And the file is marked **DORMANT** at line 1 (`tests/desktop-e2e was demolished with the v1 layout`) and still builds `@cipherbox/crypto`, `core`, `api-client`, `sdk-core`, `sdk` — none of which exist. It cannot run today regardless of my change. It needs a rewrite when the v2 desktop matrix lands, not patching.

## 2. Republisher — chose "boot report stays, walk signal is a separate decision"; filed **#1229**

Both options that are contained in `apps/api` are wrong on the merits:

- **`walkComplete(0, 0)`** sets exactly the gauges a *configured* republisher with zero registered names sets (`observeRepublisherWalk` writes `republisherLastWalkNames`/`republisherLastWalkRepublished`). It manufactures a healthy-looking signal for an unhealthy state — worse than the current silence.
- **Throwing** would surface via `WorkerScheduler`'s catch, but it contradicts an explicitly supported shape: `record-transport.ts` documents "unit deploys, BYO-only", and the early return exists precisely to avoid alert-storming them. That is the identical mistake this PR avoided for `KUBO_API_URL`.

The real fix is a *distinct* signal — a `republisher_configured` gauge or a walk-outcome label — which adds to the `RepublisherAlerter` contract and `MetricsService` and changes what dashboards and alert rules receive. That is your stated bar for filing. #1229 carries the mechanism: the `runOnce`/`walkComplete` ordering with line refs, why the gauges can't distinguish the two states, and why each alternative was rejected.

## 3. Double boot log — implemented

`RegistryModule` now exports `PinStore`; `ContentModule` imports it instead of declaring a second `{ provide: PinStore, useClass: KuboPinStore }`. I tested rather than guessed at "is it small": three lines, no cycle (`RegistryModule` doesn't import `ContentModule`), and it matches what `ContentModule`'s own doc already claims — *"Reuses the registry's pin-store port"*, which two instances quietly contradicted. Verified: typecheck 0, 181 tests pass, app boots, each unset-variable error now prints **exactly once**, and `POST /content/upload` still returns **201** through the shared instance.

<result>Done. PR **#1217** — https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1217 — still a draft, 4 commits, all 27 CI checks green, working tree clean, no rebase needed (`origin/main` unmoved at `21b78a13f`).

## #1225 — folded in

`ScopeRootIdentity` now carries `owes_ascent_link`, and `reseal_scope_root` refuses with `ResealError::AscentLinkDropped` before anything is sealed or signed. The flag is sourced from what the replaced record carried (`SweptScopeSource::carried_ascent_link`, `plane.section.ascent_link.is_some()`) — never from `parent_node_seed`, since the field that *produces* the link cannot also be the evidence one is owed.

Test: `a_descendant_re_sealed_with_no_parent_seed_fails_closed_release_active`, verified passing under `cargo test --release` (exit 0), asserting both the refusal and that the same identity with a seed does seal a link.

One honest caveat the review surfaced: at both `net/rotation.rs` sites the flag and the seed are coupled through the same gate, so the check cannot fire there today. Its teeth are at the caller-supplied `ParentScopePlan` / `RotateScopePlan`, whose doc now names the field as the caller's to set.

## #1226 — implemented, then reverted. Not fixed.

I built it, and the security review you mandated killed it. Two HIGH findings I verified in the code myself:

1. **The preservation is dishonest on the exact path it targets.** The upload loop `remove_staged_bytes`es each leaf as it confirms, while `abandon` → `registered_by` retires the whole version's CIDs read back from the manifest. So a part-way upload has its early leaves gone locally *and* unpinned remotely — preserving the remaining suffix keeps a version that can never be assembled again.
2. **Preserved dead letters have no reclaim path.** `prune_preserved_dead_letters` drops an entry only once its root block is gone, and nothing releases that root; `discard_upload` needs the op still queued. Routing the common server-driven failure there means a standing 503 fills the staging budget permanently and `beginWrite` refuses every future write, never recovering.

So preserving trades silent data loss for a remotely-triggerable permanent wedge. Per your instruction to stop rather than design an escalation-policy change, I reverted to `main`'s behaviour and wrote the shape into #1226: it needs either the retire to stop covering already-uploaded leaves, or a reclaim/retry surface for preserved dead letters, or `CONTEXT.md` amended — and the partial-upload case has to be decided explicitly, since no arrangement of local blocks recovers a version whose early leaves were unpinned.

## #1224 assessment — needs a rule for the proposed fix, but there is a different mechanical one

**The fix in the issue is wrong.** `Ancestry::record` has no self-reference filter (unlike `eager_set.rs`, which skips `child.scope_id == root_scope_id`), and `canonicalize` dedups by id only. A committed writer plants S's own id in S's index → `parent_node_seed(&amp;S)` returns `Some` → the proposed rule fires on a vault root with no ascent link → `Rejected`, which the cascade treats as non-retryable. That is a permanent, writer-triggerable abort of the owner's own revocation. It is also under-strict the other way: contingent on the ancestry being seeded.

**But it is mechanical via a different edit.** `gated_write_plane` is *statically* a child-index read — both callers (`direct_child_index` via `enumerate_eager_set`, `resolve` via `cascade_rotate_scope`) pre-seed the root into `visited` and re-key it through `rekey_one` without ever calling `resolve` on it. So give `gated_write_plane` its own root read ending in the `gated_child_root` helper this PR added, and leave `gated_root` for the two callers that legitimately gate a scope's own root. That also closes the ancestry-contingency gap, since the requirement becomes unconditional at the call site. Full analysis is on the issue.

I have not implemented it — your call.

## Condition 1 — the static claim **held**, but my earlier reading was incomplete

I re-traced it rather than trusting myself, and found a **third** production call site I had missed. There are two *method* callers but three call sites through the traits:

| Call site | Why the walk root never reaches it |
|---|---|
| `eager_set.rs:265` `direct_child_index` | `visited.insert(root_scope_id)` at :231, checked at :258 **before** the resolve; the root's own index arrives as a *parameter*, never via a read |
| `cascade.rs:499` `resolve` | `visited.insert(root_scope_id)` at :462, checked at :493 before the resolve; the root is re-keyed via `rekey_one`/the root plan |
| `create.rs:405` `resolve` | **no `visited`, no root pre-seed** — I had this wrong |

The third holds for a different reason: every production-minted descendant carries an ascent link (`create.rs:356`, `create.rs:420`, `cascade.rs:531` hardcode `owes_ascent_link: true`, and #1225's guard refuses to publish one that would drop it), and a read grant is on an interior folder, so the vault root can never appear in `subtree_child_index`. My doc comment originally claimed the walks' `visited` was the reason for all three — the review correctly called that incomplete, and I rewrote it to state the caller contract instead.

The two edges that legitimately gate a scope's *own* root — `publish_scope_root` and `SweepResolver::resolve_scope` — still call `gated_root`. The cascade is not broken.

## Condition 2 — red proof

Reverted `gated_child_root` → `gated_scope_root`, ran the new test: **FAILED** at `expect_err`, meaning the planted vault root was *silently adopted into the eager set*. Restored, test passes. The guard is load-bearing, not decorative. I also added `a_scope_naming_itself_as_its_own_child_is_skipped_not_fatal`, which pins that the rejected proposal's DoS shape stays non-fatal.

## Condition 3 — `/security-review`: **do not block**

Clean on verdict mapping, idempotence/`reread_at_floor`, park interaction, zeroization, and borrows. It also confirmed the new `envelope.id == expected_id` binding closes a real gap — the gate previously bound only `env.scope`, so a committed writer of scope C could author a self-consistent record at C's name with an arbitrary `env.id`.

**But it found a HIGH adjacent to my own #1225 work, and I want you to see it.** My `owes_ascent_link` guards the *drop* direction only. `reseal_scope_root` still mints a link off the *presence of the seed*, and `Ancestry::record` has no self-reference filter — so a writer planting a self-entry gets `parents[R] = R`, the index self-heal re-seals the vault root with a fabricated parent seed, and **an ascent link is minted onto the vault root**. Every subsequent reader is then rejected at the gate's ascent stage, the sequence floor has advanced, and `reread_at_floor` only recovers at the exact floor. Permanent, writer-triggerable vault lockout — rule 8 in the other direction.

Pre-existing, out of this diff, latent only because `OwnerRotationNet` has no production facade constructor. **Filed as #1230** with both one-line guards and the release-active test shapes, blocked-by #1225. I did not fold it in: the reseal half alone converts a silent bricking mint into a hard publish failure, which is better but still writer-triggerable, and the `Ancestry::record` filter is what removes the trigger — that is a second crypto change to unchanged code with its own fail-closed tradeoff, and this PR is already large.

## Nitpick 1 — `crates/core/src/seal/owner_local.rs:240-262` — "isolate the AAD discriminator from the HPKE `info`"

**Claims:** the cross-kind test can't show *which* of the two bindings (per-kind `info` vs AAD discriminator) does the separating; add a test that seals with one fixed `info` and per-kind AAD to pin the discriminator as an independent separator.

**Correct?** The observation is technically true and the conclusion is wrong.

- The proposed test cannot use `seal_owner_local`/`open_owner_local` at all — both derive `info` from the `kind` argument (`owner_local.rs:142`, `:195`). It would have to hand-roll `hpke::hpke_seal_auth` with a fixed `info` and a per-kind AAD, i.e. test a configuration that no production path can construct.
- What it would actually assert — "AEAD open fails under a different AAD" — is **already a unit test** in `crates/core/src/suite/hpke.rs:555-565` ("Wrong AAD"), alongside "Wrong info" at `:566-575`.
- CR's stated risk ("a future change collapses the per-kind `info` back to a single family string") **cannot land silently**. `crates/core/kat/manifest.json` freezes all three `hpkeInfo` strings verbatim, `owner_local_kind_registry_is_frozen` (`kat_manifest.rs:4830-4835`) asserts `spec.hpke_info == kind.hpke_info()` per kind, and the 4 accept vectors pin exact blob bytes under fixed ephemerals. Collapsing the `info` breaks three gates before it reaches the test CR is worried about.

**Repo-rule conflict:** yes, in spirit — it is a test for a code path that does not exist, exactly the speculative generality `/simplify` exists to remove.

**Cost:** ~25 lines, test-only, one file.

**Recommendation: reject.** Push back with the manifest-freeze mechanism if you reply.

---

## Nitpick 2 — `crates/core/tests/kat_manifest.rs:4853-4857` — derive the count from `infos.len()` instead of the literal `3`

**Claims:** `3 + m.owner_local.kinds.len()` reads from two sources that must stay in step with `infos`; compare against `infos.len()`.

**Correct?** Yes — and it is understated. There is a real **fail-open** in a distinctness assertion this PR *adds*:

`infos` is 3 literals + `ALL.len()` = 6 entries; expected is `3 + kinds.len()` = 6. Now suppose someone adds a fourth enc-subkey structure and pushes its `info` literal onto the vec: `infos.len()` becomes 7.
- New literal is distinct → set size 7 ≠ 6 → test fails, they bump the `3`. Fine.
- New literal **collides** with an existing one → set size 6 == 6 → **test passes**, and the collision — two structures sharing a key schedule, i.e. transplantable — goes undetected.

This is the only *general* guard against info-string collision; the pairwise transplant tests (`an_op_record_and_a_content_key_blob_never_open_as_each_other`) only cover pairs someone already wrote. So it fails open precisely at the moment it matters: when a new structure is added. CR's `infos.len()` form has no such hole.

**Repo-rule conflict:** none. It cuts toward AGENTS.md rule 8's fail-closed posture.

**Cost:** two lines in one test — swap the expected count, drop the now-unused `let m = manifest();`. Test-only, no vector regeneration, no production logic.

**Recommendation: fold.** Real, small, fail-open — but it cannot bite before the fix would naturally land: the only way to trip it is to add a new enc-subkey `info` literal, which means editing this exact test. Land it in the next PR that adds a structure or otherwise touches `crates/core/tests/kat_manifest.rs`.

---

## Nitpick 3 — `crates/core/examples/kat_gen.rs:6991` — distinct ephemeral for the cross-family transplant

**Claims:** the settings-record transplant blob reuses `eph` (`:6957`) from the owner-local probe (`:6958`), so the corpus models ephemeral reuse — violating the rule the author states at `:7080-7083`.

**Correct?** The premise is false as a safety concern, and the consistency argument is self-defeating.

- `hpke.rs:70-79` scopes the invariant precisely: reuse is a break only under **one `recipient_pub`/`info`** — same ephemeral + same sender + same recipient + same `info` re-derives an identical AEAD key *and base nonce*. Here the two seals use different `info` strings (`cipherbox/v2/owner-local/received-shares` vs the settings-record info), so the key schedules diverge. No key reuse, no nonce reuse, no break. These are also hardcoded test scalars published in a JSON corpus — there is no confidentiality to break.
- The shared ephemeral is **the sharper vector**. It forces an identical KEM shared secret, so the transplant's rejection is attributable to the `info` and struct tag alone — which is exactly what the comment at `:6988-6990` says the vector is for. CR's change would make the negative slightly *less* isolating.
- The precedent is pre-existing on `main`: `kat_manifest.rs:4010-4022` (unchanged by this PR, confirmed against `c390fe499`) deliberately seals an op record and a content-key blob "under one enc subkey with one ephemeral" so they "differ only in their HPKE `info` and AAD." Applying CR's fix would leave the repo with two contradictory conventions rather than one.

The one grain of truth: `seal_owner_local`'s doc comment (`owner_local.rs:123-125`) drops the `info` qualifier and says reuse "under one recipient key" is a break — stricter than what `hpke.rs` actually claims. That is a doc-precision nit in the new file, not a corpus defect, and not what CR flagged.

**Repo-rule conflict:** the fix would require regenerating frozen KAT vector bytes (`owner_local_reject.json` `cross-family-transplant`) to satisfy a non-issue.

**Cost:** highest of the three — `kat_gen.rs` edit, run the generator, commit changed frozen corpus bytes. Touches the frozen artifact, not just a test.

**Recommendation: reject.**

---

## Overall call

**Not worth a review slot. Merge #1215 as-is.**

Two of the three are wrong on the mechanism (1 re-tests `hpke.rs` at a layer where the config can't exist; 3 misreads the ephemeral invariant and contradicts an established convention on `main`). The third is a genuine fail-open, but it is two lines in a test whose only trigger condition is a future PR editing that same test — so folding it costs nothing in exposure.

Fold nitpick 2 into the next PR touching `crates/core/tests/kat_manifest.rs` or `crates/core/src/seal/`. If you want a second freebie in that same PR, tighten the `seal_owner_local` ephemeral doc at `owner_local.rs:123-125` to say "under one recipient key **and `info`**", matching `hpke.rs:74`.</result>

**`crates/core/src/seal/owner_local.rs:124`** — the `seal_owner_local` doc now says reuse "under one recipient key **and `info`**" is the break, matching the qualifier `suite/hpke.rs:74` carries.

## Red proof for nitpick 2

The fail-open shape is a *new* structure colliding, so I added a 4th entry to `infos` duplicating `OP_RECORD_HPKE_INFO`:

- Fixed assertion → **FAILS**: `left: 6, right: 7` at `kat_manifest.rs:4853`.
- Old assertion, same collision → **PASSES** (set 6 == 3 + 3).

Fail-open demonstrated in both directions, then the file was restored from backup. (Note: colliding two *existing* literals would not have proved it — the old form catches that one; only an added-entry collision exposes the hole.)

## Rejections — all mechanisms verified against the code, not taken on trust

**Nitpick 1 (isolate the AAD discriminator):** confirmed `seal_owner_local` and `open_owner_local` both pass `&amp;kind.hpke_info()` (`owner_local.rs:142`, `:195`), so the proposed test can't use the public API at all. Confirmed the generic coverage exists at `suite/hpke.rs:555-565` / `:566-575`. Confirmed `manifest.json` freezes all three `ownerLocal.kinds[].hpkeInfo` plus the prefix, and `owner_local_kind_registry_is_frozen` asserts `spec.hpke_info == kind.hpke_info()` at `kat_manifest.rs:4830-4835`.

**Nitpick 3 (distinct ephemeral):** confirmed `hpke.rs:70-79` scopes reuse to the same `recipient_pub`/`info`; confirmed the two seals use different `info` strings and both use `hpke_seal_auth` with the same owner as sender and recipient (so one ephemeral forces an identical KEM shared secret — the sharper vector). Confirmed the `main` precedent at `kat_manifest.rs:4010-4022`.

One thing the brief did not flag and I had to resolve myself: the PR's own comment at `kat_gen.rs:7080-7083` states the *opposite* rule for cross-kind probes ("a corpus must not model a pattern whose safety is the thing under test") — CodeRabbit was citing the PR against itself. The distinction is real and I put it in the reply: owner-local `info` strings are *computed* (`prefix + name`), so a one-edit collapse is representable and the cross-kind probes shouldn't lean on it; cross-family `info` strings are separate frozen literals in different families with no such edit. Without that paragraph the rejection would have looked inconsistent.

Single reply posted: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1215#issuecomment-5230503192

## PR 1146 — merge `4ee9cbf`, head `1c27b32`, review commit `20145a1`

The one post-review commit (`1c27b32`, "test: pin the media failure precedence and the ticket and timer bounds") touched `FileBrowserActions.test.tsx`, `MediaPlayer.tsx`, `useFileDownload.test.ts` — i.e. it answered the three *inline* comments, and neither of the two items below.

| file:line | asked for | verdict | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `packages/client/src/media/broker.test.ts:618-632` (nitpick) | Widen the 40 ms deadline or use fake timers so the `serve` re-arm always lands before `expire` | **NOT DONE** | Test is byte-identical at review commit and on main: `whenIdle(h.ticket, 40)` with two real-timer 25 ms waits. Real, low-severity flake: no read is started, so `waiter.read` is `false`; if the first wait overruns 40 ms, `expire` fires with zero cursors and resolves `false`, making `idle()` return `false` instead of `null` and failing the assert. 15 ms of wall-clock margin. **Test-only, no product impact.** |
| `apps/web/src/hooks/useFileDownload.ts:57-93` (outside diff, Major) | `save` returns a named outcome (success / refusal / per-file failure) instead of a boolean; subscribe to `media.onStreamError` for the minted ticket so a broker-abandoned read sets `error`; `FileBrowserActions.tsx:88-100` breaks only on refusal; add a mid-read-failure test | **NOT DONE** | On main `save` is still `Promise&lt;boolean&gt;` (line 58); `onStreamError` appears only in `MediaPlayer.tsx`/`service.ts`, never in the hook; `FileBrowserActions.tsx:95` still `break`s on any `false`; `useFileDownload.test.ts` has no mid-read-failure case. **Two real defects.** Mechanism confirmed in `broker.ts`: `fail()` → `drop()` → `settleIdle()` resolves with `waiter.read`, which `acquire()` set to `true`, so a broker read failure resolves `whenStreamIdle` **true** → `save` returns success, no error is shown, and the user gets a silently truncated file. Symmetrically, the buffered-fallback `catch` returns `false`, which the batch loop reads as a refusal and aborts the rest of the selection. No follow-up issue exists for either (searched open/closed). |

**Disposition line:** Both are still open on main — the broker test-timing nitpick is a genuine 15 ms-margin flake worth a cheap fake-timer fix, and the outside-diff item is a real pair of defects (a broker mid-read failure reports success because `settleIdle` resolves with `read=true`, and a per-file buffered-read failure aborts the whole batch) that should be filed as a follow-up issue rather than left implicit.</result>
